Locally analytic vectors in the completed cohomology of quaternionic Shimura curves
Zhenghui Li, Benchao Su, Zhixiang Wu

Abstract
We use the methods introduced by Lue Pan to study the locally analytic vectors of the completed cohomology of Shimura curves associated to an indefinite quaternion algebra D D which is ramified at a prime number p p . Let D p × D_{p}^{\times} be the group of units of D D at p p . Using p p -adic uniformization of the quaternionic Shimura curves, we compute the Hecke eigenspace of the completed cohomology with the Hecke eigenvalues associated to a classical automorphic form on another quaternion algebra D ¯ \overline{D} (switching invariants of D D at p , ∞ p,\infty ). We present this locally analytic D p × D_{p}^{\times} -representation using the de Rham complex of the Lubin–Tate tower of dimension 1 1 . This is analogous to the Breuil–Strauch conjecture for the group GL 2 ⁡ ( ℚ p ) \GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}) . We show that the locally analytic D p × D_{p}^{\times} -representation does not detect the Hodge filtration of the local de Rham Galois representation at p p in the crystalline case, and also give applications for the locally analytic Jacquet–Langlands correspondence for GL 2 ⁡ ( ℚ p ) \GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}) and D p × D_{p}^{\times} .



1
Introduction

Let
p
be a prime number. Let
E
be a sufficiently large finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. The
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence for
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
constructs a correspondence between certain
2
-dimensional
p
-adic representations of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and certain
p
-adic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
over
E

[
Bre10
,
Col10
,
CDP14
]
. There have been recent developments of the interactions between
p
-adic or mod-
p
representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
D_{p}^{\times}
, with
D_{p}
a non-split quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, known as the
p
-adic Jacquet–Langlands correspondence. For a non-exhaustive list of relevant works, see, e.g.,
[
Sch18
,
Lud17
,
Paš22
,
HM22
,
HW24a
,
HW24b
,
DC24
]
. While the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
side is more or less well understood, the representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
arising from the
p
-adic correspondences remain largely mysterious.

This paper gives a first complete description of the internal structure of the locally analytic representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
associated to de Rham representations of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with global origins, in terms of the (compactly supported) coherent cohomology groups of the
1
-dimensional Lubin–Tate tower. This establishes the quaternionic analogue of the Breuil–Strauch conjecture firstly proved by Dospinescu–Le Bras
[
DLB17
]
, which describes
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-representations using the de Rham complexes of the
1
-dimensional Drinfeld tower. Our description for the
D_{p}^{\times}
-representations exhibits several features of the
p
-adic Jacquet–Langlands correspondance.

Our work is based on the study of the locally analytic vectors in the completed cohomology of quaternionic Shimura curves using the methods introduced by Lue Pan
[
Pan22
,
Pan26
]
. Let
D
be an indefinite quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
which is ramified at
p
, and let
G
be the group of units of
D
, considered as an algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
. For
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
a neat open compact subgroup, the compact Riemann surface
S_{K}(\mathbb{C}):=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash((\mathbb{C}\backslash\mathbb{R})\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K)

admits a canonical model
S_{K}
, which is a proper smooth algebraic curve over
\mathbb{Q}
. Fix a neat open compact subgroup
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, where
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
denotes the ring of finite adeles away from
p
. As
K_{p}
varies in open compact subgroups of
D_{p}^{\times}=G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, we consider Emerton’s completed cohomology group
[
Eme06
]

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E):=E\ox_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\varprojlim_{n}\varinjlim_{K_{p}}H^{1}(S_{K^{p}K_{p}}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})),

which is a Banach space over
E
, carrying a continuous
E
-linear action of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\times D_{p}^{\times}
. It is expected, as the local-global compatibility of the
p
-adic Langlands correspondence in the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-case
[
Eme11
,
Pan26
]
, that if
\rho:\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow\GL_{2}(E)
is an absolutely irreducible Galois representation such that the
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation

\displaystyle\check{\Pi}(\rho):=\Hom_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}(\rho,\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E))

is non-zero, then its subspace of locally analytic vectors
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
should be a finite copy of the conjectural locally analytic
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation
\tau(\rho_{p})
attached to
\rho_{p}=\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
in the
p
-adic Langlands program.

On the other hand, we can give a direct construction of a locally analytic
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation
\tau(\rho_{p})
for a
2
-dimensional de Rham representation
\rho_{p}
of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
using the de Rham complexes of Lubin–Tate spaces. Let
\{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}\}_{n\geq 0}
be the Lubin–Tate tower arising from a
1
-dimensional formal
p
-divisible group over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
of height
2
. They are
1
-dimensional rigid analytic Stein spaces over
\mathbb{C}_{p}:=\widehat{\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
, and the whole tower carries an action of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times D_{p}^{\times}

[
HG94
,
RZ96
]
. For each fixed level
n\geq 0
, the compactly supported cohomology groups
H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})
and
H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})
(with
\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}
the sheaf of differentials) carry locally analytic
D_{p}^{\times}
-actions in the sense of Schneider–Teitelbaum
[
ST02
]
(see also the work
[
She20
]
). The colimits
\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})
and
\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{1})
are also smooth representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, thus are naturally modules over the smooth Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}(G)
(the convolution algebra of compactly supported smooth functions on
G
after fixing a Haar measure on
G
) with
G=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

We fix
\rho_{p}:\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow\GL_{2}(E)
to be de Rham with Hodge–Tate weight
0,1
. By Fontaine’s theory, we can associate to
\rho_{p}
a
2
-dimensional filtered
E
-vector space
D_{\dR}(\rho_{p})
, with the filtration given by the Hodge filtration, and a Weil–Deligne representation
r_{p}
over
E
. We can then associate to
r_{p}
a smooth representation
\pi_{p}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and a smooth representation
\tau_{p}
of
D_{p}^{\times}
(with coefficients in
\mathbb{C}_{p}
) by the local Langlands correspondence
1
1

1

We use the version in
[
Eme11
, §4.2]
and we only consider
\rho_{p}
such that
\pi_{p}
is irreducible and infinite-dimensional.
and the Jacquet–Langlands correspondence. Here, we set
\tau_{p}=0
if
\pi_{p}
is an irreducible principal series representation. From
\pi_{p}
, we can define the following locally analytic representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
using the Lubin–Tate tower:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}:=(\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}))\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p},

\displaystyle\tau_{c}:=(\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{1}))\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}.

We will show that the de Rham complexes of the Lubin–Tate spaces and the classical Jacquet–Langlands correspondence induce a short exact sequence of locally analytic
D_{p}^{\times}
-representations (see §
3.1
):

\displaystyle 0\to\tau_{p}^{\oplus 2}\rightarrow\widetilde{\tau}\to\tau_{c}\to 0

which depends only on
\pi_{p}
and equivalently
r_{p}
.

We define the locally analytic
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation
\tau(\rho_{p})
associated to
\rho_{p}
as the quotient representation

\tau(\rho_{p}):=\widetilde{\tau}/i_{\rho_{p}}(\tau_{p}),

where the map
i_{\rho_{p}}:\tau_{p}\hookrightarrow\tau_{p}^{\oplus 2}\subset\widetilde{\tau}
is induced by a canonical isomorphism
\tau_{p}^{\oplus 2}\simeq\tau_{p}\otimes D_{\dR}(\rho_{p})
and the
1
-dimensional subspace of
D_{\dR}(\rho_{p})
given by the Hodge filtration. Then
\tau(\rho_{p})
sits in an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\tau_{p}\rightarrow\tau(\rho_{p})\rightarrow\tau_{c}\rightarrow 0.

(1)

The following main theorem establishes the local-global compatibility for the
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation
\tau(\rho_{p})
.

Theorem 1.1

(Theorem
3.13
, Corollary
3.16
)
.

Let
\rho_{p}
be a
2
-dimensional de Rham representation of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
over
E
with Hodge–Tate weight
0,1
. Suppose that there exists an absolutely irreducible Galois representation
\rho
of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
over
E
such that
\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\simeq\rho_{p}
.

(1)

Assume that
\rho
appears in
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)
, i.e.
\check{\Pi}(\rho)=\Hom_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}(\rho,\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E))\neq 0
. Then there exists some
m\geq 1
, such that there is a
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant and topological isomorphism
2
2

2

Here we fix an embedding
E\to\mathbb{C}_{p}
.

\displaystyle\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}\widehat{\ox}_{E}\mathbb{C}_{p}\cong\tau(\rho_{p})^{\oplus m}.

Moreover, the representation
\tau(\rho_{p})
and the above isomorphism can be defined over
E
. And with the
E
-structure
\tau(\rho_{p})
is an infinite-dimensional admissible locally analytic representation of
D_{p}^{\times}
in the sense of Schneider–Teitelbaum
[
ST03
]
.

(2)

The representation
\rho
appears in
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)
if and only if
\rho
is the Galois representation associated to some classical automorphic form of the tame level
K^{p}
on the group
\overline{G}
, the algebraic group of the units of the definite quaternion algebra
\overline{D}
over
\mathbb{Q}
obtained from
D
by switching the Hasse invariants at
p,\infty
.

Remark 1.2
.

(i)

Theorem
1.1
also holds when
\rho_{p}
is de Rham of arbitrary regular Hodge–Tate weight. Moreover, following the methods of
[
QS25
]
, similar results for locally
L
-analytic representations of
D_{L}^{\times}
also hold, with
L
a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
D_{L}
a non-split quaternion algebra over
L
, by using some Shimura curves with
D_{L}^{\times}
being a local factor of the group at
p
.

(ii)

The second part of Theorem
1.1
proves the classicality for the de Rham Galois representations that appear in
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)
. This is essentially known by the proof of the Fontaine-Mazur conjecture for
\GL_{2/\mathbb{Q}}

[
Eme11
,
Pan26
]
and the global Jacquet–Langlands correspondence. The more interesting part is the non-vanishing of
\check{\Pi}(\rho)
when
\tau_{p}=0
. In this case there is no classical automorphic form on
G
associated to
\rho
while
\tau(\rho_{p})\neq 0
. Such non-vanishing result in the
p
-adic Jacquet–Langlands correspondence was previously known by Paškūnas
[
Paš22
, Theorem 1.4]
(see also
[
How22
, §1.3]
) using very different methods.

The structure of
\tau(\rho_{p})
illustrates similarities and differences between the groups
D_{p}^{\times}
and
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
under the
p
-adic local Langlands correspondences:

•

When the associated Weil–Deligne representation
r_{p}
is absolutely irreducible, the presentation of
\tau(\rho_{p})
is similar to that of the locally analytic representation
\pi(\rho_{p})
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
attached to
\rho_{p}
via the
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence. In that case, by the Breuil–Strauch conjecture proved by Dospinescu-Le Bras
[
DLB17
]
and reproved by Lue Pan
[
Pan26
, Theorem 7.3.2]
,
\pi(\rho_{p})
admits a presentation

\displaystyle\pi(\rho_{p})\cong(\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(\check{G})}\tau_{p})/i_{\rho_{p}}(\pi_{p})

(2)

where
\{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}\}_{n\geq 0}
is the Drinfeld tower
[
Dri76
]
, with
\check{G}=D_{p}^{\times}
, and the map
i_{\rho_{p}}
is determined by the Hodge filtration of
D_{\dR}(\rho_{p})
. However, if the Weil–Deligne representation
r_{p}
is reducible, for example if
\rho_{p}
is crystalline, then the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-representation
\pi(\rho_{p})
can not be described using the Drinfeld tower in a similar way.

•

In contrast, the
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation
\tau(\rho_{p})
is always (only) related to the Lubin–Tate tower, regardless of whether the Weil–Deligne representation
r_{p}
is irreducible or not. Moreover, unlike the classical non-abelian Lubin-Tate theory and the Jacquet–Langlands correspondence, e.g.
[
Car90
,
Dat07
]
, we will show directly in Theorem
1.5
that
(\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{1}))\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}\neq 0
and consequently
\tau(\rho_{p})
is never zero even if the Jacquet–Langlands transfer
\tau_{p}
of
\pi_{p}
is
0
. This will enable us to prove the appearance of
\rho
in
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)
when
\rho_{p}
is crystalline in (2) of Theorem
1.1
.

•

Suppose that
\rho_{p}
is crystalline where
\tau_{p}=0
. Then
\tau(\rho_{p})
, as well as
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
up to a multiplicity, is independent of the Hodge filtration of
D_{\dR}(\rho_{p})
(thus depends only on the Weil–Deligne representation
r_{p}
attached to
\rho_{p}
). Hence the representation
\tau(\rho_{p})
can lose some information about
\rho_{p}
(see Remark
3.18
for examples). This is a new phenomenon in the
p
-adic Langlands program for the group
D_{p}^{\times}
, which is different from the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-case where
\pi(\rho_{p})
always determines
\rho_{p}
. Note that in the mod
p
setting, a similar result was obtained by Hu–Wang
[
HW24a
, Theorem 1.3(ii)(a)]
.

Our proof of Theorem
1.1
is based on the machinery developed by Lue Pan
[
Pan22
,
Pan26
]
on the description of the locally analytic vectors in the completed cohomology. Let
\mathcal{S}_{K}
be the adic space associated to
S_{K}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C}_{p}
. Let
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\sim\varprojlim_{K_{p}}\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}}
be the perfectoid quaternionic Shimura curve, together with the Hodge–Tate period map
\pi_{\HT}:\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
(cf.
[
Sch15
]
). Here,
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
is (the base change to
\mathbb{C}_{p}
of) the Brauer–Severi variety associated to the quaternion algebra
D_{p}
. Recall that
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)
is the
E
-valued completed cohomology group of the quaternionic Shimura curve. Let
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)^{\mathrm{la}}
be the subspace of locally analytic vectors in
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)
for the
D_{p}^{\times}
-actions. By the primitive comparison theorem
[
Sch13b
]
and the geometric Sen theory
[
Pan22
,
Cam22
]
, we have the following natural isomorphism

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)^{\mathrm{la}}\widehat{\ox}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbb{C}_{p}\cong H^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}})\ox_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}E,

where
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}:=(\pi_{\HT,*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})^{\mathrm{la}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
is the completed structure sheaf of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
. Then we can study the completed cohomology group by studying the equivariant sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}
on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
. As in
[
Pan26
]
, the “de Rham part” of the completed cohomology is killed by the Fontaine operator, which can be representation-theoretically expressed as some differential operators along the tower of Shimura curves and the flag variety. For example, there is a differential operator
d_{K^{p}}
on the weight
(0,0)
-part (for the horizontal action of the Cartan subalgebra) of
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}
which induces a “de Rham” complex (see §
2.2.4
for more details)

\displaystyle d_{K^{p}}:\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,0)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,0)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{p}}.

The above complex extends the classical de Rham complexes of Shimura curves of finite levels and its cohomology contributes to the “de Rham part” of the completed cohomology.

The cohomology of these differential operators, such as
d_{K^{p}}
above, can be analysed using the
p
-adic uniformization of the quaternionic Shimura curves, which expresses the tower of quaternionic Shimura curve as a finite disjoint union of quotients of the Drinfeld tower by some discrete cocompact subgroups of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(see §
2.1.2
). Let
\overline{D}
be the quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
obtained from
D
by switching invariants at
p
and
\infty
, and let
\overline{G}
be the associated algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
as in Theorem
1.1
. We view
K^{p}
as an open compact subgroup of
\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
under the isomorphism
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\cong\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Let
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},0}
be the space of smooth functions on the Shimura set
\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{p}
(of infinite level at
p
). Then
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},0}
can be viewed as the space of classical algebraic automorphic forms on
\overline{G}
with the trivial weight and the tame level
K^{p}
, and is naturally a smooth representation for the action of
\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\simeq\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Both
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)
and
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},0}^{K^{p}}
carry an action of the spherical Hecke algebra
\mathbb{T}^{S}
away from a finite set
S
of prime numbers including
p
. We have the following typical product formula obtained from the
p
-adic uniformization.

Proposition 1.3

(Proposition
2.25
)
.

There exists a
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant isomorphism of
D_{p}^{\times}
-representations

\displaystyle R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\ker d_{K^{p}})[1]

\displaystyle\cong(\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}))\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},0}.

To obtain such a product formula, we first do the computation on the local Shimura variety (as in
[
Pan26
, §5.2, 5.3]
) and then descend the results along the quotients by discrete cocompact subgroups in
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Here, the key point is that we need do descent for some huge sheaves (e.g. sheaves related to
\ker d_{K^{p}}
) along some infinite-sheet coverings. For this reason, in Appendix
A
, we developed a general theory of the compactly supported cohomology group with coefficients in some (possibly solid) abelian sheaves (like
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la}}
) on partially proper adic spaces, and we also developed some relevant descent theory. While the theory is not general enough (we do not establish a full
6
-functor formalism), it suffices for our purpose.

Remark 1.4
.

Using the
p
-adic uniformization, we can also establish similar product formulae relating the coherent cohomology of Shimura curves with that of the Drinfeld tower of dimension
1
and the completed (co)homology of Shimura sets, see Remark
2.27
. Such uniformization results for coherent cohomology of (infinite or finite level) Shimura varieties seem to be new and very useful. For example, formula (
36
) will allow us to calculate some higher extension groups for certain locally analytic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
arising from the coherent cohomology of Drinfeld spaces in
[
DLB17
]
(e.g. (
2
)), which will be treated in future work.

Another key ingredient towards Theorem
1.1
is the following non-vanishing result of the
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation
\tau_{c},\widetilde{\tau}
defined before Theorem
1.1
.

Theorem 1.5

(Theorem
3.9
)
.

The
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation

\tau_{c}=(\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{1}))\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}

is infinite-dimensional. In particular, it is non-zero.

The proof for the theorem is mostly local (with some global input to understand the de Rham cohomology of the Lubin–Tate tower). We use some explicit formula for the action of the Lie algebra of
D_{p}^{\times}
on equivariant sheaves on the Lubin–Tate spaces (deduced from the Lie algebra action formula on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
via the Gross–Hopkins period map) in
[
HG94
, §25]
. We refer the reader to §
3.2.1
for more details.

Remark 1.6
.

(1)

If
\pi_{p}
is a local component of a regular algebraic cuspidal automorphic representation of
\overline{G}
, in Proposition
2.38
, we also prove that for all
i>0
,

\Tor_{i}^{\mathcal{H}(G)}(\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}),\pi_{p})=0.

(2)

We can also establish some representation-theoretic properties of
\tau_{c}
when
\pi_{p}
has global origins as in (1). The representation
\tau_{c}
admits a natural
E
-structure denoted by
\tau_{c,E}
. It is an admissible locally analytic
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation over
E
(by Theorem
1.1
). Then we show that
\tau_{c,E}
has Gelfand–Kirillov dimension
1
(see Theorem
3.11
, which is based on the work
[
DPS23
]
), and it is pure (see Lemma
3.22
).

Remark 1.7
.

We expect that when the Weil–Deligne representation
r_{p}
is absolutely irreducible, the representation
\tau(\rho_{p})
we constructed should still determine
\rho_{p}
as in the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
case, see for example
[
Din22
]
. Using the methods in
[
QS25
,
Su25
]
and our geometric description of
\tau_{c}
using the Lubin–Tate spaces, we can show that
\dim\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{D_{p}^{\times}}(\tau_{c},\tau_{p})=2
. Hence the extension class (
1
) given by
\tau(\rho_{p})
should determine the Hodge filtration in the
2
-dimensional space
D_{\dR}(\rho_{p})
. However, to show that
\tau(\rho_{p})
is non-isomorphic for distinct
\rho_{p}
associated with the same
r_{p}
, further input is required (e.g.
\dim\Hom_{D_{p}^{\times}}(\tau_{c},\tau_{c})=1
). This will be left to future work.

Overview

We briefly describe the structure of this article.

In §
2.1
, we introduce the perfectoid quaternionic Shimura curve and its
p
-adic uniformization. In §
2.2
, we recall and adapt various constructions and results of Lue Pan to our quaternion setting. In §
2.3
, we apply the
p
-adic uniformization and the descent results developed in Appendix
A
to establish the product formula for compactly supported cohomology (Proposition
1.3
). In §
2.4
and §
2.5
, we prove the main results on the “de Rham” part of the locally analytic vectors of the completed cohomology of the Shimura curves (e.g. the spectral decomposition in Theorem
2.42
).

In §
3
, we study the locally analytic representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
that appear in the completed cohomology attached to de Rham Galois representations. In §
3.1
and §
3.2
, we define these representations in terms of the Lubin–Tate tower (e.g.
\tau_{c}
and
\widetilde{\tau}
) and study various properties of them, including the non-vanishing result (Theorem
1.5
), the admissibility (in Theorem
1.1
) and the Gelfand-Kirillov dimension (Theorem
3.11
). Then we apply the results to the completed cohomology and the
p
-adic Langlands correspondence for
D_{p}^{\times}
in §
3.3
. Finally, in §
3.4
, we discuss the
p
-adic Jacquet–Langlands correspondence for
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(i.e. Scholze’s functor).

Notation and conventions

In the whole paper, the field
E
will be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\ur}
be the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in an algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and let
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
be its
p
-adic completion with the ring of integers
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}=W(\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}})
. We write
C=\mathbb{C}_{p}:=\widehat{\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
.

For
F=E
or
C
a
p
-adic field and
H
a
p
-adic manifold, write
\mathcal{C}_{(c)}^{\mathrm{sm}}(H,F),\mathcal{C}_{(c)}^{\mathrm{la}}(H,F)
and
\mathcal{C}_{(c)}^{\rm cont}(H,F)
respectively for the space of (compactly supported)
F
-valued locally constant, locally analytic, and continuous functions on
H
. If
H
is a
p
-adic Lie group, we write
\Lie H
for its Lie algebra over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

If
g\in\GL_{n}
is a matrix, we write
g^{t}
for its transpose.

For
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}
, we write
V^{(a,b)}=\mathrm{Sym}^{a-b}V^{(1,0)}\otimes\det^{b}
or
W^{(a,b)}
for the algebraic representation of
\GL_{2}
or its Lie algebra
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
(over a field) with highest weight
(a,b)
(with respect to the Borel subgroup of upper triangular matrices), where
V^{(1,0)},W^{(1,0)}
denote the standard
2
-dimensional representation of
\GL_{2}
. Let
\mathfrak{h}
be the subalgebra of diagonal matrices in
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
. Then we view
(a,b)
as a character of
\mathfrak{h}
by sending
\begin{pmatrix}x&0\\
0&y\end{pmatrix}
to
ax+by
.

If
X
is a space (scheme, adic space, linear space, etc.) with a left (resp. right) action of a group
H
, then we also view
X
as a right (resp. left)
H
-space via the involution
g\mapsto g^{-1}
on
H
. If
\mathcal{F}
is an equivariant sheaf on a left
H
-space
X
, then the global sections (or cohomology groups) of
\mathcal{F}
naturally form a left
H
-representation via the usual dual actions.

If
M
is a vector space over a field
F
with a linear action of a commutative ring
\mathbb{T}
, and
\lambda:\mathbb{T}\rightarrow F
is a ring homomorphism, then we write
M[\lambda]
for the subspace
\{m\in M\mid tm=0,\forall t\in\mathrm{Ker}(\lambda)\}\subset M
.

Our convention is that the Hodge–Tate weight of the
p
-adic cyclotomic character of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is
-1
.

We normalize the local reciprocity map
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}^{\times}\rightarrow\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}/\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})^{\rm ab}
by sending
\ell
to a geometric Frobenius element for any prime number
\ell
.

As in
[
Pan22
,
Pan26
]
, the sheaves on adic spaces considered in this paper are equipped with natural topologies (e.g.
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}
is a sheaf of LB-spaces, i.e. countable locally convex inductive limits of Banach spaces) and the isomorphisms between cohomology groups are topological isomorphisms. The topology and functional analysis issues have been discussed carefully in
loc. cit.
and can also be treated using condensed mathematics as in
[
Cam24
,
BCGP25
]
. Since our setting is similar to these previous works and the different settings are not essential to the main results, we will generally omit the discussions on topology in this paper. We note that all completed tensor products
-\widehat{\ox}-
over
p
-adic fields in this paper can be replaced by solid tensor products.

2
Locally analytic cohomologies of Shimura curves

2.1
Quaternionic Shimura curve

First, we introduce our perfectoid quaternionic Shimura curve and describe its
p
-adic uniformization, cf.
[
Sch18
, §5, §6]
. We also recall the Drinfeld and Lubin–Tate spaces (of dimension
1
) and the period maps on them.

2.1.1
The Shimura curve

Let
D
be a quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
, which defines a reductive group
G
over
\mathbb{Q}
by
G(R):=(D\ox_{\mathbb{Q}}R)^{\times}
for any
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
R
. Assume that
D
splits over
\mathbb{R}
. We assume that
D_{p}:=D\ox_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is a division algebra (the unique non-split quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
), and then
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=D_{p}^{\times}
.

Let
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be an open compact subgroup of the finite adelic points of
G
. Assume that there is a decomposition
K=K^{p}K_{p}
, where
K_{p}\subset D_{p}^{\times},K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
are open compact subgroups. We will usually fix
K^{p}
throughout this article and will always assume
K^{p}
to be sufficiently small (so that to avoid problems of non-representability and to get the desired torsion freeness of some cocompact subgroups of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
defined by
K^{p}
, see for example
[
BC91
, 1.5]
and §
2.1.2
).

Let
X=\mathbb{C}\backslash\mathbb{R}
be the union of the usual Poincaré upper and lower half planes, which can be seen as the
G(\mathbb{R})
-conjugacy class of
h:\mathbb{S}=\mathrm{Res}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow G_{\mathbb{R}}=(D\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R})^{\times}
sending
a+bi\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}=\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{R})
to
\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
-b&a\end{pmatrix}\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{R})\cong(D\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R})^{\times}
. Hence
G(\mathbb{R})
acts on
X
naturally. The compact Riemann surface

S_{K}(\mathbb{C}):=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(X\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K)

is known to be canonically defined over
\mathbb{Q}
. Let
S_{K}
be the (projective smooth) Shimura curve over the reflex field
\mathbb{Q}
associated with the Shimura datum
(G,X)
and of level
K
.

Fix an algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
and denote
C
to be the completion of
\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
for the
p
-adic norm. Let
\mathcal{S}_{K}
be the adic space over
C
associated with
S_{K}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}C
. As
K_{p}
varies in all open compact subgroups of
D_{p}^{\times}
, we get a perfectoid space over
C
:

\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\sim\varprojlim_{K_{p}}\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}}

together with the Hodge–Tate period map

\pi_{K^{p},\HT}:\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\to\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,

where
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
is the adic space associated with
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C}
. See
[
Sch15
,
CS17
,
She17
,
Sch18
]
for more details. Here
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
carries the natural
D_{p}^{\times}
-action by viewing
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C}
as the base-change of the Brauer-Severi variety over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
associated to
D_{p}
via
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\rightarrow C
, and (roughly speaking) the Hodge–Tate period map is defined by considering the Hodge filtration in the linear dual of the (trivialized) universal Tate module. The Hodge–Tate period map
\pi_{K^{p},\HT}
is
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant, compatible with the
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action. It is also compatible with the following Hecke actions. Let
S
be a finite set of prime numbers containing
p
, such that
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}
is unramified for
\ell\not\in S
and
K^{p}=K^{p}_{S}K^{S}
where
K^{S}\subset G(\mathbb{A}^{S}_{f})
is a product of hyperspecial compact open subgroups. Let

\mathbb{T}^{S}=\mathbb{Z}[K^{S}\backslash G(\mathbb{A}^{S}_{f})/K^{S}]

be the abstract Hecke algebra. Then
\pi_{K^{p},\HT}
is equivariant for the Hecke action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
(with the trivial
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-action on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
).

2.1.2
Shimura sets, Drinfeld tower and the
p
-adic uniformization

The
p
-adic uniformization theorem expresses the Shimura curve
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
in terms of quotient of the Drinfeld tower of dimension
1
at infinite level by some discrete cocompact subgroups of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(see (
6
) below) which will be important in our study of the completed cohomology of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
.

First, we introduce the quaternionic Shimura set. Let
\overline{D}
be the quaternion algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
obtained from
D
by changing the local invariants exactly at the archimedean place and
p
. Let
\overline{G}
be the reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
associated to
\overline{D}
. Hence
\overline{G}(\mathbb{R})
is compact modulo the center and the
p
-component
\overline{G}_{p}:=\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of
\overline{G}(\mathbb{A})
is isomorphic to
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
induced by the splitting of
\overline{D}
at
p
. We can and we do fix an isomorphism

G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\cong\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})

so that
K^{p}
can be viewed as an open subgroup of
\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. The Shimura set

\displaystyle Z_{K^{p}}:=\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{p}

(3)

is a profinite set (by finiteness of class numbers
[
Bor63
, Theorem 5.1]
) with commuting actions of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
and
\overline{G}_{p}=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Next, we introduce the Drinfeld tower. The Drinfeld space of dimension
1
over
C
of level
0
is non-canonically given by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},0}\simeq\sqcup_{h\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{P}^{1,\mathrm{an}}_{C}\setminus\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, the base change to
C
(via
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\hookrightarrow C
) of the adic generic fiber of a formal scheme over
\Spf\mathcal{O}_{\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
, which is certain moduli space (Rapoport–Zink space) of
p
-divisible special formal
\mathcal{O}_{D_{p}}
-modules, see
[
Dri76
]
or
[
RZ96
, Theorem. 3.72]
. Let
V_{\mathrm{Dr}}
be the
dual
of the
p
-adic Tate module of the universal
p
-divisible group on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},0}
. The Drinfeld tower
(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n})_{n\geq 0}
consists of étale Galois coverings
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},0}
with the Galois group
(\mathcal{O}_{D_{p}}/p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{D_{p}})^{\times}=\mathcal{O}_{D_{p}}^{\times}/(1+p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{D_{p}})
trivializing
V_{\mathrm{Dr}}/p^{n}V_{\mathrm{Dr}}
. By
[
SW13
, Theorem 6.5.4]
, there exists a perfectoid space
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
over
C
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\sim\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}

and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
is equipped with a natural continuous left action of
D_{p}^{\times}\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. The group
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts also continuously on each layer
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}
,
n\geq 0
. The Hodge filtration of the covariant Dieudonné module of the universal
p
-divisible group defines the Gross–Hopkins/Grothendieck–Messing period map
[
SW13
, Prop. 6.5.5]

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\mathrm{GM}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\to{\mathscr{F}\ell}

where
\mathscr{F}\ell
means the adic space associated to
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C}
equipped with the natural
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action and the trivial action of
D_{p}^{\times}
. The Hodge filtration of the dual of the rational Tate module of the universal
p
-divisible group over
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
defines the Hodge–Tate period map
[
SW13
, Prop. 7.1.1]

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}.

Note that our normalization for the
D_{p}^{\times}
-action is different from
loc. cit.
by the involution of
D_{p}
and follows that of
[
Pan26
]
, since here the tower
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
trivializes the dual of the Tate modules (rather than the Tate modules). See §
2.2.1
for relevant discussions. The map
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}
is equivariant for the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times D_{p}^{\times}
-action where
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts trivially on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
.

The following theorem gives the
p
-adic uniformization of the perfectoid Shimura curve
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
.

Theorem 2.1

(Čerednik, Drinfeld)
.

We have a
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphism of adic spaces over
C
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\cong\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/K^{p}),

(4)

which is compatible with the
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-structure, the (effective) Weil descent data to
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
on both sides and the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
.

Proof.

See for example
[
Dri76
]
,
[
RZ96
, Theorem III]
,
[
BZ00
,
BZ22
]
or
[
Sch18
, §6]
for more details. More precisely, for each
n\geq 0
there exists an isomorphism

\mathcal{S}_{K^{p},n}\cong\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}\times\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/K^{p})

where
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p},n}
is the Shimura curve of level
K^{p}(1+p^{n}\mathcal{O}_{D_{p}})
. Moreover, after we replace
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
by its quotient of a discrete cyclic subgroup of the center of
\overline{G}_{p}
, the Weil descent datum on the right hand side is effective and the isomorphism is
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-rational. See
[
RZ96
, Proposition 6.16]
,
[
Har97
, (A.11)]
for example. Then we take inverse limits on
n
.
∎

Remark 2.2
.

Here, in (
4
), we note that the action of
\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
is induced from the left action of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
via the map
\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
twisted by the Cartan involution of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(the inverse transpose
g\mapsto(g^{t})^{-1}
) as in
[
Pan26
, Theorem 5.4.2]
. The action of
\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})
on
\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is given by multiplication from the left. The group
K^{p}
acts on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
trivially, and acts on
\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
by multiplication from the right.

We can rewrite the isomorphism (
4
) in terms of the Shimura set
Z_{K^{p}}=\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{p}
. There is a canonical isomorphism (cf.
[
Har97
, (A.10)]
or
[
Far04
, 4.5.2.1]
)

\displaystyle\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}\times\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/K^{p})

\displaystyle\cong(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}\times\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{p})/\overline{G}_{p},[x,yK^{p}]\mapsto[x,\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})yK^{p}]

where the right
\overline{G}_{p}
-action on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}\times\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{p}
is given by
(x,y^{p},y_{p})g=(g^{t}x,y^{p},y_{p}g)
for
x\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}
,
y^{p}\in\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
,
y_{p}\in\overline{G}_{p}\cong\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and
g\in\overline{G}_{p}
. Under the above isomorphism, we rewrite (
4
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\cong(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times(\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{p}))/\overline{G}_{p}.

(5)

This isomorphism is compatible with the
G_{p}
-actions on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
, the actions of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
and
\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{p}
.

Remark 2.3
.

Under the isomorphism (
5
), the
G_{p}=D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant Hodge–Tate period map
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\to\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
induces a
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant map on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
, which is exactly the Hodge–Tate period map on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
. See
[
She17
, Claim 3.4.2]
for more examples and the discussions below.

For further applications, it is convenient to rewrite the
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphism (
5
) as

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\cong\sqcup_{x\in Z_{K^{p}}/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\Gamma_{x}\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}.

(6)

Here,
Z_{K^{p}}
has finitely many
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-orbits. For every
x\in Z_{K^{p}}
represented by
x\in\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, we put
\Gamma_{x}=x^{-1}\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})x\cap K^{p}
as the stabilizer of
x
for the right action of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
Z_{K^{p}}
. The groups
\Gamma_{x}
are discrete cocompact subgroups of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
since
\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})
is discrete in
\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
by
[
Gro99
, Proposition 1.4]
, and after shrinking
K^{p}
if necessary, we may assume all these stabilizer groups
\Gamma_{x}
are torsion-free (cf.
[
BC91
, 5.4]
or take
\ell\neq p
such that the
\ell
-component
K_{\ell}
of
K^{p}
is torsion-free).

Let
\Gamma
be one of the above cocompact subgroups
\Gamma_{x}
for some
x
. Then
\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}:=\Gamma\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
is an adic space over
C
(as it is a component of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
). The projection map
\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\to\Gamma\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
is an analytic
\Gamma
-covering of adic spaces by the following proposition.

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
\Gamma
be a closed discrete cocompact subgroup of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. If
\Gamma
is torsion-free, then the projection map
\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\to\Gamma\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
is a local isomorphism in the following sense: for any
x\in\Gamma\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
, there exists an open affinoid perfectoid open subset
U
which contains
x
, such that
\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}^{-1}(U)
is a disjoint union
\sqcup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\gamma(V)
, with
V\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
an affinoid perfectoid open subset, such that
\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}|_{V}:V\to U
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We only need to prove similar statements for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},0}
(using
[
CDN20
, Proposition 4.2(ii)]
). The result was proved for subgroups of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acting on
\Omega=\mathbb{P}^{1,\mathrm{an}}_{C}\setminus\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
in
[
SS91
, §5, Theorem 2]
. Let
\mathcal{B}
be the Bruhat-Tits tree for
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. In the proof of
loc. cit.
, there exists an open covering of
\Omega
by open affinoids
U_{\sigma}^{a}
constructed by Drinfeld where
0<a<1
and
\sigma
vary in all simplices of
\mathcal{B}
. Moreover,
g(U_{\sigma}^{a})=U_{g(\sigma)}^{a}
and
U_{g(\sigma)}\cap U_{\sigma}=\emptyset
if
g(\sigma)\neq\sigma
. Let
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
act on
\mathcal{B}\times\mathbb{Z}
via
g.(x,m)=(g.x,m+v_{p}(\det(g)))
as in
[
RZ96
, §3.68]
. By Drinfeld, see
[
RZ96
, §3.71]
, there exists a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant isomorphism
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},0}\simeq\Omega\times\mathbb{Z}
(defined over
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
). For a simplex
\sigma=(\sigma^{\prime},m)
of
\mathcal{B}\times\mathbb{Z}
, let
U_{\sigma}^{a}
be the open affinoid
U_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{a}\times\{m\}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},0}
via the previous isomorphism. Then
(U_{\sigma}^{a})
form an open covering of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
when
\sigma
vary in all simplices of
\mathcal{B}\times\mathbb{Z}
. We still have
g(U_{\sigma}^{a})=U_{g(\sigma)}^{a}
for
g\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
U_{g(\sigma)}^{a}\cap U_{\sigma}^{a}=\emptyset
if
g(\sigma)\neq\sigma
. The stabilizer
\Gamma_{\sigma}\subset\Gamma
of each simplex
\sigma
in
\mathcal{B}
is an open compact subgroup of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Since
\Gamma
is discrete, we see that each
\Gamma_{\sigma}
is a finite group, and is furthermore trivial since
\Gamma
is torsion-free. Hence
U_{\gamma^{\prime}(\sigma)}^{a}\cap U_{\gamma(\sigma)}^{a}=\emptyset
for all
\gamma\neq\gamma^{\prime}\in\Gamma
. We can then conclude as in the proof of
[
SS91
, §5, Theorem 2]
.
∎

Moreover, as
\Gamma
acts trivially on
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
, the Hodge–Tate period map
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
descends to a map of adic spaces

\pi_{\Gamma,\HT}:\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}

which coincides with the restrictions of
\pi_{K^{p},\HT}
via (
6
).

Lemma 2.5
.

There exists a basis
\mathfrak{B}_{\Gamma}
for the analytic topology of
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
, such that each
U\in\mathfrak{B}_{\Gamma}
is an affinoid open subset,
V_{\infty}=\pi_{\Gamma,\HT}^{-1}(U)
is an affinoid perfectoid open subset inside
\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}
, and there exists a sufficiently large
n
such that
V_{\infty}=\pi_{\Gamma,n}^{-1}(V_{n})
for some
V_{n}\subset\Gamma\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}
affinoid open subset. Here
\pi_{\Gamma,n}:\Gamma\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\to\Gamma\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}
is the projection map.

Proof.

This follows from the
p
-adic uniformization (
6
) and the similar properties of
\pi_{K^{p},\HT}:\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\to\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
, see
[
BP21
, Proposition 4.4.53]
.
∎

2.1.3
The two towers

We will also need the perfectoid Lubin–Tate space (cf.
[
SW13
, §6.3]
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\sim\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}.

The
0
-th layer
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
is the base change to
C
of the adic generic fiber of of the formal scheme over
\mathrm{Spf}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
parametrizing deformations (up to quasi-isogeny) of
1
-dimensional formal
p
-divisible groups of height
2
over
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}
-copies of the open unit disk (see
[
HG94
]
or
[
RZ96
, §3.78]
). Each
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
is an étale Galois covering with Galois group
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. There is a natural continuous (left) action of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
. Also
D_{p}^{\times}
acts on each layer
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
,
n\geq 0
(using the moduli description). Similarly as for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
, we have the Gross–Hopkins period map
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\to{\mathscr{F}\ell}

[
HG94
]
and the Hodge–Tate period map
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\HT}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}

[
SW13
]
. Following
[
Pan26
]
, we twist the usual
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action (in
[
SW13
]
) on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
by the Cartan involution
g\mapsto(g^{-1})^{t}
, which corresponds to the usual
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
under the following isomorphism. As a consequence of our normalization, the actions of the centers
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
of
D_{p}^{\times}
and
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
coincide on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
.

Theorem 2.6

(
[
SW13
, Theorem 7.2.3]
)
.

There is a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\cong\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}

(7)

such that under this isomorphism,
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}
is identified with
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}
, and
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\HT}
is identified with
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\mathrm{GM}}
.

The period maps
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}=\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}
(resp.
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\HT}=\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\mathrm{GM}}
) realize
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
as a proétale
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-torsor (resp.
D_{p}^{\times}
-torsor) over
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
(resp. over
\mathbb{P}^{1,\mathrm{an}}_{C}\setminus\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset\mathscr{F}\ell
)
[
SW20
, Corollary 23.5.3]
. Besides, the map
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
factors through

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathrm{LT},0}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}}}{{\to}}\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}.

where
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},0}
denotes the projection map
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
. The map
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},0}
is a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-torsor, and there exists a basis
\mathfrak{B}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
consisting of affinoid open subsets, such that for each
U\in\mathfrak{B}
,
\pi^{-1}_{\mathrm{LT},0}(U)
is affinoid perfectoid (cf.
[
Wei16
]
,
[
CDN20
, Proposition 4.2(i)]
). The Gross–Hopkins period map
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
is étale, and there exists an open cover
\{U_{i}\}
of
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
, such that each
U_{i}
is partially proper, and
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{-1}(U_{i})
is a disjoint union of finite étale coverings of
U_{i}
(cf.
[
DJ95
, Proposition 7.2]
). Moreover, the map
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}
is surjective with local sections
[
SW13
, Lemma 6.1.4]
. We note that there are infinitely many components of
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{-1}(U_{i})
, as the geometric fibers of
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}
are
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Besides, the Hodge–Tate period map
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}
decomposes as the composition
\pi_{\Gamma,\HT}\circ\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}
, where
\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\to\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}
is a
\Gamma
-torsor for the analytic topology and a local isomorphism in the notation and in the sense of Proposition
2.4
, and
\pi_{\Gamma,\HT}:\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
is “affinoid” in the sense of Lemma
2.5
. We summarize the picture as follows

\hbox to207.22pt{\vbox to83.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 103.60768pt\lower-41.89934pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

2.2
Locally analytic vectors à la Pan

Let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}
be the completed structure sheaf on the infinite level perfectoid Drinfeld/Lubin–Tate space under the isomorphism in Theorem
2.6
. We recall some basic properties and constructions about the sheaf of locally analytic sections in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}
as in
[
Pan22
,
Pan26
]
.

Let
\check{\mathfrak{g}}:=\Lie D_{p}^{\times}\ox_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}C
. We fix a splitting throughout this article

\displaystyle D_{p}\ox_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}C\cong\Mat_{2}(C).

(8)

Using this splitting of
D_{p}
, we get a Lie algebra isomorphism
\check{\mathfrak{g}}\cong\mathfrak{g}:=\gl_{2}(C)
, where
\mathfrak{g}
is the Lie algebra of
\GL_{2}(C)
. We will identify them via this isomorphism.

2.2.1
Equivariant line bundles on the flag varieties

The splitting (
8
) induces an isomorphism
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C}\simeq(\GL_{2}/\overline{B})_{C}
where
\overline{B}
is the opposite of the Borel subgroup of upper triangular matrices
B\subset\GL_{2}
and we will also identify
\mathscr{F}\ell
with
(\GL_{2}/\overline{B})_{C}
. For any weight
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
of the Cartan subalgebra
\check{\mathfrak{h}}\simeq\mathfrak{h}
of diagonal matrices, we write
\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}^{(a,b)}
for the
\GL_{2}
-equivariant line bundle on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
associated to the character
(a,b):\overline{B}\to C,\left(\begin{matrix}x&0\\
y&z\end{matrix}\right)\mapsto x^{a}z^{b}
. And we similarly write
\omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{(a,b)}
for the line bundle on
\mathscr{F}\ell
associated to
(a,b)
(also using the Borel subgroup consisting of lower triangular matrices). Hence the dualizing sheaves are given by
\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}=\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}^{-2\rho},\Omega^{1}_{\mathscr{F}\ell}=\omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{-2\rho}
where
2\rho=(1,-1)
. In this normalization, we have (see Remark
2.7
below)

\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{*}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(a,b)}\simeq\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\mathrm{GM}}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{(-a,-b)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}(-a)

(9)

for
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, where
(-a)
denotes the
-a
th Tate twist.

Remark 2.7
.

Let
V^{(1,0)}
be the standard representation over
C
of
\GL_{2}
. The pullback along
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\HT}
of the the short exact sequence of equivariant bundles on
\mathscr{F}\ell
induced from the
\overline{B}
-sequence
0\rightarrow(0,1)\rightarrow V^{(1,0)}|_{\overline{B}}\rightarrow(1,0)\rightarrow 0

\displaystyle 0\to\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\HT}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{(0,1)}\to V^{(1,0)}\ox_{C}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}\to\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\HT}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{(1,0)}\to 0

(10)

gives the (dual) Hodge–Tate sequence (cf. (
9
)):

0\rightarrow\Lie(\mathcal{G}^{\vee})\rightarrow V_{p}(\mathcal{G})^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\rightarrow\Lie(\mathcal{G})^{-1}(-1)\rightarrow 0

on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
, where we denote by
\mathcal{G}
the univeral
p
-divisible group on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
,
V_{p}(\mathcal{G})
the rational Tate module and
\Lie(\mathcal{G})
the Lie algebra.

Let
W^{(1,0)}
denote the standard representation over
C
of
\GL_{2}
on which on which
D_{p}^{\times}
acts via (
8
). The
\overline{B}
-filtration on
W^{(1,0)}|_{\overline{B}}
together with
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}
induces the Grothendieck–Messing sequence on the infinite level
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
, which is a
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{*}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(0,1)}\to W^{(1,0)}\ox_{C}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}\to\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{*}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(1,0)}\to 0.

(11)

The above exact sequence is identified with the pullback of the sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\Lie(\mathcal{G}^{\vee})^{-1}\to M(\mathcal{G})\to\Lie(\mathcal{G})\to 0

where
M(\mathcal{G})
denotes the covariant Dieudonné crystal of
\mathcal{G}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
.

Let
\check{\mathcal{G}}
be the univeral
p
-divisible special formal
\mathcal{O}_{D_{p}}
-module over
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
. Let
N
be the isocrystal over
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
of slope
-\frac{1}{2}
so that
D_{p}=\End(N)
. Under the isomorphism (
7
), we have (see proof of
[
SW13
, Theorem 7.2.3]
or
[
FF19
, §8.1.2, §8.3.2]
)
D_{p}
-equivariant isomorphisms

M(\check{\mathcal{G}})\simeq\Hom_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(V_{p}(\mathcal{G}),N\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}),V_{p}(\check{\mathcal{G}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}\simeq\Hom(M(\mathcal{G}),N\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}})

and

\Lie(\check{\mathcal{G}})=\Hom(\Lie(\mathcal{G})(1),N\otimes_{\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}).

There are similar Hodge–Tate and Grothendieck–Messing sequences on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
for
V_{p}(\check{\mathcal{G}})^{\vee}
and
M(\check{\mathcal{G}})
corresponding to (
11
) and (
10
) respectively.

Let

\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b)}:=\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\mathrm{GM}}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{(a,b)}=\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\HT}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{(a,b)}

and

\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b)}:=\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{*}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(a,b)}=\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{*}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(a,b)}

be the pullback of the line bundles on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\simeq\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
for any
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. Then there is a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant and Galois equivariant isomorphism

\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b)}=\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-a,-b)}(-a)

by (
9
). The Grothendieck–Messing maps factor through finite levels:
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\mathrm{GM},n}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{F}\ell
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathrm{LT},n}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n}}}{{\rightarrow}}\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
. Let

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}^{(a,b)}:=\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\mathrm{GM},n}^{*}\omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{(a,b)},

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)}:=\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\mathrm{GM},n}^{*}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(a,b)}

and set

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}:=\colim_{n}\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},n}^{-1}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}^{(a,b)},

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}:=\colim_{n}\pi_{\mathrm{LT},n}^{-1}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)}.

2.2.2
Locally analytic vectors and geometric Sen thoery

Recall that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}
denotes the completed structure sheaf of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
(reps.
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}
) be the subsheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}
consisting of locally analytic sections for the
D_{p}^{\times}
-action (resp.
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action). By
[
Pan26
, Corollary 5.3.9]
,
[
QS25
, Proposition 4.3.6]
or
[
DC24
, Corollary 5.1.9]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}.

Similarly we define
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}
) as the subsheaf consisting of
D_{p}^{\times}
-smooth (resp.
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-smooth) sections. Also let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
) be the subsheaf consisting of locally algebraic sections for the
D_{p}^{\times}
-action (resp.
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-action). By
[
Eme17
, Corollary 4.2.7]
, we know

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg}}\cong\bigoplus_{W}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{W\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}},

where
W
varies in the set of irreducible algebraic representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
over
C
. Similar decomposition result also holds for
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg}}
.

For
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, we also have subsheaves
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{lalg}}\subset\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la}}

of the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant sheaf
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b)}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}
defined similarly as in §
2.2.1
. We have
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}
. Again, similar results hold on the Lubin–Tate side.

We recall below results from the geometric Sen theory, developed in
[
Pan22
,
Pan26
,
Cam22
,
DC24
]
. As in
[
Pan22
, §4.2.6]
and following Beilinson–Bernstein, let
\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{0}:=\check{\mathfrak{g}}\ox_{C}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell},\mathfrak{g}^{0}:=\mathfrak{g}\ox_{C}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{F}\ell}
, which are Lie algebroids over
\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}
or
\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{F}\ell}
. Let
\mathfrak{h}\simeq\check{\mathfrak{h}}\subset\check{\mathfrak{g}}\simeq\mathfrak{g}=\gl_{2}
be the Cartan subalgebra consisting of diagonal matrices. Let
\check{\mathfrak{n}}^{0}\subset\check{\mathfrak{b}}^{0}\subset\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{0}
be the universal nilpotent subalgebra and the univeral Borel subalgebra over
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
, with
(\check{\mathfrak{n}}^{0})^{\vee}
the dual of
\check{\mathfrak{n}}^{0}
. Similarly, let
\mathfrak{n}^{0}\subset\mathfrak{b}^{0}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{0}
be the univeral nilpotent subalgebra and the universal Borel subalgebra over
\mathscr{F}\ell
. The preimage of these Lie algebroids, e.g.
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{0}
, acts on the locally analytic vectors
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
by derivations.

Let
\mathcal{Z}(\check{\mathfrak{g}})
be the center of the universal enveloping algebra of
\check{\mathfrak{g}}
which is isomorphic to
S(\check{\mathfrak{h}})^{W}
via the Harish-Chandra isomorphism, where
W
is the Weyl group for
\check{\mathfrak{g}}
and
W
acts on
S(\check{\mathfrak{h}})
via the dot action. Recall that
\mathcal{Z}(\check{\mathfrak{g}})
is a
C
-algebra generated by
z=\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right)
and the Casimir operator
\Omega=u^{+}u^{-}+u^{-}u^{+}+\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\in Z(U(\check{\mathfrak{g}}))
, where
u^{+}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)
,
u^{-}=\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{matrix}\right)
and
h=\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{matrix}\right)
. Here we used our fixed isomorphism
\check{\mathfrak{g}}\cong\mathfrak{g}
to define these elements
z
and
\Omega
.

As in
[
Pan22
, §5.1.4]
or
[
Cam24
, Theorem 6.3.6]
, there is an action of the arithmetic Sen operator
\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}
on
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}
. We also have the following relation between the arithmetic Sen operator, the horizontal action
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}
of the Cartan subalgebra
\check{\mathfrak{h}}
(defined below), and the action of the center
\mathcal{Z}(\check{\mathfrak{g}})
.

Theorem 2.8
.

The following statements hold true:

(i)

The locally analytic vectors
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
is killed by the “geometric Sen operators”
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\check{\mathfrak{n}}^{0}
and
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\HT}^{-1}\mathfrak{n}^{0}
.

(ii)

Let
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}
(resp.
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}
) be the horizontal action of
\check{\mathfrak{h}}\simeq\mathfrak{h}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
) via
\check{\mathfrak{b}}^{0}/\check{\mathfrak{n}}^{0}\simeq\check{\mathfrak{h}}\otimes_{C}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}
(resp.
\mathfrak{b}^{0}/\mathfrak{n}^{0}\simeq\mathfrak{h}\otimes_{C}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{F}\ell}
). Then
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}=\theta_{\mathfrak{h}}
.

(iii)

The action of
z=\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right)
and
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}(z)
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
are the same.

(iv)

We have
\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}=\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)
and
(2\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}-z+1)^{2}-1=2\Omega
as operators on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to
[
Pan22
, Theorem 5.1.8]
,
[
Pan26
, Proposition 6.5.4]
, and the results also follow by
[
Cam24
, Theorem 5.2.5, Theorem 6.3.6]
.
Here, we note that in
[
Pan22
]
Pan uses the upper triangular matrices to define the flag variety, and we use the lower triangular matrices. So that there is a small difference about the description of results on horizontal actions of
\check{\mathfrak{h}}
.
∎

Some of the above results on various operators on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
also generalize to certain twists of these sheaves. As the nilpotent Lie algebra
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\check{\mathfrak{n}}^{0}
kills
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}
, there is also a horizontal action
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}
of
\check{\mathfrak{h}}
on
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la}}
. For
(a,b),(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}
(resp.
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la},(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}
) be the subsheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
(resp.
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la}}
) such that the action of
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}
is given by the weight
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
. Using the fact that
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}
acts on
\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}^{(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}
via
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
and (
9
), we have the following formula relating these twists:

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(a,b)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a^{\prime},b^{\prime}),\mathrm{sm}}

\displaystyle\cong\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a^{\prime},b^{\prime}),\mathrm{la},(a,b)},

(12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(a,b)}\ox_{\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}}\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}^{(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\cong\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(-a^{\prime},-b^{\prime}),\mathrm{la},(a+a^{\prime},b+b^{\prime})}(-a^{\prime}).

(13)

Using the above formulae, one can check that

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\Omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}^{1}\ox_{\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,0)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(-1,1)}(1).

We can calculate some
W
-locally algebraic vectors explicitly for some algebraic representation
W
of
D_{p}^{\times}
over
C
.

Proposition 2.9
.

For
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2},a\geq b
, let
W^{(a,b)}
be the algebraic representation of
D_{p}^{\times}\subset\GL_{2}(C)
over
C
of highest weight
(a,b)
(with respect to the Borel subgroup consisting of upper triangular matrices). Then

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(a,b)}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{W^{(a,b)}\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}\simeq W^{(a,b)}\otimes_{C}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}(a).

Proof.

See
[
QS25
, §3.4]
for example. We briefly illustrate the case when
(a,b)=(0,-1)
. The subsheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(a,b)}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg}}
is the subsheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(a,b)}
consisting of sections that are locally algebraic for the
D_{p}^{\times}
-action. The first equality can be obtained by explicit descriptions of the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(a,b)}
in terms of certain power series expansions as in
[
Pan26
, Theorem 3.2.2]
. Next, using (
11
) together with (
9
), we deduce an exact sequence

0\to\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(0,-1)}\to W^{(1,0)}\otimes_{C}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}\to\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(-1,0)}(-1)\to 0.

Then the term
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(0,-1),\mathrm{sm}}
will contribute to the locally
(W^{(1,0)})^{*}
-algebraic part of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}
. The general cases follow similarly.
∎

2.2.3
The differential operators

There are various differential operators on

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)},k\geq 0

that are constructed in
[
Pan26
, §4]
, which extend the connections on the corresponding algebraic parts.

The differential operator (see
[
Pan26
, Remark 5.3.18]
or
[
QS25
, Proposition 4.3.10]
)

d_{\mathrm{Dr}}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\otimes(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}})^{\otimes k+1}

extends the connection on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\simeq W^{(0,-k)}\otimes_{C}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}
, or more classically the Gauss–Manin connections

\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}^{(0,-k)}\rightarrow\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}^{(0,-k)}\otimes(\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}^{1})^{\otimes(k+1)}\simeq\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}^{(-k-1,1)}.

(14)

Remark 2.10
.

These connections are pullbacks of the connections on
\omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{(0,-k)}
via the étale map
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}\to\mathscr{F}\ell
. We note that we use the Borel subgroup consisting of lower triangular matrices to define the flag variety
\mathscr{F}\ell
(in particular
\omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{(1,0)}
is ample). When
k=0
, it’s clear that
d_{\mathrm{Dr}}
is the pullback of the de Rham complex on
\mathscr{F}\ell
. For general
k
, the connection can also be constructed by translations of the de Rham complex as in
[
Pan26
, Lemma 5.6.6]
or as in the usual BGG construction
[
Fal06
]
. We use the notation of Remark
2.7
. Since we have trivialized the univeral Dieudonné module
M(\check{\mathcal{G}})
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},0}

[
RZ96
, Proposition 5.15]
which coincides with
V^{(1,0)}\ox_{C}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}
up to a multiplicity space given by a finite-dimensional
D_{p}^{\times}
-space, the connection on
V^{(1,0)}\ox_{C}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}
is trivial on
V^{(1,0)}
(cf.
[
Van24
, Lemma 3.11]
). Taking a symmetric power of the dual of the Dieudonné module, we see the connection

\displaystyle V^{(0,-k)}\ox\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}\to V^{(0,-k)}\ox\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}

(15)

is trivial on
V^{(0,-k)}
and is given by the usual differential map on the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}
. Besides, the map (
15
) is equivariant for the
\GL_{2}(L)
-action. Taking the part of (
15
) with infinitesimal character equaling to the infinitesimal character of
V^{(0,-k)}
(i.e. the translation of the usual differential map
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}\to\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}
from the trivial weight to the weight
(0,-k)
), we obtain the desired map (
14
), which is quasi-isomorphic to (
15
) as complexes by
[
Pan26
, Lemma 5.6.6]
. On the other hand, there is a Hodge filtration on
V^{(0,-k)}\ox\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}
(given by the dual of the Hodge-de Rham sequence (
10
) if
k=1
) so that the map (
15
) satisfies the Griffiths transversality. The induced map given by (
15
) is an isomorphism on all graded pieces of the Hodge filtration except the top one
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},n}}^{(0,-k)}
and induces (
14
) (cf.
[
Van24
, Lemma 9.8]
).

The differential operator (
[
Pan26
, §5.3.16]
)

\overline{d}_{\mathrm{Dr}}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\Omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{1})^{\ox k+1}

arises from the action of the differential operators on the flag variety
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
, extending a twist of the pullback of the connection

\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(0,-k)}\rightarrow\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(0,-k)}\otimes(\Omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}^{1})^{\otimes(k+1)}\simeq\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}^{(-k-1,1)}.

(16)

There are also twists of these differential operators, which we denote them by
d_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{\prime},\overline{d}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{\prime}
. We summarize the results as follows.

Theorem 2.11
.

Let
k\geq 0
be an integer. There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}})^{\ox k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\Omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{1})^{\ox k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\mathrm{Dr}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{d}_{\mathrm{Dr}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{d}_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{\prime}}

such that:

(i)

The operator
d_{\mathrm{Dr}}
is
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}
-linear, and is given by the Gauss–Manin connection (
14
) when restricted to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}
.

(ii)

The operator
\overline{d}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
is
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}
-linear. If we twist
\overline{d}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
by
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}
, its restriction on
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(0,-k)}
is induced by the differential (
16
).

(iii)

The map
\overline{d}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
is surjective, with kernel
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}
.

(iv)

d^{\prime}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
is a twist of
d_{\mathrm{Dr}}
by
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}(\Omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{1})^{\ox k+1}
, and
\overline{d}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
is a twist of
\overline{d}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
by
(\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}
.

Proof.

This is
[
Pan26
, Theorem 5.2.15, Theorem 5.2.16, Theorem 5.3.17]
except that we don’t take the direct image along the period maps. We note that by (
12
) and (
13
) there is an isomorphism

(\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\Omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{1})^{\ox k+1}\ox_{\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}})^{\ox k+1}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1).

The construction of these differential operators follow by the explicit description of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}
in terms of power series expansions as in
[
Pan26
, Theorem 3.2.2]
.
∎

The above theorem also holds
mutatis mutandis
for the differential operators
d_{\mathrm{LT}},\overline{d}_{\mathrm{LT}}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}
(and their twists) in
[
Pan26
, §5.2.14]
, replacing everywhere “
\mathrm{Dr}
” by “
\mathrm{LT}
” and “
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
” by “
\mathscr{F}\ell
”. In particular, we also have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}})^{\ox k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\HT}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{F}\ell}}(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\HT}^{-1}\Omega_{\mathscr{F}\ell}^{1})^{\ox k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\mathrm{LT}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{d}_{\mathrm{LT}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{d}_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\mathrm{LT}}^{\prime}}

where
\overline{d}_{\mathrm{LT}}
is surjective with kernel
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}
. It turns out that (up to a non-zero constant)
d_{\mathrm{LT}}
coincides with
\overline{d}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
and
\overline{d}_{\mathrm{LT}}
coincides with
d_{\mathrm{Dr}}
, and the above diagram is essentially the same as the one in Theorem
2.11
.

Theorem 2.12
.

Under the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}
, we have
d_{\mathrm{Dr}}=c\overline{d}_{\mathrm{LT}},\overline{d}_{\mathrm{Dr}}=c^{\prime}d_{\mathrm{LT}}
for some invertible constants
c,c^{\prime}
.

Proof.

This is
[
Pan26
, Theorem 5.3.20]
and
[
QS25
, Theorem 4.3.12]
.
∎

The identification of these operators allows us to compute the cohomology of the differential operator
d_{\mathrm{Dr}}
.

Corollary 2.13
.

The differential operator
d_{\mathrm{Dr}}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}})^{\ox k+1}
is surjective, with kernel
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}
. Similar results hold for
d_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{\prime}
.

From the above corollary we obtain a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{\mathrm{Dr}}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}})^{\ox k+1}\rightarrow 0

(17)

which is
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant. This sequence will be crucial for our study of the locally analytic representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
from the compactly supported cohomology groups of these sheaves and the completed cohomology of the quaternionic Shimura curve.

2.2.4
Passage to the Shimura curve and the flag variety

So far we have discussed some equivariant line bundles on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
in §
2.2.1
, subsheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la}}
§
2.2.2
and the differential operators in §
2.2.3
. These objects and constructions are
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant, hence descend to the analytic quotients
\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}=\Gamma\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
where
\Gamma\subset\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a closed cocompact subgroup satisfying the assumption for Proposition
2.4
. Via the
p
-adic uniformization
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}=\sqcup_{x\in Z_{K^{p}}/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma_{x}}
in the notation of (
6
), we can obtain corresponding objects on the Shimura curve
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
. Note that we can make the same constructions directly on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
as is done in
[
Pan26
]
or
[
QS25
]
.

Recall that
\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}
is the quotient map and
\pi_{K^{p},\HT}:\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\to\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
is the Hodge–Tate map for
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
. Let
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\oplus 2}
and
k\geq 0
. In summary, there are

•

automorphic line bundles
\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{(a,b)}=\pi_{K^{p},\HT}^{*}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(-a,-b)}(-a)
and the smooth vectors
\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}
for
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that

\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}^{-1}(\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{(a,b)}|_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}})=\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b)},\quad\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}^{-1}(\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}|_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}})=\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}.
