In particular,
\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
is naturally identified with
\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{(-1,1),\mathrm{sm}}
.

•

subsheaves of

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{W\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{lalg}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}},

and similarly subsheaves of
\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la}}
, whose pullbacks to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
via
\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}
coincides with the corresponding sheaves on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
in §
2.2.2
. These sheaves are killed by the nilpotent Lie algebra
\pi_{K^{p},\HT}^{-1}\check{\mathfrak{n}}^{0}
and also admit horizontal actions of
\check{\mathfrak{h}}
still denoted by
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}
.

•

the subsheaves
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})},\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la},(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}
, etc. where
\check{\mathfrak{h}}
acts via the weight
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
whose pullback to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
coincide with
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la},(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}
, etc.

•

the differential operators
d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}},\overline{d}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}},d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\prime},\overline{d}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}
for
k\geq 0
, which satisfy the results of Theorem
2.11
replacing everywhere “
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
” by “
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
” and “
\mathrm{Dr}
” by “
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
”. All these differential operators are surjection of sheaves as in (iii) of Theorem
2.11
and Corollary
2.13
, which will be discussed in Proposition
2.15
below.

Remark 2.14
.

The sheaves
\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}
we constructed above coincide with the usual automorphic vector bundles on the Shimura varieties constructed using the Grothendieck–Messing period maps, see
[
DLLZ23
, Remark 5.2.11, Corollary 5.3.3]
.

Finally we can pushforward the above sheaves along the “affinoid” map
\pi_{K^{p},\HT}:\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\to\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
to obtain sheaves on
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
and the corresponding differential operators. We set, exactly as in
[
Pan22
,
Pan26
]
or
[
QS25
]
,

•

\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}:=\pi_{K^{p},\HT,*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}},\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}:=\pi_{K^{p},\HT,*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{sm}},\omega_{K^{p}}^{(a,b)}:=\pi_{K^{p},\HT,*}\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{(a,b)}
, etc.

•

the subsheaf of locally analytic or locally algebraic vectors

\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{W\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}

of the
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}
. There are similar subsheaves of
\omega_{K^{p}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la}}
. These sheaves are killed by the nilpotent Lie algebra
\check{\mathfrak{n}}^{0}
and also admit horizontal actions of
\check{\mathfrak{h}}
still denoted by
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}
, hence are equivariant twisted
\mathcal{D}
-modules in a suitable sense (e.g.
[
BCGP25
, §3]
).

•

the subsheaves
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}},\omega_{K^{p}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la},(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}\subset\omega_{K^{p}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{la}}
, etc. where
\check{\mathfrak{h}}
acts via the weight
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.

•

there are differential operators as in Theorem
2.11

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{p}})^{\ox k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{K^{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{d}_{K^{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{d}_{K^{p}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{\prime}_{K^{p}}}

(18)

for
k\geq 0
integers.

Proposition 2.15
.

Let
d_{K^{p}},\overline{d}_{K^{p}},d^{\prime}_{K^{p}},\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}}
be the differential operators on
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}
in (
18
). Then the following statements hold:

(1)

The map
\overline{d}_{K^{p}}:\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1}
is surjective, with kernel

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\cong W^{(0,-k)}\ox_{C}\omega_{K^{p}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}.

(19)

Similarly, the map
\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}}:\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)
is surjective, with kernel
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}\cong W^{(0,-k)}\ox_{C}\omega_{K^{p}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}}
.

(2)

The maps
d_{K^{p}}:\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}
and
d^{\prime}_{K^{p}}:\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)
are surjective.

Proof.

(1) The statement for
\overline{d}_{K^{p}}
can be proved using similar arguments as in
[
Pan26
, Proposition 4.2.9]
and
\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}}
is a twist of
\overline{d}_{K^{p}}
.

(2) Since
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}=\sqcup_{x\in Z_{K^{p}}/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma_{x}}
and each map
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma_{x}}
is a local isomorphism (Proposition
2.4
), the differential operator
d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}
is surjective. To show that
d_{K^{p}}
is surjective, we show that there exists a covering of
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
by affinoid open subset
V\subset\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
such that the map

d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}(\pi_{\Gamma,\HT}^{-1}(V))\rightarrow(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}}|_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}})^{\ox k+1})(\pi_{\Gamma,\HT}^{-1}(V))

(20)

is surjective for any
\Gamma
satisfying the assumption of Proposition
2.4
. Let
V\subset\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
be an affinoid open subset such that

\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{-1}(V)=\sqcup_{g\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}gU,

where
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
is the Gross–Hopkins map discussed in the end of §
2.1.3
and
U\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
is an affinoid open subset such that

\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}|_{U}
is finite étale. We claim that for such open subset
V
the map (
20
) is surjective.

By Corollary
2.13
, the differential operator
d_{\rm Dr}
is surjective on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
, with kernel
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}
. For an affinoid open
U_{\infty}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\simeq\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
that is the pullback of an open affinoid subset of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
, we have
H^{1}(U_{\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})=0
using Tate’s acyclicity (see the proof of Lemma
2.22
below). We see that the differential

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{Dr}}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}(U_{\infty})\to(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})(U_{\infty})

(21)

is still surjective after taking sections on such affinoid open subsets
U_{\infty}
.

Let
U_{\infty}
be the pre-image of
U
inside
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
via
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
. Then
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariantly

\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{-1}(V)=\sqcup_{g\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}gU_{\infty}.

Let
\Gamma\subset\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be a discrete cocompact torsion-free subgroup. The assumption on
\Gamma
implies that
\Gamma\cap c\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})c^{-1}=\{1\}
for any
c\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Hence

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{-1}(V)=\sqcup_{g\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}gU_{\infty}=\sqcup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\sqcup_{c\in\Gamma\backslash\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\gamma cU_{\infty}.

Therefore,
\pi_{\Gamma,\HT}^{-1}(V)=\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{-1}(V)\cong\sqcup_{c\in\Gamma\backslash\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}c\cdot U_{\infty}
is isomorphic to a finite copy of
U_{\infty}
. As a consequence, the map (
20
) can be identified with a finite direct sum of copies of the map (
21
). In particular, the map (
20
) is surjective, equivalently, the map

\displaystyle d_{K^{p}}:\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}(V)\to(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{{K^{p}}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})(V)

is surjective.

Since
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
can be covered by
V
such that (
20
) is surjective and
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
is a disjoint union of finitely many components of the form
\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}
, we conclude that
d_{K^{p}}
is a surjective map of sheaves. Finally, as
d^{\prime}_{K^{p}}
is a twist of
d_{K^{p}}
, we deduce that
d^{\prime}_{K^{p}}
is also surjective.
∎

2.2.5
The intertwining operator on
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}

Let
k\geq 0
. We define the intertwining operator on
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}
as
[
Pan26
, Definition 4.3.1]
.

Definition 2.16
.

We denote
I:=d^{\prime}_{K^{p}}\circ\overline{d}_{K^{p}}=\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}}\circ d_{K^{p}}
to be the composition of these operators in (
18
), and we call
I
the intertwining operator.

In the same way as
[
Pan26
, §6]
, we can interpret the intertwining operator Galois-theoretically as the Fontaine operator
[
Fon04
]
. We sketch the construction of the Fontaine operator. Let
\mathbb{B}_{\dR,\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{+}
be the positive de Rham period sheaf on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
(we mean the slice of the pro-étale sheaf on the analytic site of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
). Define
\mathbb{B}_{\dR}^{+}:=\pi_{K^{p},\HT,*}\mathbb{B}_{\dR,\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
, which has a decreasing filtration given by
\Fil^{i}\mathbb{B}_{\dR}^{+}=\pi_{K^{p},\HT,*}\Fil^{i}\mathbb{B}_{\dR,\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
, with graded pieces
\gr^{i}\mathbb{B}_{\dR}^{+}\cong\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}(i)
. Here we use
R^{j}\pi_{K^{p},\HT,*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}=0
for
j\geq 1
. For
k\geq 0
an integer, put
\mathbb{B}_{\dR,k}^{+}:=\mathbb{B}_{\dR}^{+}/\Fil^{k}\mathbb{B}_{\dR}^{+}
. For
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, let
\widetilde{\chi}_{(a,b)}
be the character of
Z(\check{\mathfrak{g}})
such that
Z(\check{\mathfrak{g}})
acts on the Verma module
U(\check{\mathfrak{g}})\ox_{U(\check{\mathfrak{b}})}(a,b)
via
\widetilde{\chi}_{(a,b)}
. There is an induced filtration on
\mathbb{B}_{\dR,k+2}^{+,\mathrm{la},\widetilde{\chi}_{(0,-k)}}
from
\mathbb{B}_{\dR}^{+}
and one can show that
\mathbb{B}_{\dR,k+2}^{+,\mathrm{la},\widetilde{\chi}_{(0,-k)}}
has
i
-th graded pieces given by
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},\widetilde{\chi}_{(0,-k)}}(i)
, where
i=0,1,...,k+1
. Besides, the arithmetic Sen operator
\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}
acts on
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},\widetilde{\chi}_{(0,-k)}}(i)
via
i,-k-1+i
. In
[
Pan26
, §6]
, Pan developed a Sen theory for colimits of Banach spaces which are also
B_{\dR}^{+}/\Fil^{k}B_{\dR}^{+}
-modules. For an open affinioid
U\subset\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
as in Lemma
2.5
, we may assume there exists a finite extension
K
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
such that
U
is defined over
K
. Let
\zeta_{p^{n}}
be a primitive
p^{n}
-th root of unity, and let
K_{\infty}\subset\varinjlim_{n}K(\zeta_{p^{n}})
be the maximal
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-extension. For
W
an LB
B_{\dR}^{+}/\Fil^{k}B_{\dR}^{+}
-module, let
W^{K}\subset W
be the subspace of
\Gal(\overline{K}/K_{\infty})
-invariant,
\Gal(K_{\infty}/K)
-analytic vectors in
W
. It turns out
\mathbb{B}_{\dR,k+2}^{+,\mathrm{la},\widetilde{\chi}_{(0,-k)}}(U)
satisfies the assumption in
[
Pan26
, 6.1.17]
, and
\mathbb{B}_{\dR,k+2}^{+,\mathrm{la},\widetilde{\chi}_{(0,-k)}}(U)^{K}
has
i
-th graded pieces given by
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},\widetilde{\chi}_{(0,-k)}}(U)^{K}(i)
. Then the action of
\Lie\Gamma=\Lie\Gal(K_{\infty}/K)
induces a map

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}(U)^{K}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(U)^{K}(k+1).

(22)

This map is functorial, and after we base change to
C
it induces a natural sheaf morphism

\displaystyle N:\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)

(23)

which we call it the Fontaine operator. Roughly speaking, the Fontaine operator measures the nilpotency of the arithmetic Sen action on
\mathbb{B}_{\dR,k+2}^{+,\mathrm{la},\widetilde{\chi}_{(0,-k)}}
. Using the machinery developed in
[
Pan26
, §6]
, similar arguments as for
[
Pan26
, Theorem 6.2.6]
give the following result.

Theorem 2.17
.

Up to an invertible constant, the Fontaine operator
N
equals to the intertwining operator
I
.

2.2.6
Completed cohomology of quaternionic Shimura curves

Recall that
S_{K^{p}K_{p}}
is the quaternionic Shimura curve of level
K^{p}K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}^{\infty})
, which is a complete algebraic curve over
\mathbb{Q}
. The completed cohomology groups associated to the tower
\{S_{K^{p}K_{p}}\}_{K_{p}\subset D_{p}^{\times}}
are given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{i}(K^{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle:=(\varprojlim_{n}\varinjlim_{K_{p}\subset D_{p}^{\times}}H^{i}(S_{K^{p}K_{p}}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}))[\frac{1}{p}]

\displaystyle\simeq(\varprojlim_{n}\varinjlim_{K_{p}\subset D_{p}^{\times}}H^{i}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(S_{K^{p}K_{p},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}))[\frac{1}{p}]

for
i\geq 0
, which carry commuting actions of
D_{p}^{\times}
, the Galois group
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
and the Hecke algebra
\mathbb{T}^{S}
. Note that
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\widehat{H}^{1}(K^{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is a unitary Banach representation of
D_{p}^{\times}
(cf.
[
Eme06
, §4.1]
). Given any Banach space
W
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, let

\widetilde{H}^{i}(K^{p},W):=\widetilde{H}^{i}(K^{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p})\widehat{\ox}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}W.

Recall that
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}=\pi_{K^{p},\HT,*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
with
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
the completed structure sheaf on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
, and
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}
the subsheaf consisting of locally analytic sections.

Theorem 2.18
.

For
i\geq 0
, we have
D_{p}^{\times}\times\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant isomorphisms

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{i}(K^{p},C)

\displaystyle\cong H^{i}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}})

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{i}(K^{p},C)^{\mathrm{la}}

\displaystyle\cong H^{i}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}).

Proof.

The first one is the primitive comparison theorem
[
Sch13b
]
, cf.
[
Sch15
, §4.2]
. The second one follows from
[
Pan22
, Theorem 4.4.6]
or
[
Cam24
, Theorem 1.1.8]
.
∎

Let
E
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For any compact open subgroup
K_{p}\subset D_{p}^{\times}
, we define
\mathbb{T}(K^{p}K_{p})\subset\End_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(H^{1}(S_{K^{p}K_{p}}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
as the image of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
. Then we set

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}(K^{p}):=\varprojlim_{K_{p}}\mathbb{T}(K^{p}K_{p}).

It acts faithfully on
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)
and commutes with the action of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\times D_{p}^{\times}
. Moreover, the action of
\mathbb{T}(K^{p})
and
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
on
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)
satisfy the usual Eichler-Shimura congruence relation:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}^{2}-T_{\ell}\circ\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}+\ell S_{\ell}=0

(24)

where
\ell\notin S
is a prime number,
\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}
denotes the geometric Frobenius at
\ell
and

T_{\ell}=[\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\begin{pmatrix}\ell&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})],S_{\ell}=[\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\begin{pmatrix}\ell&0\\
0&\ell\end{pmatrix}\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})]

are the usual Hecke operators (cf.
[
Car86
, §10.3, Remarque 0.3]
, while our normalization follows those in
[
Eme11
,
Pan22
,
Pan26
]
and the choice of the
G(\mathbb{R})
-conjugacy class
h
in §
2.1.1
, hence the Galois action (the canonical model), is different from those in
[
Car86
]
).

Let
\rho
be a
2
-dimensional continuous
E
-linear absolutely irreducible representation of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. Set

\displaystyle\check{\Pi}(\rho):=\Hom_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}(\rho,\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)).

(25)

Suppose that
\check{\Pi}(\rho)\neq 0
. This is a unitary Banach representation of
D_{p}^{\times}
. From
\rho
, we can associate a character
\lambda:\mathbb{T}(K^{p})\to E
using the congruence relation (
24
), such that (cf.
[
Eme11
, Prop. 6.1.12]
)

\displaystyle\Hom_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}(\rho,\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E))=\Hom_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}(\rho,\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)[\lambda]).

Moreover, by the main result of
[
BLR91
]
, as
\rho
is
2
-dimensional and absolutely irreducible, we know that
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)[\lambda]
is
\rho
-isotypic:

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)[\lambda]\cong\rho\ox_{E}\check{\Pi}(\rho).

(26)

Similar to the treatment in
[
Pan26
, Theorem 7.2.2]
and
[
QS25
, Theorem 5.4.2]
, we get the following result on a first step towards the description of the locally analytic part of the isotypic space
\check{\Pi}(\rho)
. Let
I^{1}
be the operator induced by the intertwining operator
I
on the level of cohomology groups

\displaystyle I^{1}:H^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})\to H^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)).

Theorem 2.19
.

Let
\rho
be a
2
-dimensional
E
-linear continuous absolutely irreducible representation of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. Suppose:

(i)

\rho
appears in
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)^{\mathrm{la}}
, namely
\Hom_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}(\rho,\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)^{\mathrm{la}})\neq 0
.

(ii)

\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
is de Rham of Hodge–Tate weight
0,k+1
with
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Then after fixing an embedding
E\hookrightarrow C
, we have

\displaystyle\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}\widehat{\ox}_{E}C\cong\ker I^{1}[\lambda].

where
\lambda:\mathbb{T}^{S}\rightarrow C
is the Hecke eigensystem associated to
\rho
.

Proof.

The proof is literally the same as in
[
Pan26
, Theorem 7.2.2]
or
[
QS25
, Theorem 5.4.2]
. Roughly speaking, the regular de Rhamness condition of
\rho
and the assumption
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}\neq 0
ensures that the multiplicity space
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
is killed by the Fontaine operator (
23
), which can be identified with the intertwining operator using Theorem
2.17
.
∎

2.3
A product formula

Motivated by Theorem
2.19
, we proceed to study the cohomology of the intertwining operator
I
on
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{(0,-k)}
. Let
k\geq 0
and let

\displaystyle d_{K^{p}}:\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}

be the differential map we constructed in §
2.2.4
. Being part of the intertwining operator for which we need to understand the cohomology, we will need to compute the cohomology of
\ker d_{K^{p}}
and determine the Hecke action on it (Proposition
2.25
).

The lemma below shows that the cohomology of
\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
is the same as the cohomology of
\ker d_{K^{p}}
on
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
.

Lemma 2.20
.

There is a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle R\Gamma(S_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})\cong R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\ker d_{K^{p}}).

(27)

Proof.

By Proposition
2.15
(2), we know
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{K^{p}}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}
is surjective. Hence the sheaves
\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}},\ker d_{K^{p}}
admit resolutions:

\displaystyle 0\to\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to\ker d_{K^{p}}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{K^{p}}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}\to 0.

By definition,
\pi_{K^{p},\HT,*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}=\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}
. Also
R^{j}\pi_{K^{p},\HT,*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}=0
for
j\geq 1
, the proof of which is similar to the one in
[
Pan26
, Proposition 3.2.8]
. Indeed, using explicit power series expansions of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}
, one can locally exhibit
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}
as colimits of sheaves isomorphic to “direct products” of
\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}
. More precisely, for
U\subset\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
a good affinoid perfectoid open subset (which comes from the pullback of an affinioid open subset from finite level and an affinioid open subset from the flag variety along
\pi_{\HT}
), then
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}(U)
can be written as a filtered colimit (with injective transition maps)
\varinjlim_{n}A^{n}
, with each
A^{n}\cong(\prod^{\infty}_{i=0}\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{n}^{\prime}}}(U_{K_{n}^{\prime}}))\ox_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(for some open compact subgroup
K_{n}^{\prime}\subset D_{p}^{\times}
and
U_{K_{n}^{\prime}}
is an affinioid open in
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{n}^{\prime}}
whose preimage is
U
) by sending the power series expansion to its coefficients. Then one deduces the desired vanishing result using Tate’s acyclicity of coherent sheaves on affinioid open subsets. Similar results are valid for the third terms in the above two exact sequences. Hence
R\pi_{K^{p},\HT,*}\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}=\ker d_{K^{p}}[0]
and the Leray spectral sequence degenerates, giving the desired isomorphism.
∎

Instead of computing
\ker d_{K^{p}}
directly, using the
p
-adic uniformization, we first pass these sheaves from
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
. Let
\Gamma\subset\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be a cocompact torsion-free discrete subgroup appearing in (
6
). Let
d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}:=d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}|_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}
and similarly
d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}^{\prime}:=d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}
where
\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}/\Gamma
. By the construction and an analogue of Proposition
2.9
, we have

\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}^{-1}\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}=\ker d_{\mathrm{Dr}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}=V^{(0,-k)}\otimes\omega_{{}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}.

(28)

Then we descend via the
\Gamma
-torsor
\pr_{\Gamma,\infty}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}
and exhibit the results using
\Gamma
-(co)homology for cocompact torsion-free discrete subgroups
\Gamma\subset\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(Lemma
2.21
below). We find that it is better to descend compactly supported cohomology instead of cohomology of sheaves on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
, partly because that the cover
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\to\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}
is an “infinite-sheet” étale covering map. In order to handle the compactly supported cohomology groups, we develop some results on proper pushforwards of abelian sheaves on adic spaces in Appendix
A
.

Lemma 2.21
.

The
\Gamma
-covering
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\simeq\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}/\Gamma
induces
D_{p}^{\times}
-isomorphisms

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}),

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,k+1),\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}^{L}\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle\simeq R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}^{\prime}).

Proof.

Let
*/\Gamma
is the classifying stack of the group
\Gamma
, let
p:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}/\Gamma\to*/\Gamma
and
f:*/\Gamma\to*
be the natural maps. The desired isomorphism for
\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}
arises from that the composition of “proper pushforwards”:

Rf_{!}Rp_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}=R(f\circ p)_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}.

For a rigorous proof, we apply Proposition
A.24
for
{\mathscr{F}}=\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}},X=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty},Y=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}/\Gamma
and
g=f\circ p
. Note that
\Gamma
has finite cohomological dimension
[
BS76
, §6.1]
.

The map
d^{\prime}_{\mathrm{Dr}}
is a twist of
d_{\mathrm{Dr}}
by
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}^{-1}(\Omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{1})^{\otimes k+1}=\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{-1}(\Omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{1})^{\otimes k+1}=\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{-1}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(-k-1,k+1)}
over
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}
by (iv) of Theorem
2.11
. Hence

\ker d^{\prime}_{\mathrm{Dr}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\otimes_{\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}^{-1}\omega_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}^{(-k-1,k+1)}=\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,k+1),\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}.

The result for
\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}^{\prime}
follows similarly using Proposition
A.24
again.
∎

When
k=0
, we have
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}
and
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,k+1),\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}=\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-1,1),\mathrm{sm}}=\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}
. The cohomologies
R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}})
and
R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-1,1),\mathrm{sm}})
are (derived) smooth representations of
G=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by Lemma
2.22
below. Hence they are naturally (left) modules over the smooth Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}(G)=\varinjlim_{K}\mathcal{H}(G,K)
, where
K
runs through open compact subgroups of
G
and
\mathcal{H}(G,K)=\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(K\backslash G/K,C)
. See for example
[
Ren10
, II.3.12, III.1.4]
or
[
Man04
, §5.1]
.

For general
k\geq 0
, by (
28
),

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\ker d_{\mathrm{Dr}})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\otimes_{C}V^{(0,-k)}

(29)

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\ker d_{\mathrm{Dr}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}})\otimes_{C}V^{(0,-k)}

(30)

are locally
V^{(0,-k)}
-algebraic representations of
G
.

Lemma 2.22
.

For
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}})\cong\varinjlim_{n}R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\omega^{(a,b)}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})=\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\omega^{(a,b)}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})[-1].

Proof.

Recall that
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}
can be equivalently defined as follows: given an affinoid open subset
U_{\infty}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
, which we assume that there is a sufficiently large
n
such that
U_{\infty}
is the pre-image of an affinoid open subset
U_{n}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
. Then
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}(U_{\infty})=\varinjlim_{m\geq n}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},m}}^{(a,b)}(\pi_{m,n}^{-1}(U))
with
\pi_{m,n}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},m}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
the natural projection. Let
U_{0}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
be an affinoid open subset, and we denote its pre-image in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
being
U_{n}
and its pre-image in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
being
U_{\infty}
. By
[
Sch22
, Proposition 14.9]
applied to
Y_{0}=U_{n}
with
Y_{i}
’s being the étale coverings
\{U_{m}\}_{m\geq n}
and
\mathcal{F}_{0}=\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)}|_{U_{n}}
. Then

\displaystyle R\Gamma(U_{\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(U_{\infty},\varinjlim_{n}\pi_{n}^{-1}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)})=\varinjlim_{n}R\Gamma(U_{\infty},\pi_{n}^{-1}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)})

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{n}\varinjlim_{m\geq n}R\Gamma(U_{m},\pi_{m,n}^{-1}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)})=\varinjlim_{m}\varinjlim_{n\leq m}R\Gamma(U_{n},\pi_{m,n}^{-1}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)})=\varinjlim_{m}R\Gamma(U_{m},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},m}}^{(a,b)})

where
\pi_{n}
is the natural projection
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
. In particular, this implies that
R\pi_{0,*}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}}\cong\varinjlim_{n}R\pi_{n,0,*}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)}
.

Next we verify the identification of compactly supported cohomologies. By definition and Proposition
A.22
,

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}})=R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},R\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},!}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}})

where
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
is the composition of the projection
\pi_{0}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
and the Gross–Hopkins period map
\pi_{\mathrm{GM},0}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
. The map
\pi_{\mathrm{GM},0}
is partially proper, the map
\pi_{0}
is proper, and
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
is proper. Hence

\displaystyle R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},R\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},!}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},R\pi_{\mathrm{GM},0,!}R\pi_{0,*}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},R\pi_{\mathrm{GM},0,!}\varinjlim_{n}R\pi_{n,0,*}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)})

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{n}R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},R\pi_{\mathrm{GM},0,!}R\pi_{n,0,*}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)})

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{n}R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},R\pi_{\mathrm{GM},n,!}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)})

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{n}R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(a,b)}),

where for the third isomorphism above we used (3) of Lemma
A.19
. Finally, the last isomorphism in the statement follows from the higher vanishing of the coherent comologies of the Stein spaces
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
and the Serre duality.
∎

We now temporarily suspend our discussion on Lubin–Tate spaces and turn our attention to the Shimura set that arises in the
p
-adic uniformization theorem. First we introduce the space of classical algebraic automorphic forms for
\overline{G}
in §
2.1.2
with fixed weights and of the level
K^{p}
outside
p
(cf.
[
Gro99
]
and
[
Loe11
, §3]
).

Definition 2.23
.

Suppose that
V
is an irreducible algebraic representation of
\overline{G}_{p}=G=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
over
C
. Let
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},V}
denote the set of maps

\displaystyle f:Z_{K^{p}}=\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{p}\to V

(31)

such that
f(zg_{p})=g_{p}^{-1}f(z)
for any
z\in Z_{K^{p}}
and all
g_{p}
in some open compact subgroup of
\overline{G}_{p}
. When
V=V^{(0,-k)}
, we write
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}:=\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},V^{(0,-k)}}
.

If
k=0
,
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},0}=\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}(Z_{K^{p}},C)
is the space of
C
-valued locally constant functions on the
p
-adic manifold
Z_{K^{p}}
. The space
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}
admits a smooth action of
\overline{G}_{p}
via
(g_{p}.f)(z)=g_{p}.f(zg_{p})
. Both
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}
and
R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})
are equipped with the action of the Hecke algebra
\mathbb{T}^{S}
defined in §
2.1.1
via the isomorphism
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\simeq\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and the Hecke correspondences.

Remark 2.24
.

We recall the definiton of the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
on
R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})
. For
g\in\overline{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{S})
and the Hecke operator
[K^{S}gK^{S}]
, we consider the Hecke correspondence
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}\cap gK^{p}g^{-1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\times g}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{S}_{g^{-1}K^{p}g\cap K^{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}}

Via the
p
-adic uniformization of
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
(
6
), the maps
h_{1},h_{2}
are unions of the maps of the form
\Gamma^{\prime}_{x^{\prime}}\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\rightarrow\Gamma_{x}\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
where
(K^{p})^{\prime}=K^{p}\cap gK^{p}g^{-1}
or
g^{-1}K^{p}g\cap K^{p}
,
x^{\prime}\in Z_{(K^{p})^{\prime}}
,
x
is the image of
x^{\prime}
under the projection
Z_{(K^{p})^{\prime}}\rightarrow Z_{K^{p}}
,
\Gamma_{x}=\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap x^{-1}K^{p}x\supset\Gamma_{x^{\prime}}:=\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q})\cap(x^{\prime})^{-1}(K^{p})^{\prime}x^{\prime}
. By Proposition
2.4
, both
h_{1},h_{2}
are analytic finite coverings. We have an isomorphism
(\times g)^{-1}\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{g^{-1}K^{p}g\cap K^{p}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}\cap gK^{p}}g^{-1}}
. The action of the Hecke operator
[K^{S}gK^{S}]
is the composition

\displaystyle R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\rightarrow}}R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},h_{1,*}\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}\cap gK^{p}g^{-1}}})=R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}\cap gK^{p}g^{-1}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}\cap gK^{p}g^{-1}}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{g^{-1}K^{p}g\cap K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{g^{-1}K^{p}g\cap K^{p}}})=R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},h_{2,!}\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{g^{-1}K^{p}g\cap K^{p}}})\rightarrow R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})

(32)

where the first and last maps are respectively induced by the natural maps
\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}\rightarrow h_{1,*}h_{1}^{-1}\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}=h_{1,*}\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}\cap gK^{p}g^{-1}}}
and
h_{2,!}\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{g^{-1}K^{p}g\cap K^{p}}}=h_{2,!}h_{1}^{-1}\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}\rightarrow\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
.

The following proposition is our product formula.

Proposition 2.25
.

There exist
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant isomorphisms of
D_{p}^{\times}
-representations

\displaystyle R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})

\displaystyle\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}

(33)

\displaystyle R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}.

(34)

Here the action of
G=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on
R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})
or
R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}})
is induced by the left
G
-action on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
in §
2.1.3
twisted by the inverse transpose of
G
as in Remark
2.2
.

Proof.

We will only prove the first isomorphism, and the second one follows similarly.

The Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}(G)\simeq\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\rm sm}(G,C)
is itself a smooth
G\times G^{\op}
-modules, where the action of
(g_{1},g_{2})\in G\times G^{\op}
on
f\in\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(G,C)
is given by
(g_{1},g_{2}).f(-)=f(g_{2}-g_{1})
. By
[
Cas81
, Lemma A.6(a)]
, for a discrete subgroup
\Gamma\subset G
,
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(G,C)\cong\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(\Gamma\backslash G,C)
as
\Gamma
-modules where
\Gamma
acts on the
\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]
-factor of the tensor product. Hence
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(G,C)\ox^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}V=\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(G,C)\ox_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}V
for any
\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]
-module, i.e. higher Tor groups vanish.

Let
V=V^{(0,-k)}
and let
C^{\rm sm}(\Gamma\backslash G,V)
be the space of functions
f:\Gamma\backslash G\to V
such that
f(gk)=k^{-1}f(g)
for any
k
in some open compact subgroup of
G
, with a
G
-action given
(g.f)(z)=gf(zg)
for any
z,g\in G
. There exists a natural map
\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\rm sm}(G,C)\otimes_{C}V\rightarrow\mathcal{C}^{\rm sm}(\Gamma\backslash G,V):f\otimes v\mapsto(g\in G\mapsto\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}f(\gamma g)g^{-1}\gamma^{-1}.v)
. This map is equivariant for the left
G\times\Gamma
-actions (where
G
acts on
\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\rm sm}(G,C)\otimes_{C}V
only on the first factor via right translations and
\Gamma
acts on it diagonally via the left translation on
\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\rm sm}(G,C)
) and induces a natural
G
-isomorphism

\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(G,C)\ox^{L}_{C[\Gamma]}V=\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(G,C)\ox_{C[\Gamma]}V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}C^{\rm sm}(\Gamma\backslash G,V).

We write
Z_{K^{p}}=\sqcup_{x\in Z_{K^{p}}/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\Gamma_{x}\backslash\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
so that we have
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}=\sqcup_{\Gamma_{x}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}/\Gamma_{x}
. By the above discussions,

\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}=\oplus_{x}C^{\rm sm}(\Gamma_{x}\backslash G,V)=\oplus_{x}\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\rm sm}(G,C)\otimes_{C[\Gamma_{x}]}^{L}V=\oplus_{x}\mathcal{H}(G)\otimes_{C[\Gamma_{x}]}^{L}V

as smooth left
\mathcal{H}(G)
-modules. Hence by Lemma
2.21
and (
29
) we have

\displaystyle R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})

\displaystyle\cong\oplus_{x}R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}/\Gamma_{x},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma_{x}}})

\displaystyle\cong\oplus_{x}R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\otimes_{C[\Gamma_{x}]}^{L}V

\displaystyle\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}(\oplus_{x}\mathcal{H}(G)\otimes_{C[\Gamma_{x}]}^{L}V)

\displaystyle\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}.

where for the third isomorphism, we used the
\mathcal{H}(G)
-isomorphism (
[
Ren10
, (I.2.3.3)]
)

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\mathcal{H}(G).

We remain to show that the isomorphism (
33
) we just built is
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant. For this, we show that the composition

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{C}^{L}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}\to R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}\rightarrow R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})

(35)

is
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant, where we identify
R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}
as the
G
-coinvariant (in the derived category of smooth
\mathcal{H}(G)
-modules) of
R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{C}^{L}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}
(cf.
[
Ren10
, (III.1.15.1)]
). For this purpose we will write (
35
) in a more geometric way as the descent of certain cohomology along a
G
-torsor.

First, the
p
-adic uniformization (
5
) is equivariant for the
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-action. This gives a
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant maps

\displaystyle\pi_{G}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}=\sqcup_{x}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times(\Gamma_{x}\backslash\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))\rightarrow\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}=\sqcup_{x}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}/\Gamma_{x}.

Here we identify
Z_{K^{p}}
with the perfectoid space over
C
associated to the profinite set
Z_{K^{p}}
. Consider the action map
\mathrm{act}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}:(x,g)\mapsto g.x
which is
\Gamma_{x}
-equivariant where
\Gamma_{x}
acts on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by the left multiplications on the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-factor. Taking the quotient by
\Gamma_{x}
we get a Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times\Gamma_{x}\backslash\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}/\Gamma_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{act}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{G}}

We see that the map
\pi_{G}
is a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-torsor locally trivial for the analytic topology.

Let
V^{*}
be the
C
-dual of
V
and let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}
be the subsheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}
consisting of sections which are locally
(V,V^{*})
-algebraic under the left
G\times G
-action. Note that
\mathcal{C}^{\cont}(Z_{K^{p}},C)^{V^{*}\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}=V^{*}\otimes_{C}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg},\mathrm{sm}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}
be the subsheaf consisting of sections which are smooth under the diagonal action of
\Delta G\subset G\times G
, corresponding to the one-dimensional summand
(V\otimes_{C}V^{*})^{G}\subset V\otimes_{C}V^{*}
.

By the same proof for Lemma
2.22
, we have a natural
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-isomorphism

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})-\mathrm{lalg}})

\displaystyle\cong\colim_{n,K_{p}}R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(0,-k)})\ox_{C}^{L}V\otimes_{C}^{L}V^{*}\ox_{C}^{L}(\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k})^{K_{p}}

\displaystyle=R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{C}^{L}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}\ox_{C}^{L}\End_{C}(V)

where
K_{p}
runs through open compact subgroups of
G
. Taking the smooth part for the diagonal
G
-action, we get

R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})-\mathrm{lalg},\mathrm{sm}})=R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{C}^{L}\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}

Moreover, we have

R\pi_{G,!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}=\pi_{G,!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}

where
\pi_{G,!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}(U)=(\ker d_{\mathrm{Dr}}(U))\otimes_{C}\mathcal{C}^{\cont}_{c}(G,C)^{V^{*}\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}
for
U\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}/\Gamma
such that
\pi_{G}^{-1}(U)\cong U\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. (Here we note that the constant sheaf (also the structure sheaf) on the locally profinite group
G
is flasque, as given a section
s
on a compact open, one can also extend
s
to a larger compact open via extension by zero. Hence by Lemma
A.21
there are no higher pushforwards on the
G
component, see also
[
Sch22
, Lemma 7.2]
). And there is a natural map
\pi_{G,!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}\rightarrow\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
, which can be obtained from a similar map
\mathrm{act}_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}\rightarrow\ker d_{\mathrm{Dr}}
, identiying
\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
as the
G
-coinvariant of the locally algebraic
G
-module
\pi_{G,!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}
, or the smooth
G
-module
\pi_{G,!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg},\mathrm{sm}}
.

Restricting to a component
\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}/\Gamma
, the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-smooth part of the map

R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times\Gamma\backslash\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}})=R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}/\Gamma,R\pi_{G,!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}})\rightarrow R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}/\Gamma,\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}}),

contributes to (
35
). The above map is the (derived)
\Gamma
-coinvariant of the map

R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}})\rightarrow R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}})

by the isomorphisms in Lemma
2.21
.
Hence the map

R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg},\mathrm{sm}})\rightarrow R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}})\rightarrow R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})

induced by
R\pi_{G,!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg},\mathrm{sm}}\subset\pi_{G,!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}^{(V,V^{*})\text{-}\mathrm{lalg}}\rightarrow\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
coincides with (
35
). By the definition of the Hecke actions on the (compactly supported) cohomologies as in (
2.24
), we can check that (
35
) is
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant.
∎

Remark 2.26
.

In the
\ell
-adic cohomology setting, the Mantovan product formula, e.g.
[
Man04
,
Man05
,
CS17
,
Zha23
,
DvHKZ24
]
expresses the compactly supported
\ell
-adic cohomology of a fixed Newton stratum of the tower of certain Shimura varieties in terms of the cohomology of some local Shimura varieties and the cohomology of some Igusa varieties. The results in Proposition
2.25
extend the known product formula for de Rham cohomology of the Shimura curve (cf.
[
DLB17
, §4.2]
)

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},0}^{K^{p}}\cong R\Gamma_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}).

that will be discussed in the next subsection.

Remark 2.27
.

It is natural to expect a similar product formula for
R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})
or
R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}^{\mathrm{la}})
. Let
C[[G]]=C[G]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{C}[\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})]}\mathcal{O}_{C}[[\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})]]
be the Iwasawa algebra for
G
and let
\mathcal{C}^{\cont}(X,C)
denote the space continuous functions on a
p
-adic manifold
X
. We have

R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}})=R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}})\widehat{\otimes}_{C}^{L}\mathcal{C}^{\cont}(Z_{K^{p}},C).

However, its (derived continuous)
G
-coinvariant does not give
R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})
. This can be seen already on the sheaf level:
\pi_{G,*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}\widehat{\otimes}^{L}_{C[[G]]}C\neq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}
since
\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\cont}(G,C)\widehat{\otimes}^{L}_{C[[G]]}C=C[\dim G]
using
[
RJRC23
, Proposition 3.2.11]
. But the same arguments as in the proof of Proposition
2.25
for
\Gamma
-descent shows that

\displaystyle R\Gamma(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})

\displaystyle\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}})\widehat{\otimes}^{L}_{C[[G]]}\mathcal{D}^{\cont}(Z_{K^{p}},C)

(36)

\displaystyle\cong R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}})\widehat{\otimes}^{L}_{C[[G]]}\mathcal{C}^{\cont}(Z_{K^{p}},C)[-\dim G]

where
\mathcal{D}^{\cont}(Z_{K^{p}},C)=\Hom^{\cont}_{C}(\mathcal{C}^{\cont}(Z_{K^{p}},C),C)
is the space of continuous measures on
Z_{K^{p}}
. This matches with the heuristic that
\pi_{G}^{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\times Z_{K^{p}}}[\dim G]
.

2.4
Cohomology of Intertwining operator I

Let
k\geq 0
be an integer. Following
[
Pan26
, §5]
, we focus on the spectral decomposition of
\ker I^{1}
with respect to the Hecke action, where

\displaystyle I^{1}:H^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H^{1}(I)}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)).

and the intertwining operator
I
is (Definition
2.16
):

\displaystyle I=\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}}\circ d_{K^{p}}:\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{p}})^{\ox k+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{d}^{\prime}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1).

As in
[
Pan26
, §5]
, as well as
[
QS25
, Theorem 4.7.2]
, we will express
\ker I^{1}
in terms of cohomology groups of some (de Rham) complexes associated to the differentials operators
d_{K^{p}},\overline{d}_{K^{p}}
. Put

\displaystyle\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}}:=[\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}_{K^{p}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{K^{p}}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}_{K^{p}}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}]

\displaystyle\DR:=[\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{K^{p}}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}]\simeq\ker d_{K^{p}}

\displaystyle\DR^{\prime}:=[\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d^{\prime}_{K^{p}}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)]\simeq\ker d_{K^{p}}^{\prime}

which are complexes of abelian sheaves on
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
concentrating in cohomological degrees
[0,1]
. The quasi-isomorphisms
\DR\simeq\ker d_{K^{p}},\DR^{\prime}\simeq\ker d_{K^{p}}^{\prime}
follow from the surjectivity of
d_{K^{p}}
and
d_{K_{p}}^{\prime}
by Proposition
2.15
.

Recall that we write
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}
(Definition
2.23
) for the space of algebraic automorphic forms of
p
-adic algebraic weight
V^{(0,-k)}
for
k\geq 0
on the Shimura set
Z_{K^{p}}
(defined in §
2.1.2
). The sheaves on
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
we considered above and their cohomology groups carry natural
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-actions, and morphisms between them are Hecke equivariant. It will follow from the results below that the Hecke eigensystems appearing in those cohomology groups are subsets of the eigensystems in the classical automorphic forms.

Definition 2.28
.

Let
\sigma_{k}^{K^{p}}
be the set of systems of Hecke eigenvalues (i.e. characters
\mathbb{T}^{S}\rightarrow C
) that appear in
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}
(i.e.
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda]\neq 0
). Let
\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
be the subset of
\sigma_{k}^{K^{p}}
consisting of Hecke eigensystems such that the corresponding automorphic representations of
\overline{G}(\mathbb{A})
are cuspidal, i.e., don’t factor through the reduced norm of
\overline{G}
.

If
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
, let
\pi_{\lambda}
be the irreducible automorphic representation of
\overline{G}
corresponding to
\lambda
. Write
\pi_{\lambda}=\pi_{\lambda,\infty}\ox\pi_{\lambda}^{\infty}
where
\pi_{\lambda,\infty}
is the archimedean component and
\pi^{\infty}_{\lambda}
is the non-archimedean component. Decompose further
\pi^{\infty}=\pi^{\infty,p}_{\lambda}\ox_{C}\pi_{\lambda,p}
, where
\pi_{\lambda,p}
is a smooth irreducible representation of
\overline{G}_{p}=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Theorem 2.29
.

The action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
on
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}
is semisimple and there is a decomposition

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\sigma_{k}^{K^{p}}}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda].

(37)

Moreover,
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda]=(\pi^{\infty,p}_{\lambda})^{K^{p}}\ox_{C}\pi_{\lambda,p}
and
\pi_{\lambda,p}
is infinite-dimensional for
\lambda\in\sigma_{k}^{K^{p}}
.

Proof.

The representation
V=V^{(0,-k)}=V_{E}\otimes_{E}C
of
\overline{G}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}C
, as well as the space
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k}=\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k,E}\otimes_{E}C
can be defined over a finite extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. If we base change to
\mathbb{C}
along an embedding
E\hookrightarrow\overline{E}\simeq\mathbb{C}
, then there is a
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant decomposition (cf.
[
Loe11
, Proposition 3.8.1]
)

\mathcal{A}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},k,E}\otimes_{E}\mathbb{C}=\bigoplus_{\pi}m(\pi)(\pi^{p})^{K^{p}}\otimes\pi_{p}\otimes(\pi_{\infty}\otimes(V_{E}\otimes_{E}\mathbb{C}))^{\overline{G}(\mathbb{R})}

where
\pi=\pi^{p}\otimes\pi_{p}\otimes\pi_{\infty}
runs through all automorphic representations of
\overline{G}
such that
\pi_{\infty}
is the linear dual of
V_{E}\otimes_{E}\mathbb{C}
and
m(\pi)
is the multiplicity of
\pi
. If
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\pi>1
, then
m(\pi)=1
by the classical Jacquet–Langlands correspondence and the multiplicity one theorem for
\GL_{2}
(cf.
[
Jac70
, Proposition 11.1.1]
), and in this case the corresponding system of Hecke eigenvalues
\lambda:\mathbb{T}^{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
can be defined over a finite extension of
E
. Moreover,
\pi_{\lambda,p}
is infinite-dimensional being generic, see
[
Jac70
, §11]
.
∎

Lemma 2.30
.

Let
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
be integers. Then

\displaystyle H^{i}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\omega_{K^{p}}^{(a,b),\mathrm{sm}})\cong\varinjlim_{K_{p}\subset D_{p}^{\times}}H^{i}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}},\omega_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}}}^{(a,b)}).

(38)

Proof.

The proof is similar to
[
Pan22
, Lemma 5.3.5]
.
∎

Proposition 2.31
.

The following statements hold.

(1)

There are natural isomorphisms

\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbb{H}^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}),\\
H^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathbb{H}^{2}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})\end{array}

and an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to H^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})\to H^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})\to 0.

(39)

Moreover,
\mathbb{H}^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})=\mathbb{H}^{2}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})=0
if
k>0
.

(2)

The actions of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
on
\mathbb{H}^{i}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})
are semisimple. And the actions of
D_{p}^{\times}
on
\mathbb{H}^{i}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})
factors through the reduced norm for
i=0,2
.

Proof.

(1) For
k=0
, the hypercohomology of
\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}}
is essentially the algebraic de Rham cohomology of the (finite level) Shimura curves by Lemma
2.30
and Proposition
2.9
:

\mathbb{H}^{i}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})=\colim_{K_{p}\subset D_{p}^{\times}}H^{i}_{\dR}(\mathcal{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}})

The proposition follows from the degeneration of the Hodge-de Rham spectral sequence. For
k>0
, the statements follow from (2) of Lemma
2.33
below.

(2) Since
\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}}=W^{(0,-k)}\otimes_{C}[\omega^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{p}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\rightarrow}}\omega^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{p}}]
, it shall be enough to consider the hypercohomology of the complex
[\omega^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{p}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\rightarrow}}\omega^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{p}}]
. Fix a level
K=K^{p}K_{p}
. Let
S_{K,\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
denote the base change to
\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
of the
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme in §
2.1.1
. Let
(V_{\rm dR},\nabla,\Fil^{\bullet}V_{\rm dR})
be the filtered connexion corresponding to the étale local system
\underline{V}^{(k,0)}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
on
S_{K,\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
attached to the algebraic representation of
D_{p}^{\times}
of the highest weight
(k,0)
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
by the
p
-adic Riemann-Hilbert correspondence in
[
DLLZ23
, Proposition 5.2.17]
. The
p
-adic correspondence in
loc. cit.
is compatible with the complex correspondence
[
DLLZ23
, Theorem 5.3.1]
and the pullback to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},K_{p}}

[
LZ17
, Theorem 3.9]
. Hence, the pullback and restriction of
V_{\rm dR}
to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
coincides with the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant bundle
V^{(0,-k)}\otimes_{C}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}}
(there is a dual due to our convention of the
D_{p}^{\times}
-action on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
, see Remark
2.7
). Hence, by the usual dual BGG construction (cf.
[
Fal06
]
,
[
LLZ23
, Remark 6.1.9]
and Remark
2.10
), we have

R\Gamma(S_{K,\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}},[\omega_{S_{K,\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}}^{(0,-k)}\rightarrow\omega_{S_{K,\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}}^{(-k-1,1)}])\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\mathbb{C}\simeq R\Gamma(S_{K}(\mathbb{C}),[\omega_{S_{K}(\mathbb{C})}^{(0,-k)}\rightarrow\omega_{S_{K}(\mathbb{C})}^{(-k-1,1)}])\simeq R\Gamma(S_{K}(\mathbb{C}),\underline{V}_{\mathbb{C}}^{(k,0)}).

By Matsushima’s formula (see
[
BW00
, §VII Theorem 5.2]
,
[
GH24
, §15.5]
as well as
[
Car94
, §2.2.4]
)

H^{i}(S_{K}(\mathbb{C}),\underline{V}_{\mathbb{C}}^{(k,0)})=\oplus_{\pi}(\pi^{\infty})^{K}\otimes H^{i}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2,\mathbb{R}},\mathrm{SO}_{2}(\mathbb{R}),(\pi_{\infty}\otimes V_{\mathbb{C}}^{(k,0)})^{\mathbb{R}_{>0}})

where
\pi
runs through irreducible automorphic representations of
G(\mathbb{Q})
such that the central character of
\pi_{\infty}
has the same restriction on
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
with that of
V^{(0,-k)}
and has the same (regular) infinitesimal character as
V^{(0,-k)}
. It’s then clear that the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
on
\mathbb{H}^{i}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})
is semi-simple.

By the proof of Lemma
2.33
, the action of
D_{p}^{\times}
on
\mathbb{H}^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})
factors through the reduced norm. Then the same statement for
\mathbb{H}^{2}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})
follows from the Poincaré duality.
∎

We will also need the algebraic de Rham cohomology of the Lubin–Tate space. Set

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}):=\mathrm{fib}(R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\to R\Gamma_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}})).

Lemma 2.32
.

We have isomorphisms of locally algebraic
D_{p}^{\times}
-representations

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})=H^{i}(R\Gamma_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}))\ox W^{(0,-k)}\cong H^{i}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})\ox W^{(0,-k)}

for
i=1,2
.

Proof.

The cohomology groups of
R\Gamma_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})
vanish on all degrees other than
i=1,2
by acyclicity results for coherent sheaves on quasi-Stein spaces and Serre duality. The complex
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}\to\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}}
can be seen as the translation of the standard complex
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}\to\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}
from the trivial weight
(0,0)
to the weight
(0,-k)
(see
[
Pan26
, Lemma 5.6.6]
for the Drinfeld case and Remark
2.10
). Note that the compactly supported cohomology groups of
[\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}\to\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}]
are smooth for the
D_{p}^{\times}
-action
[
CDN20
, Théorème 4.1]
, so that the translations of these cohomology groups equal to the tensor products with the algebraic representation
W^{(0,-k)}
.
∎

2.5
Cohomology of Intertwining operator II

The goal of this subsection is to prove our main results: Theorem
2.39
, Theorem
2.42
and Corollary
2.43
. We have defined complexes
\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}},\DR,\DR^{\prime}
in §
2.4
. By Proposition
2.15
, we have the following commutative diagram with exact rows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\DR}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\DR^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{p}})^{\ox k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{p}})^{\ox k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{K^{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{d}_{K^{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{K^{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{K^{p}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}}}

(40)

We write for short
\mathbb{H}^{i}(\mathcal{F})
for the hypercohomology
\mathbb{H}^{i}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{F})
for a complex of sheaves on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
and
H^{i}(\mathcal{F})
for
H^{i}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{F})
if
\mathcal{F}
is an abelian sheaf. Recall that the analytic space
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell
has cohomological (Krull) dimension
1
(see
[
dJVdP96
, Proposition 2.5.8]
and also the proof of
[
Sch15
, Corollary IV.2.2]
).

We collect some lemmas for later uses.

Lemma 2.33
.

Let
k\geq 0
.

(1)

For
(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, we have
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la},(a,b)}_{K^{p}})=0
if
a\neq b
.

(2)

If
k>0
, then
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})=0
and
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})=0
.

(3)

The natural map
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})\to H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})
is an isomorphism.

(4)

The natural map
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})\to H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(1) This follows from the same arguments as
[
Pan22
, Corollary 5.1.3 (i)]
, and we briefly sketch a proof here. By Theorem
2.18
, there is a natural isomorphism
H^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{K^{p}})\cong\widetilde{H}^{0}(K^{p},C)^{\mathrm{la}}
. The action of
D_{p}^{\times}
on
\widetilde{H}^{0}(K^{p},C)
factors through the reduced norm map. (For example, by definition
\widetilde{H}^{0}(K^{p},C)
is the completed cohomology of the tower of connected components of finite level Shimura curves, then using the
p
-adic uniformization (Theorem
2.1
) we are reduced to the description of the geometric connected components of Drinfeld towers of dimension
1
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and then we are reduced to the Lubin–Tate side by the isomorphism of the two towers, which follows from the main result of
[
Str08
]
.) From the explicit description of the
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}
-action, we see
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{matrix}\right)\in\Lie D_{p}^{\times}\ox_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}C
acts trivially on
H^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la},(a,b)}_{K^{p}})
. But we know
\theta_{\check{\mathfrak{h}}}\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{matrix}\right)
acts on
\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la},(a,b)}_{K^{p}}
via
a-b
, so that it also acts on
H^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la},(a,b)}_{K^{p}})
via
a-b
. Hence
H^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la},(a,b)}_{K^{p}})=0
if
a-b\neq 0
.

(2) Since
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}=W^{(0,-k)}\otimes\omega_{K^{p}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}=W^{(0,-k)}\otimes\omega_{K^{p}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}}
( cf. Proposition
2.9
), it’s enough to show that
H^{0}(\omega_{K^{p}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})=0
and
H^{1}(\omega_{K^{p}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}})=0
. Using Lemma
2.30
and by the usual Serre duality on the proper spaces
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}}
, it suffices to show
H^{0}(\omega_{K^{p}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})=0
, equivalently
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})=0
, if
k>0
. But there is an injection
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})
and
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})
vanishes for
k>0
by (1).

(3) If
k\neq 0
, then
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})=0
by (1) and (2). Hence it suffices to handle the case when
k=0
. In this case, as in (1), the action of
D_{p}^{\times}
on
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})
factors through the reduced norm map. Besides, the central element
\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right)\in\Lie(D_{p}^{\times})\ox_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}C
acts trivially on
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})
by Theorem
2.8
. Hence the
D_{p}^{\times}
-action on
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})
is smooth, which shows
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})\cong H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})
.

(4) By Proposition
2.15
,
\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}}:\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)
is surjective with kernel
\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}
. The desired isomorphism follows from that
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1))=0
by (1).
∎

Lemma 2.34
.

The natural map
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})\to H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}})
is injective.

Proof.

By Theorem
2.18
, we can identify
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}})
with
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)^{\mathrm{la}}
. By Lemma
2.30
and Proposition
2.9
, we can identify
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})
with (a translation of) the cohomology of automorphic line bundles on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}}
. Therefore, using the
p
-adic étale comparison theorem applied to
\varinjlim_{K_{p}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}},W)
, where
W
is a local system associated to the algebraic representation
W^{(k,0)}
of
D_{p}^{\times}
, it suffices to show some non-completed cohomology groups is a subspace (the Sen weight
0
part of the locally
W^{(0,-k)}
-algebraic vectors) of the completed cohomology group of the Shimura curves. This follows from the isomorphism

\varinjlim_{K_{p}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}},W)=\Hom_{\mathfrak{\gl}_{2}}(W^{(0,-k)},H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}))

which can be proved using similar arguments as for the modular curves
[
Eme06
, (4.3.4)]
.

We also sketch a (slightly different) argument for the
k=0
case. We have
R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,R\Gamma(\gl_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}))=R\Gamma(\gl_{2},R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}))
(cf.
[
Gee25
, Theorem 4.1]
). Hence there is a Grothendieck spectral sequence

E_{2}^{ij}=H^{i}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,H^{j}(\gl_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}))\Rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{i+j}(R\Gamma(\gl_{2},R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}))).

Since
H^{0}(\gl_{2},\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}})=\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}
, we get an injection
H^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}})\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(R\Gamma(\mathfrak{g},R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}})))=H^{0}(\mathfrak{g},H^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}))
where the last equality can be obtained using a version of
[
Eme06
, Corollary 4.3.2]
for Shimura curves as in the proof for
[
Eme06
, (4.3.4)]
.
∎

Recall that
\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
(Definition
2.28
) is the set of cuspidal Hecke eigensystems that appear in the space of algebra automorphic forms of weight
V^{(k,0)}
in
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}
defined in §
2.3
. We specialize to the Hecke eigenspaces for
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
.

Lemma 2.35
.

Let
\lambda\in\sigma^{K^{p}}_{k,c}
.

(1)

The natural map
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})[\lambda]\to H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})[\lambda]
is injective.

(2)

H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1})[\lambda]=0
.

(3)

H^{0}(\ker d^{\prime}_{K^{p}})[\lambda]=0
.

Proof.

(1) Using similar arguments as in the proof for
[
Pan22
, Corollary 5.1.3]
, there is a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow\Ext^{1}_{C[\mathfrak{h}]}(\chi,H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}))\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},\chi})\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}})^{\chi}.

where
\chi
denotes the character of
C[\mathfrak{h}]
corresponding to the weight
(0,-k)
of
\mathfrak{h}
. As in the proof for (1) of Lemma
2.33
.
H^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{la}}_{K^{p}})\cong\widetilde{H}^{0}(K^{p},C)^{\mathrm{la}}
and the action of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on
\colim_{K^{p}}\widetilde{H}^{0}(K^{p},C)^{\mathrm{la}}
factors through the reduced norm of
G
(cf.
[
Eme06
, (4.2)]
). Hence
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}})
vanishes after being localized at the ideal of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
corresponding to the cuspidal eigensystem
\lambda
. It’s then not hard to see that
\Ext^{1}_{C[\mathfrak{h}]}(\chi,H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}}))[\lambda]=0
. Then, by the above short exact sequence, we see that the map
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})[\lambda]\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K_{p}}^{\mathrm{la}})
is an injection. Hence the map
H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})[\lambda]\to H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})[\lambda]\subset H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la}})[\lambda]
is injective by Lemma
2.34
.

(2) Taking cohomology for the short exact sequence in the second row of (
40
) for
\overline{d}_{K^{p}}
, we see that the statement is a direct consequence of (1) together with (3) of Lemma
2.33
.

(3) As
\ker d^{\prime}_{K^{p}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1}
, we deduce
H^{0}(\ker d^{\prime}_{K^{p}})[\lambda]=0
from (2).
∎

By (2) of Proposition
2.15
, there is an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\ker d_{K^{p}}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1}\to 0.

(41)

Lemma 2.36
.

There exists a
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant isomorphism of
D_{p}^{\times}
-representations:

\displaystyle H^{0}(\ker d_{K^{p}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})

And
H^{1}(\ker d_{K^{p}})
fits into a
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant short exact sequence of
D_{p}^{\times}
-representations:

\displaystyle 0\to H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})\to H^{1}(\ker d_{K^{p}})\to\ker H^{1}(d_{K^{p}})\to 0.

where
H^{1}(d_{K^{p}}):H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})\to H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})
.

Proof.

By (3) of Lemma
2.33
, we know
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})=H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})
. In particular,
H^{0}(d_{K^{p}}):H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)})\to H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})
is zero by Proposition
2.31
and the commutative diagram (
40
).
Taking the long exact sequence of (
41
) after applying
R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},-)
, we see there is an isomorphism

H^{0}(\ker d_{K^{p}})=H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})

and a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})\rightarrow H^{1}(\ker d_{K^{p}})\rightarrow\ker H^{1}(d_{K^{p}})\rightarrow 0.

Finally,

H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})=H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})

by (4) of Lemma
2.33
.
∎

We consider also the short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\ker d^{\prime}_{K^{p}}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1}\to\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1)\to 0.

which is a twist of (
41
).

Lemma 2.37
.

There exist
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant isomorphisms of
D_{p}^{\times}
-representations:

\displaystyle H^{0}(\ker d_{K^{p}}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1})

\displaystyle H^{1}(\ker d_{K^{p}}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\ker H^{1}(d_{K^{p}}^{\prime})

where
H^{1}(d_{K^{p}}^{\prime}):H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1})\to H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1))
is surjective.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the previous lemma using that
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(-k-1,1)}(k+1))=0
for
k\geq 0
by (1) of Lemma
2.33
.
∎

Proposition 2.38
.

Let
\lambda\in\sigma^{K^{p}}_{k,c}
be a system of cuspidal Hecke eigenvalues. For
k\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tor}_{i}^{\mathcal{H}(G)}(\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda],H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}))=0,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tor}_{i}^{\mathcal{H}(G)}(\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda],H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}}))=0

for
i\geq 1
.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.25
, together with the vanishing results in Lemma
2.22
