and the fact that the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
in the isomorphism of Proposition
2.25
is semisimple (Theorem
2.29
), we see that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tor}_{i}^{\mathcal{H}(G)}(\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda],H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}))=H^{1-i}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})[\lambda]

and the similar result holds for
\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}}
. This implies that the
\mathrm{Tor}
groups vanish for
i\geq 2
.

When
i=1
, by Lemma
2.35
(3), and Lemma
2.36
, we see that
H^{0}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})[\lambda]=0
and
H^{0}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\ker d^{\prime}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})[\lambda]=0
. This shows that the
\mathrm{Tor}
groups also vanish for
i=1
.
∎

We can now describe the cohomology of
\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}},\DR,\DR^{\prime}
after localizing at a Hecke eigensystem
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
.

Theorem 2.39
.

Let
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
. There is a
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR)[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\prime})[\lambda]\to 0

where we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR)[\lambda]

\displaystyle\cong H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda],

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\prime})[\lambda]

\displaystyle\cong H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda].

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})[\lambda]

\displaystyle\cong W^{(0,-k)}\ox_{C}R\Gamma_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}})[1]\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda].

Proof.

By Lemma
2.20
, Lemma
2.22
and Proposition
2.25
, there exists an isomorphism

R\Gamma(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\ker d_{K^{p}})=\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})[-1].

Hence
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR)=H^{1}(\ker d_{K^{p}})=\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})
. Similarly,
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR^{\prime})=H^{1}(\ker d_{K^{p}}^{\prime})=\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}})
. And
\mathbb{H}^{2}(\DR)=H^{2}(\ker d_{K^{p}})=0
. By Lemma
2.37
,

\mathbb{H}^{0}(\DR^{\prime})=H^{0}(\ker d_{K^{p}}^{\prime})=H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1}).

By (2) of Lemma
2.35
,
\mathbb{H}^{0}(\DR^{\prime})[\lambda]=0
for
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
.

Taking the long exact sequence of the hypercohomology for the complex
0\rightarrow\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}}\rightarrow\DR\rightarrow\DR^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
, we get an exact sequence

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{0}(\DR^{\prime})\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR)\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR^{\prime})\to\mathbb{H}^{2}(\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})\to 0.

Using the decomposition (
37
), the vanishing of
\mathbb{H}^{2}(\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})[\lambda]
(obtained from the vanishing of
H^{0}(\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})[\lambda^{\prime}]
, when
\lambda^{\prime}
is assumed to be cuspidal, and duality, see Proposition
2.31
) and
\mathbb{H}^{0}(\DR^{\prime})[\lambda]
, we get the desired short exact sequence
0\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR)[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR^{\prime})[\lambda]\to 0
. The description of
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})[\lambda]
follows from Lemma
2.32
.
∎

Remark 2.40
.

From the above theorem we see that (for simplicity we set
k=0
)

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},0}^{K^{p}}[\lambda]

is always isomorphic to the
\lambda
-isotypic part of the de Rham cohomology of the tower of quaternionic Shimura curves of finite levels, and so when
\lambda\in\sigma^{K^{p}}_{k,c}
it concentrates only in degree
1
. The following formula in Proposition
2.41
, together with
[
CDN20
, Théorème 4.1, Théorème 5.8, §5.1]
(the results in
loc. cit.
are stated for a quotient of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
, but one can use for example (
49
) below to obtain the results for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
itself), is useful to do some explicit calculation of the representations. When
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda]
is a finite sum of copies of a supercuspidal representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, only
H^{1}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})
will contribute to the above term and the derived tensor product can be replaced by the usual tensor product. But when
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda]
is a sum of copies of a twist of the smooth Steinberg representation
\mathrm{St}_{2}^{\rm sm}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, both
H^{1}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})
and
H^{2}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})
will contribute to the above term nontrivially, and the derived tensor product cannot be replaced by the usual tensor product (see (
44
) below). For example, if we denote
G^{p}
to be the quotient of
G=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by
\begin{pmatrix}p&0\\
0&p\end{pmatrix}
, then
\mathrm{St}_{2}^{\rm sm}\ox^{L}_{\mathcal{H}(G^{p})}\mathrm{St}_{2}^{\rm sm}=1[0]
and
1\ox^{L}_{\mathcal{H}(G^{p})}\mathrm{St}_{2}^{\rm sm}\cong 1[1]
. Finally, when
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda]
is a sum of smooth irreducible principal series representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, the above term is zero.

The following formula describes some relation between taking
R\Hom_{\mathcal{H}(G)}(-,-)
and
-\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}-
. One can also find the result in
[
SS97
, §III.3, Duality theorem]
, providing that the central character is fixed. See also
[
FS24
, Theorem V.5.1]
.

Proposition 2.41
.

Let
M,N
be smooth representation of
G
, with
M
being finitely generated over
\mathcal{H}(G)
. Then there is a natural isomorphism

\displaystyle R\Hom_{\mathcal{H}(G)}(\mathbb{D}_{\rm BZ}(M),N)\cong M\ox^{L}_{\mathcal{H}(G)}N.

where
\mathbb{D}_{\rm BZ}(M)
is the smooth Bernstein–Zelevensky dual of
M
, defined as
\mathbb{D}_{\rm BZ}(M):=R\Hom_{\mathcal{H}(G)}(M,\mathcal{H}(G))
(cf.
[
Ber92
, IV.5]
).

Proof.

As
M
is of finite type, there exists a projective resolution
P_{\bullet}\to V
with each
P_{i}
of the form
\cInd^{G}_{K}\sigma
for some open compact subgroup
K\subset G
and some finite dimensional smooth representation
\sigma
of
K
. Here we use the fact that every subrepresentation of a finitely generated representation of
G
is still finitely generated
[
Ber92
, Proposition 32]
. Objects such as
\cInd^{G}_{K}\sigma
are flat over
\mathcal{H}(G)
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{H}(G)}(\cInd^{G}_{K}\sigma,-)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{C}(\sigma,-)^{K},

\displaystyle\cInd^{G}_{K}\sigma\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}-

\displaystyle=(\sigma\ox_{C}-)_{K}

are all exact functors for smooth representations as
\sigma
is finite dimensional and
K
is compact. As
\mathbb{D}_{\rm BZ}(\cInd^{G}_{K}\sigma)=\cInd^{G}_{K}\sigma^{*}[0]
where
\sigma^{*}=\Hom_{C}(\sigma,C)
, the isomorphism in
[
Man04
, Lemma 8.4]
:

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{H}(G)}(\cInd^{G}_{K}\sigma,-)\cong\Hom_{C}(\sigma,-)^{K}\cong(\sigma^{*}\ox_{C}-)^{K}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\sigma^{*}\ox_{C}-)_{K}\cong\cInd^{G}_{K}\sigma^{*}\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}-.

implies that

\displaystyle R\Hom_{\mathcal{H}(G)}(\mathbb{D}_{\rm BZ}(M),N)

\displaystyle\cong R\Hom_{\mathcal{H}(G)}(\mathbb{D}_{\rm BZ}(P_{\bullet}),N)\cong P_{\bullet}\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}N\cong M\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}N.

We get the desired isomorphism.
∎

The following results are analogue of
[
Pan26
, Theorem 5.5.7, Corollary 5.5.14]
in the quaternionic setting.

Theorem 2.42
.

For
k\geq 0
, there is a decomposition

\displaystyle\ker I^{1}\cong\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\sigma_{k}^{K^{p}}}\ker I^{1}\widetilde{[\lambda]},

where
(-)\widetilde{[\lambda]}
means the generalized eigenspace of the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
with eigenvalue
\lambda
. When
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
,
\ker I^{1}\widetilde{[\lambda]}=\ker I^{1}{[\lambda]}
.

Proof.

As
I^{1}=H^{1}(\overline{d}_{K_{p}}^{\prime})\circ H^{1}(d_{K^{p}})
and
H^{1}(d_{K^{p}})
is surjective (by Proposition
2.15
), we see
\ker I^{1}
fits into a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\ker H^{1}(d_{K^{p}})\to\ker I^{1}\to\ker H^{1}(\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}})\to 0.

By Lemma
2.36
and Proposition
2.25
,
\ker H^{1}(d_{K^{p}})
is a
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-quotients of
\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})
. Similarly, by Proposition
2.31
and Proposition
2.15
,
\ker H^{1}(\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}})
receives a surjection from
H^{1}(\ker\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}})=H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{K^{p}})^{\ox k+1})=\mathbb{H}^{2}(\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})
. Hence the actions of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
on both
\ker H^{1}(d_{K^{p}})
and
\ker H^{1}(\overline{d}^{\prime}_{K^{p}})
are locally finite by Theorem
2.29
and Proposition
2.31
. We see
\ker I^{1}
also admits a generalized eigenspace decomposition for the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
. Moreover, the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
on
\ker I^{1}\widetilde{[\lambda]}
when
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
is semisimple since
\mathbb{H}^{2}(\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})[\lambda]=0
by Proposition
2.31
.
∎

Corollary 2.43
.

Let
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
. There are
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant exact sequences:

\begin{array}[]{c}0\to H^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR)[\lambda]\to\ker I^{1}[\lambda]\to 0,\\
0\to H^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})[\lambda]\to\ker I^{1}[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\prime})[\lambda]\to 0.\end{array}

Proof.

From the proof of Theorem
2.42
above and Lemma
2.36
, we get the first desired short exact sequence

0\to H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR)[\lambda]\to\ker H^{1}(d_{K^{p}})[\lambda]=\ker I^{1}[\lambda]\to 0.

For the second short exact sequence, since
I=d_{K^{p}}^{\prime}\circ\overline{d}_{K^{p}}
, similarly as in the proof of Theorem
2.42
, we get a short exact sequence

0\to\ker H^{1}(\overline{d}_{K^{p}})\to\ker I^{1}\to\ker H^{1}(d_{K^{p}}^{\prime})\to 0.

By Proposition
2.15
and (3) of Lemma
2.33
, there is a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1})\rightarrow H^{1}(\ker\overline{d}_{K^{p}})=H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})\rightarrow\ker H^{1}(\overline{d}_{K^{p}})\rightarrow 0.

For
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
,
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{la},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})^{\ox k+1})[\lambda]=0
by (2) of Lemma
2.35
. Hence
\ker H^{1}(\overline{d}_{K^{p}})[\lambda]\simeq H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})[\lambda]
. By Lemma
2.37
,
\ker H^{1}(d_{K^{p}}^{\prime})=H^{1}(\ker\overline{d}_{K^{p}})=\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR^{\prime})
. Put these together, we get the desired short exact sequence
0\to H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)})[\lambda]\to\ker I^{1}[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR^{\prime})[\lambda]\to 0.

∎

In the next section we will study representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
appeared in the above cohomology groups.

3
Applications to the
p
-adic local Langlands program for
D_{p}^{\times}

In this section, we will use the results in the previous sections to study the regular de Rham representations appearing in the locally analytic vectors of the completed cohomology of quaternion Shimura curves.

3.1
Locally analytic representations of
D_{p}^{\times}

We fix an irreducible smooth representation
\pi_{p}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We assume that there exists a sufficiently small tame level
K^{p}
and a system of Hecke eigenvalues
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
such that
\pi_{\lambda,p}=\pi_{p}
(Definition
2.28
). Fix
k\geq 1
and recall we have defined the de Rham cohomology for the Lubin–Tate tower before Lemma
2.32
.

Definition 3.1
.

We define the following (locally analytic) representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}:=H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p},

\displaystyle\tau_{c}:=H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}.

Here, as in Proposition
2.25
, the actions of
G=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
on the cohomology groups of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
is induced by the left
G
-action on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
in §
2.1.3
twisted by the inverse transpose of
G
as in Remark
2.2
. We also set

\tau_{p}:=\mathrm{JL}(\pi_{p})

to be the Jacquet–Langlands transfer of
\pi_{p}
to
D_{p}^{\times}

[
Jac70
]
(if
\pi_{p}
is not a discrete series representation, then we set
\tau_{p}=\mathrm{JL}(\pi_{p})=0
).

Remark 3.2
.

(1)

From our normalization of the group actions, one can check that the central characters of
\widetilde{\tau},\tau_{c},\tau_{p}
coincide with that of
\pi_{p}
.

(2)

If
\pi_{p}
is a discrete series representation (essentially square-integrable), the representation
\tau_{p}
is an irreducible smooth representation of
D_{p}^{\times}
. We will prove in §
3.2.1
that
\widetilde{\tau}
and
\tau_{c}
are never zero. These representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
in Definition
3.1
are local, namely they depend only on
\pi_{p}
but not on
\lambda
.

Since
\pi_{\lambda,p}=\pi_{p}
, by Theorem
2.29
and its proof, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda]=\pi_{p}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}.

(42)

The following results are reformulations of Theorem
2.39
and Corollary
2.43
in terms of these representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
\tau_{p}
,
\widetilde{\tau}
, and
\tau_{c}
be the representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
in Definition
3.1
.

(1)

There exists a
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to(W^{(0,-k)}\ox_{C}\tau_{p})^{\oplus 2}\to\widetilde{\tau}\to\tau_{c}\to 0.

(43)

(2)

There are
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphisms:

\begin{array}[]{c}0\to W^{(0,-k)}\ox_{C}\tau_{p}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\to\widetilde{\tau}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\to\ker I^{1}[\lambda]\to 0,\\
0\to W^{(0,-k)}\ox_{C}\tau_{p}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\to\ker I^{1}[\lambda]\to\tau_{c}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\to 0.\end{array}

Proof.

(1) Using the
p
-adic uniformization (see also Remark
2.40
), we have

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\pi_{p}=\tau_{p}^{\oplus 2}[-1].

(44)

By Theorem
2.39
, Proposition
2.25
, Lemma
2.32
and (
42
), there is a
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-equivariant isomorphism

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow(W^{(0,-k)}\ox_{C}\tau_{p})^{\oplus 2}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\tau}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\rightarrow\tau_{c}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\rightarrow 0.

Take colimits for all
K^{p}
small enough and apply
\Hom_{G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty},-)
to the above exact sequence, we get the desired exact sequence in (1).

(2) By the Hodge–Tate decomposition and the spectral decomposition of the classical de Rham cohomology of quaternion Shimura curves (cf.
[
LLZ23
, Theorem 6.2.3]
and the proof for Proposition
2.31
), we can get isomorphisms

\displaystyle H^{1}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\omega^{(0,-k),\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})[\lambda]\cong\tau_{p}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\cong H^{0}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\omega^{(-k-1,1),\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}})[\lambda].

The two short exact sequences in (2) follow from Corollary
2.43
(using Lemma
2.30
and (
19
)).
∎

Remark 3.4
.

The map
H^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR)[\lambda]
in Corollary
2.43
is the composition

H^{0}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell,\DR)[\lambda],

where the map
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{lalg},(0,-k)}\ox_{\mathcal{O}_{K^{p}}^{\mathrm{sm}}}(\Omega_{K^{p}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})^{\ox k+1})[\lambda]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR^{\mathrm{lalg}})[\lambda]
gives the Hodge filtration, see (the proof of) Proposition
2.31
. Let
\rho:\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow\GL_{2}(\overline{E})
be the Galois representation attached to
\lambda
and let
\rho_{p}:=\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
. Let
K^{p}
vary and apply
\Hom_{G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty},-)
as in the proof of Proposition
3.3
above, we see the last map, in the notation of Proposition
3.3
(2),

W^{(0,-k)}\ox_{C}\tau_{p}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\to W^{(0,-k)}\ox_{C}\tau_{p}\otimes_{C}D_{\dR}(\rho_{p})_{C}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\subset\widetilde{\tau}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}

is induced by the Hodge filtration inside
D_{\dR}(\rho_{p})
after applying the de Rham-étale comparison for (
26
).

Remark 3.5
.

The short exact sequence (
43
) is stated for
\pi_{p}
being a local component of an automorphic representation. But one can also prove directly that the same sequence is exact for any irreducible smooth representation
\pi_{p}
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with
\dim\pi_{p}>1
(and
\widetilde{\tau},\tau_{c}
defined as in Definition
3.1
) using the results on the compactly supported de Rham cohomology of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
.

We will discuss later the relation between these sequences in the above proposition and the
p
-adic local Langlands program for
D_{p}^{\times}
.

3.2
Representation-theoretic properties of
\tau_{c}

Let
\pi_{p}
be the irreducible smooth representation of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
fixed in §
3.1
. Let
\tau_{c}
be the representation of
D_{p}^{\times}
in Definition
3.1
. In this subsection, we will study some representation-theoretic properties of
\tau_{c}
.

The organization of this part is as follows. First, we will show that
\tau_{c}
is always non-zero. Using the geometric definition of
\tau_{c}
involving Lubin–Tate spaces, we find a natural
E
-structure
\tau_{c,E}
of
\tau_{c}
, where
E
is a sufficiently large finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Then we show that
\tau_{c,E}
is admissible. We will also show that
\tau_{c,E}
has Gelfand-Kirillov dimension
1
.

3.2.1
Non-vanishing of
\tau_{c}

We start by showing that
\tau_{c}
in Definition
3.1
is non-zero for any
\lambda\in\sigma_{0,c}^{K^{p}}
(Definition
2.28
).

Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}
be the component of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
such that the quasi-isogeny in the moduli problem for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
(§
2.1.3
) is of height zero. Abstractly
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}
is an open unit disk over
C
, and we let
u\in H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}})
be a coordinate. Write
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{(0)}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}\cong\mathbb{P}^{1,\ad}_{C}
as the restriction of the Gross–Hopkins period map of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}
. From the discussion in
[
HG94
, §25]
, we know there exist two rigid analytic functions
\phi_{0},\phi_{1}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}})
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}
with no common zeros, such that
\pi_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}}(x)=[\phi_{0}(x):\phi_{1}(x)]\in\mathbb{P}^{1,\mathrm{ad}}_{C}
for
x\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}
. Moreover, all zeros of
\phi_{0},\phi_{1}
are simple.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}
be the preimage of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}
under the natural projection
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
. Let
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\circ}=\{g\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\mid\det(g)\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\}
. Then
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\circ}\times\mathcal{O}_{D_{p}}^{\times}
acts on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}
(cf. the end of
[
HG94
, §23]
).

Proposition 3.6
.

Then multiplication by
\phi_{0}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}}^{\mathrm{sm}}
induces an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\circ},C)\to H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}}^{\mathrm{sm}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\times\phi_{0}}}{{\to}}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}}^{\mathrm{sm}})\to 0.

Proof.

We first show that multiplication by
\phi_{0}
on the sheaf of Kähler differentials
\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}}^{1}
induces an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)},\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\times\phi_{0}}}{{\to}}H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)},\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}})\to\prod_{x\in(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])}C_{x}\to 0

(45)

where
[0:1]\in\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell=\mathbb{P}^{1,\mathrm{ad}}_{C}
and
C_{x}
is the skyscraper sheaf at
x\in(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}
with value
C
. Indeed, if we pick a Stein covering
\{U_{n}\}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}
by affinioids (i.e.,
U_{n}\Subset U_{n+1}
for
n\geq 0
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}=\bigcup_{n}U_{n}
), then as
(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])\simeq\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\circ}/\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is discrete,
(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])\cap U_{n}
is finite for each
n
being discrete and quasi-compact. Since
\phi_{0}
vanishes exactly on
(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])
with simple zeros and
\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}}
as a coherent
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}}
-module, we see that for each
n
, the sequence

\displaystyle 0\to H^{0}(U_{n},\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\times\phi_{0}}}{{\to}}H^{0}(U_{n},\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}})\to\prod_{x\in U_{n}:\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}(x)=[0:1]}C_{x}\to 0.

is exact. Taking limits, as
R^{1}\lim_{n}H^{0}(U_{n},\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}})=0
(
H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)},\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}})
is nuclear Fréchet), we deduce the exactness of the original sequence (
45
). Taking the strong dual, by Serre duality
[
Bey97
,
Chi06
,
vDP92
]
and
[
Sch13a
, Proposition 9.11]
(these results do not depend on whether the coefficient field is spherical complete), we deduce that there exists an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\bigoplus_{x\in(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])}C_{x}\to H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}})\to H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}})\to 0.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}^{(0)}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
be the preimage of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}
under the finite étale map
\pi_{n}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}
. Since
\pi_{n}
is étale, the pullback via
\pi_{n}^{*}
of the sequence
0\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}}\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}}\rightarrow\prod_{x\in(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])}C_{x}\rightarrow 0
is still exact. Hence the same argument as above shows that multiplication by
\phi_{0}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}^{(0)}}
induces an exact sequence

0\to\bigoplus_{x\in(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])}C_{x}\to H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}^{(0)}})\to H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}^{(0)}})\to 0

where
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n}^{(0)}=\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},0}^{(0)}\circ\pi_{n}
. Taking colimits on the level
n
, using Proposition
2.22
, we get an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\varinjlim_{n}\bigoplus_{x\in(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])}C_{x}\to H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\times\phi_{0}}}{{\to}}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}})\to 0.

Finally, we can identify
\varinjlim_{n}\bigoplus_{x\in(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])}C
as the space of compactly supported smooth functions on the locally profinite set
\varprojlim_{n}(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n}^{(0)})^{-1}([0:1])=(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}})^{-1}([0:1])\cap\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\circ}
.
∎

We remark that the exact sequence in Proposition
3.6
is not
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\circ}
-equivariant. (Indeed, if so, then
\phi_{0}
will induce a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\circ}
-equivariant map
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}\to\mathbb{A}^{1,\mathrm{an}}_{C}
, which will descend to
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
as
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
is a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\circ}
-torsor. But such a map must be a constant.) However, a variant of this exact sequence is
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant and is useful, as we will see in Proposition
3.8
below.

By
[
HG94
, §25, (25.14)]
, there exists an element
t_{00}\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}/\check{\mathfrak{z}}
, such that the action of
t_{00}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}}
is given by
t_{00}=\frac{\phi_{0}(u)\phi_{1}(u)}{\epsilon(u)}\frac{\partial}{\partial u}
. Here,
\check{\mathfrak{z}}
is the center of
\check{\mathfrak{g}}=\Lie D_{p}^{\times}\ox_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}C
, which acts trivially on
\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}}
, and
\epsilon=\phi_{0}\phi_{1}^{\prime}-\phi_{0}^{\prime}\phi_{1}
. Moreover,
t_{00}
acts naturally on the
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}
by the derivation of the
D_{p}^{\times}
-action.

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
t_{00}\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}/\check{\mathfrak{z}}
be as above.

(1)

t_{00}=c\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}\in\check{\mathfrak{g}}
for a non-zero
c\in C^{\times}
. Here
\check{\mathfrak{g}}\simeq\mathfrak{gl}_{2,C}
via (
8
).

(2)

The action map
t_{00}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}
is a composition

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{\mathrm{LT}}}}{{\rightarrow}}\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Phi}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}

of morphisms of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant abelian sheaves on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
, where
d_{\mathrm{LT}}
is the differential operator in §
2.11
and
\Phi
is a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}}
-modules whose restriction to
\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}}
is given by
fdu\mapsto\frac{\phi_{0}\phi_{1}}{\epsilon}f
.

Proof.

By definition,
t_{00}
is a derivation on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}
. By
[
Sta25
,
Tag 00RO
]
, the derivation is determined by an element
\Phi_{\mathrm{LT},n}\in\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}}(\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})
so that the action of
t_{00}
is given by

t_{00}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Phi_{\mathrm{LT},n}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}.

Since
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}\rightarrow\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
is étale (thus
\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{1}=(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n})^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}
) and domimnant, and
t_{00}
acts on
\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}
, we see that the element
\Phi_{\mathrm{LT},n}
is the image of an element

\Phi\in\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})\subset\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell},(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}}(\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}})

which gives the action of
t_{00}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}
by
loc. cit.
In other words, first order differential operators on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}
stabilizing
(\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n})^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}
are in natural bijection with differential operators on
\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}
.

To prove (1), which was already noticed after the definition of
t_{00}
in
[
HG94
, §25]
, we calculate the action of
h:=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}
. Write
\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell=\GL_{2}/\overline{B}=N\overline{B}/\overline{B}\cup sN\overline{B}/\overline{B}
. Let
z
be the coordinate of
N=\{\begin{pmatrix}1&z\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\}
and
z^{\prime}=\frac{1}{z}
be the coordinate
z^{\prime}
of
sN=\{\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&z^{\prime}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\}
. Since
\begin{pmatrix}t&0\\
0&t^{-1}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}1&z\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1&t^{-2}z\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}t&0\\
0&t^{-1}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}
, we see
h
acts on
\mathcal{O}(N)
via
f\mapsto\frac{d}{d\epsilon}f(\exp(\epsilon)^{-2}z)=-2z\frac{d}{dz}f
. Similarly,
h
acts on
\mathcal{O}(sN)
via
f(z^{\prime})\mapsto 2z^{\prime}\frac{d}{dz^{\prime}}f
. Hence the corresponding element in
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})
is given by
dz\mapsto-2z
on
N\overline{B}/\overline{B}
and
dz^{\prime}\mapsto 2z^{\prime}
on
sN\overline{B}/\overline{B}
. After possibly changing variables, we may assume that
z=\frac{\phi_{0}(u)}{\phi_{1}(u)}
where
u
is the coordinate of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},0}^{(0)}
. Then
dz=d\frac{\phi_{0}(u)}{\phi_{1}(u)}=\frac{\phi_{0}^{\prime}(u)\phi_{1}(u)-\phi_{1}^{\prime}(u)\phi_{0}(u)}{\phi_{1}(u)^{2}}du
. Hence
h
corresponds to the morphism sending
du=-\frac{\phi_{1}^{2}}{\epsilon}dz
to
-\frac{\phi_{1}^{2}}{\epsilon}\cdot(-2\frac{\phi_{0}}{\phi_{1}})=2\frac{\phi_{0}\phi_{1}}{\epsilon}
, which equals to
t_{00}
up to a scalar.

The decomposition in (2) of the
t_{00}
-action follows from the discussions above. The morphism
\Phi
is
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant since it is the pullback of a morphism in
\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell}}(\Omega^{1}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell},\mathcal{O}_{\check{\mathscr{F}}\ell})
along the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant map
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}
. The composition
t_{00}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\rm sm}\rightarrow\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\rm sm}
is a priori
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant since the action of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
commutes with the action of the Lie algebra of
D^{\times}_{p}
.
∎

The element
t_{00}
also acts on the
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant sheaf
\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}
by derivation.

Proposition 3.8
.

The action of
t_{00}
on
H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})
induces a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\ker t_{00}\to H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})\to H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})\to\coker t_{00}\to 0

with
\coker t_{00}\cong H^{2}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})
, and there exists a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),C)^{\oplus 2}\to\ker t_{00}\to H^{1}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})\to 0.

Proof.

By (2) of Lemma
3.7
, the map
H^{0}(t_{00}):H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}})\to H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}})
can be written as the composition of
H^{0}(d_{\mathrm{LT}}):H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}})\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}})
and
H^{0}(\Phi):H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega^{1,\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}})\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}})
.

We first work on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
, and by abuse of notation we let
d,\Phi
be the corresponding maps on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
. By the kernel-cokernel lemma, we see
t_{00}
induces an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\ker H^{0}(d)\to\ker H^{0}(t_{00})\to\ker H^{0}(\Phi)\to\coker H^{0}(d)\to\coker H^{0}(t_{00})\to\coker H^{0}(\Phi)\to 0.

(46)

Locally on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
, as on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}^{(0)}
,
\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{1}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}
as
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}
-modules and
\Phi
is then given by multiplying a non-zero function as
\frac{\phi_{0}\phi_{1}}{\epsilon}
. Since
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
is locally integral, we see
H^{0}(\Phi)
is injective on
H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})
. Hence
\ker H^{0}(\Phi)=0
. Then
\ker H^{0}(t_{00})=\ker H^{0}(d)
. By the vanishing of higher cohomology groups of coherent sheaves on the Stein space
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}
, we know
\ker H^{0}(t_{00})=\ker H^{0}(d)=H^{0}_{\dR}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n})
and
\coker H^{0}(d)=H^{1}_{\dR}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n})
. Moreover,
\coker H^{0}(\Phi|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}^{(0)}})=\prod_{x\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}^{(0)},\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM},n}(x)\in\{[0:1],[1:0]\}}C_{x}
by using similar methods as in the proof of Proposition
3.6
and that
\phi_{0},\phi_{1}
have no common zeros while
\epsilon
is invertible.

Now we take the strong dual (for the Fréchet topology on the cohomology groups) and take colimits on
n
, so that by Serre duality the original maps
H^{0}(t_{00}),H^{0}(d),H^{0}(\Phi)
induce

\displaystyle H^{0}(t_{00})^{\vee}:H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H^{0}(\Phi)^{\vee}}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{\mathrm{sm}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H^{0}(d)^{\vee}}}{{\to}}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}}).

By the discussions on groups in (
46
) above and the duality, we see
\coker H^{0}(\Phi)^{\vee}=0
, and
\ker H^{0}(\Phi)^{\vee}=\cInd_{\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})^{\circ}}^{\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\ker H^{0}(\Phi|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}})^{\vee}=\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}((\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}})^{-1}(\{[0:1],[1:0]\}),C)=\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),C)^{\oplus 2}
by Proposition
3.6
and the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariance of
\Phi
. Also
\ker H^{0}(d)^{\vee}=H^{1}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}):=\colim_{n}H^{1}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n})
and
\coker H^{0}(d)^{\vee}=H^{2}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})
by the duality of de Rham cohomology, cf.
[
GK00
, Theorem 4.11]
. Then we see
\coker H^{0}(t_{00})^{\vee}=\coker H^{0}(d)^{\vee}=H^{2}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})
, and there is a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),C)^{\oplus 2}\to\ker H^{0}(t_{00})^{\vee}\to H^{1}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})\to 0.

Finally, the natural action of
t_{00}
on
H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{1})
is dual to its action on
H^{0}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})
via the Serre duality which is
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant.
∎

Theorem 3.9
.

Let
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
. Then
{\tau_{c}}
is of infinite-dimensional over
C
. In particular,
{\tau_{c}}\neq 0
.

Proof.

First, we assume
k=0
. Suppose
\tau_{c}=0
, which amounts to saying that
H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}=0
. Using (
42
) and Proposition
2.38
, we find that

H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\pi_{p}=0.

Besides
R\Gamma_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})\ox^{L}_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}
is a dirct summand of

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})\ox^{L}_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda]=R\Gamma_{\dR}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}})[\lambda]

(47)

which concentrates in degree
1
and is finite-dimensional (see also Remark
2.40
). The corresponding spectral sequence
[
Sta25
,
Tag 0662
]

E_{2}^{i,j}=\mathrm{Tor}_{-i}^{\mathcal{H}(G)}(H^{j}_{\rm dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}),\mathcal{A}_{\overline{G},k}^{K^{p}}[\lambda])\Rightarrow H^{i+j}_{\dR}(\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}})[\lambda]

degenerates (cf.
[
Ber92
, IV.4]
) and we see that
H_{\dR,c}^{i}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}
has finite-dimensional cohomology groups for
i=1,2
.

By Proposition
3.8
, we see there is a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\ker t_{00}\to H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t_{00}}}{{\to}}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}}^{1,\mathrm{sm}})\to\coker t_{00}\to 0

(48)

with
\coker t_{00}\cong H^{2}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})
and another
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),C)^{\oplus 2}\to\ker t_{00}\to H^{1}_{\dR,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})\to 0.

As
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{c}(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),C)^{\oplus 2}\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}^{L}\pi_{p}\cong\pi_{p}^{\oplus 2}[0]
is infinite-dimensional (see Theorem
2.29
),
\ker t_{00}\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}
has infinite-dimensional cohomology. Applying
-\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}
to (
48
), we see this contradicts to the finiteness results of
H_{\dR,c}^{2}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty})\otimes^{L}_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}
. From the proof above, we also see that
\tau_{c}
is infinite-dimensional.

For general
k
, we can use the translation functors as in
[
Su25
, §2.2]
and
[
JLS22
, Theorem 3.2.1]
, see also Remark
2.10
, to reduce to the case
k=0
.
∎

3.2.2
Admissibility of
\tau_{c}

Similar to the treatment as in
[
QS25
, §6.2]
and
[
Pan26
, Remark 7.3.5]
, as we have realized the representation
\tau_{c}
(which can be purely defined using the de Rham complex of Lubin–Tate curves) inside the completed cohomology of quaternionic Shimura curves, we can prove the admissibility of
\tau_{c}
for some natural rational structure.

Theorem 3.10
.

Let
\tau_{c}
be defined as in Definition
3.1
. There exists a locally analytic representation
\tau_{c,E}
of
D_{p}^{\times}
over a finite extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, such that
\tau_{c}\cong\tau_{c,E}\widehat{\ox}_{E}C
, and
\tau_{c,E}
is admissible.

Proof.

Using translation functors, we may assume
k=0
for simplicity. Recall that (by Lemma
2.22
)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}.

\displaystyle\tau_{c}

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}.

The action of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times D_{p}^{\times}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
factors through
H=(\GL_{2}\times D_{p}^{\times})/\{(a,a^{-1}),a\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\}
in our normalization. Consider the subgroup
H^{\circ}=\{(g,h)\mid\det(g)\mathrm{Nm}(h)\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\}\subset H
where
\mathrm{Nm}
denotes the reduced norm of
D_{p}
. Then
H^{\circ}
stabilize
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)}
, and there exist
H
-equivariant isomorphisms

\displaystyle H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}})

\displaystyle=\cInd_{H^{\circ}}^{H}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}})

(49)

\displaystyle=\chi_{\rm sm}\otimes_{C}\cInd_{H^{\circ}}^{H}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}^{(0)},\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}})

for any smooth character
\chi_{\mathrm{sm}}:H/H^{\circ}=\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow C^{\times}
. Hence we may assume that
\begin{pmatrix}p&0\\
0&p\end{pmatrix}\in G=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
acts trivially on
\pi_{p}
, after possibly a smooth twist.

The induced Weil descent datum on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}/\begin{pmatrix}p&0\\
0&p\end{pmatrix}^{\mathbb{Z}}
is effective
[
RZ96
, Theorem 2.16, Theorem 3.49]
. Besides, there exists a sufficiently large finite extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, such that
\pi_{p}=\pi_{p,E}\widehat{\ox}_{E}C
for some smooth representation
\pi_{p,E}
of
G
over
E
(since
\pi_{p}
is a local component of a regular cuspidal algebraic automorphic representation). Therefore, using the above
E
-structures of the Lubin–Tate spaces and
\pi_{p}
, and the definition of
\widetilde{\tau}
, one can directly find a locally analytic representation
\widetilde{\tau}_{E}
of
D_{p}^{\times}
over
E
, such that
\widetilde{\tau}\cong\widetilde{\tau}_{E}\widehat{\ox}_{E}C
.

Recall that by Proposition
3.3
and Proposition
2.19
, we have an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to\tau_{p}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\to\widetilde{\tau}\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}\to\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)[\lambda]^{\mathrm{la},\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}=0}\to 0.

The algebraic automorphic representation
\pi_{\lambda}
can be defined over the sufficiently large
E
and we denote the
E
-structure as
\pi_{\lambda,E}
. Let
\rho_{\lambda}:\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow\GL_{2}(E)
be the corresponding Galois representation. The above exact sequence shows that the
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)[\lambda]^{\mathrm{la},\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}=0}
, up to the multiplicity space
(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}
, is a quotient of
\widetilde{\tau}
by one copy of
\tau_{p}
. The relative position of the
\tau_{p}
inside
\tau_{p}^{\oplus 2}\subset\widetilde{\tau}
is
E
-rational as it comes from the position of the Hodge filtration of
\rho_{\lambda}
(see Remark
3.4
and the proof of Corollary
2.43
). We define
\tau_{E}
to be the cokernel of the composition
\tau_{p,E}\rightarrow\tau_{p,E}^{\oplus 2}\subset\widetilde{\tau}_{E}
induced by the Hodge filtration. This gives an
E
-structure of
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)[\lambda]^{\mathrm{la},\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}=0}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)[\lambda]^{\mathrm{la},\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}=0}\cong C\widehat{\ox}_{E}\tau_{E}\ox_{E}(\pi_{\lambda,E}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}.

(50)

On the other hand, by (
26
) we know that there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)[\lambda]^{\mathrm{la}}\cong\rho_{\lambda}\ox\check{\Pi}(\rho_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{la}}

where recall that
\check{\Pi}(\rho_{\lambda})
is defined as
\Hom_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}(\rho_{\lambda},\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E))
. Therefore,

\displaystyle(\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)[\lambda]\widehat{\ox}_{E}C)^{\mathrm{la},\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}=0}\cong(C\ox_{E}\rho_{\lambda})^{\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}=0}\widehat{\ox}_{E}\check{\Pi}(\rho_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{la}}.

(51)

Here, we fixed an embedding of
E
into
C
. By construction, the isomorphisms in (
50
) and (
51
) are compatible with the Galois action. Taking
\Gal(\overline{L}/E)
-invariants, we deduce that there is a
D_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant,
E
-linear and topological isomorphism

\displaystyle(C\ox_{E}\rho_{\lambda})^{\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}=0,{\Gal(\overline{L}/E)}}\widehat{\ox}_{E}\check{\Pi}(\rho_{\lambda})^{\mathrm{la}}\cong C^{\Gal(\overline{L}/E)}\widehat{\ox}_{E}\tau_{E}\ox_{E}(\pi_{\lambda,E}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}.

From this, we deduce that
\tau_{E}
is admissible, as
\check{\Pi}(\rho_{\lambda})
is admissible by the admissibility of the completed cohomology (cf.
[
Eme06
, Theorem 2.2.11]
). Finally, similarly as above for
\widetilde{\tau}
, one can find a natural
E
-structure
\tau_{c,E}
of
\tau_{c}
. As
\tau_{c,E}
is a quotient of
\tau_{E}
, we deduce that
\tau_{c,E}
is also admissible.
∎

3.2.3
Gelfand-Kirillov dimension of
\tau_{c}

Let
\tau_{c,E}
be the (natural)
E
-structure of
\tau_{c}
in Theorem
3.10
, where
E
is a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For definition and basic properties of the Gelfand-Kirillov dimension of a locally analytic representation, our main references are
[
ST03
, §8]
,
[
DPS23
, §3]
. The following result is known by the work of Hu–Wang
[
HW24a
, Theorem 1.1]
under some genericity condition (on the mod-
p
representations of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
).

Theorem 3.11
.

The Gelfand-Kirillov dimension of
\tau_{c,E}
is
1
.

Proof.

There are two ingredients in the proof, which give an upper bound and a lower bound. We assume again
k=0
, and the general cases can be deduced using the translation operators. Let
\tau_{E}
be the
E
-structure of
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)[\lambda]^{\mathrm{la},\Theta_{\mathrm{Sen}}=0}
constructed in the proof of Theorem
3.10
.

First, as
\tau_{E}
has trivial infinitesimal character, and is isomorphic to the locally analytic vector of an admissible unitary Banach representation (as it comes from the completed cohomology), by
[
DPS23
, Corollary 6.8]
, we know that
\tau_{E}
has Gelfand-Kirillov dimension at most
1
. As the difference between
\tau_{c,E}
and
\tau_{E}
, up to a multiplicity, is a finite dimensional representation of
D_{p}^{\times}
(whose base change to
C
is
\tau_{p}
), we deduce that
\tau_{c,E}
has Gelfand-Kirillov dimension at most
1
.

Besides, we know that
\tau_{c}
is of infinite-dimensional over
C
by Theorem
3.9
, hence
\tau_{E}
is of infinite dimensional over
E
. As
\tau_{E}
is isomorphic to the locally analytic vectors of a Banach representation, from
[
DPS23
, Lemma 3.9]
, we deduce that the Gelfand-Kirillov dimension of
\tau_{E}
is at least
1
. Again, this shows that the Gelfand-Kirillov dimension of
\tau_{c,E}
is at least
1
.
∎

Remark 3.12
.

Let
L
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For an irreducible smooth representation of
\GL_{2}(L)
, one can define locally
L
-analytic representations
\widetilde{\tau},\tau_{c}
of
D_{L}^{\times}
, where
D_{L}
denotes the unique non-split quaternion algebra over
L
, in the same way as in Definition
3.1
using the Lubin–Tate tower for
L
. One can prove similar results on the Gelfand-Kirillov dimensions for these representations. Note that
[
DPS23
, Lemma 3.9]
is only stated for
L=\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, but one can use the following argument for general
L
. Assume that
\tau_{c,E}
is an admissible locally analytic representation of
D_{L}^{\times}
of the Gelfand-Kirillov dimension
0
with the infinitesimal character the same as the smooth representations. Then
\tau_{c,E}
must be a smooth admissible representation, using
[
AW13
, Corollary 9.1]
and
[
Koh07
, Theorem 1.4.2]
. But our
\tau_{c,E}
is not smooth, otherwise its wall-crossing is zero (using similar methods as in
[
QS25
, Theorem 4.7.6]
, its wall-crossing still contains
\tau_{c,E}
, which is non-zero). See the proof of Lemma
3.22
below for more relavent details on the wall-crossing.

3.3
Structure of the Hecke eigenspaces of the completed cohomology

In this section, we summarize our study on the Hecke eigenspace
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
(
25
) of the locally analytic completed cohomology of the Shimura curves attached to a
2
-dimensional de Rham Galois representation
\rho
. Combining computations before we explain what the locally analytic
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
looks like. Then we can deduce that
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
essentially only depends on the local Galois representation, and even only depends on the Weil–Deligne representation associated to the local Galois representation in the crystabeline case.

Recall that in §
2.1
we have defined
S_{K^{p}K_{p}}
as the quaternion Shimura curve over the reflex field
\mathbb{Q}
of level
K=K^{p}K_{p}
. Let
E\subset C
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. After fixing the tame level
K^{p}
and taking limit over
K_{p}\subset D_{p}^{\times}
, we defined in §
2.2.6
the completed cohomology group
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)
for the tower
(S_{K^{p}K_{p}})_{K_{p}}
with coefficients in
E
.

Similar to
[
Pan26
, Theorem 7.1.2]
, we have the following classicality result.

Theorem 3.13
.

Let
\rho
be a
2
-dimensional
E
-linear continuous absolutely irreducible representation of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})
. Suppose:

(i)

\rho
appears in
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)^{\mathrm{la}}
, namely
\Hom_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}(\rho,\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)^{\mathrm{la}})\neq 0
.

(ii)

\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
is de Rham of Hodge Tate weight
0,k+1
with
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Then
\rho
is classical, i.e., the Hecke eigenvalue associated to
\rho
lies in
\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
(Definition
2.28
).

Proof.

This is a combination of Theorem
2.19
and Theorem
2.42
.
∎

Next, we show that any
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
must appear in
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)
. Fix
k\geq 0
and
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
. We use the notation in §
3.1
and let
\pi_{p}
be the component at
p
of the automorphic representation
\pi_{\lambda}
of
\overline{G}
. When
\pi_{p}
is a discrete series representation, we already know that
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)^{\mathrm{lalg}}[\lambda]\supset\tau_{p}\otimes_{C}W^{(0,-k)}\neq 0
by the global Jacquet–Langlands correspondence (the locally algebraic vectors of
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)
recovers the classical cohomology of
S_{K^{p}K_{p}}
, cf.
[
Eme06
, (4.3.4)]
). So the crucial part is to show that
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)[\lambda]\neq 0
even when
\pi_{p}
is an irreducible principal series representation.

Theorem 3.14
.

Suppose
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
. Then
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)^{\mathrm{la}}[\lambda]\neq 0
.

Proof.

Suppose
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)^{\mathrm{la}}[\lambda]=0
. Then, since
\ker I^{1}
is a subspace of
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)^{\mathrm{la}}
, we deduce that
\ker I^{1}[\lambda]=0
. But by Proposition
3.3
(deduced from Theorem
2.39
and Corollary
2.43
), this will imply
\tau_{c}=0
, which contradicts Theorem
3.9
.
∎

Remark 3.15
.

This result was known by
\mathrm{Pa\check{s}k\mathaccent 28694{u}nas}

[
Paš22
, Theorem 1.4]
, under some genericity conditions (on the mod
p
Galois representations).

By Theorem
2.19
, Theorem
3.14
, together with Theorem
2.39
, Corollary
2.43
(Proposition
3.3
) and (
42
), we deduce the following description of the locally analytic representation associated to
\lambda
.

Corollary 3.16
.

Let
\rho
be the Galois representation associated to
\lambda
such that
\mathbb{T}^{S}
acts on

\check{\Pi}(\rho)=\Hom_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}(\rho,\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E))

via
\lambda
. We have an isomorphism

\displaystyle\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}C\cong(\widetilde{\tau}/i_{\rho}(\tau_{p}^{\mathrm{lalg}}))\otimes_{C}(\pi_{\lambda}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}.

(52)

where
\widetilde{\tau}/i_{\rho}(\tau_{p}^{\mathrm{lalg}})
is moreover an extension of
\tau_{c}
by
\tau_{p}^{\mathrm{lalg}}
. Here,

•

\tau_{p}^{\mathrm{lalg}}=\tau_{p}\otimes_{C}W^{(0,-k)}
with
\tau_{p}
the Jacquet–Langlands transfer of
\pi_{p}
and
W^{(0,-k)}
the algebraic representation over
C
of
D_{p}^{\times}
of highest weight
(0,-k)
.

•

\widetilde{\tau}:=\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(0,-k)})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}
with
G=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\mathcal{H}(G)
the smooth Hecke algebra of
G
.

•

\tau_{c}:=\varinjlim_{n}H^{1}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n},\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},n}}^{(-k-1,1)})\ox_{\mathcal{H}(G)}\pi_{p}
.

•

The map
i_{\rho}:\tau_{p}^{\mathrm{lalg}}\to\widetilde{\tau}
comes from the position of the Hodge filtration of
\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
using the
p
-adic de Rham comparison theorem (see Remark
3.4
).

We can deduce the following locality result.

Theorem 3.17
.

Let
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
. Then up to a multiplicity, the locally analytic
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
only depends on the local Galois representation
\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
. Moreover, when
\pi_{p}
is an irreducible smooth principal series representation,
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
only depends on the Weil–Deligne representation associated to
\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
(and the Hodge–Tate weights).

Proof.

By Theorem
3.14
, we know that every
\lambda\in\sigma_{k,c}^{K^{p}}
must appear in
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},C)
. Hence the condition on
\rho
required in Theorem
2.19
is satisfied. Then using (
52
), we see that
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
only depends on the local Galois representation
\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
. Moreover, when
\pi_{p}
is an irreducible principal series representation, we know that
\tau_{p}=0
. In particular the information of Hodge filtration of
\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
disappears in
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
. Hence
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
only depends on the Weil–Deligne representation associated to
\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
.
∎

Remark 3.18
.

In particular, when
\pi_{p}
is an irreducible principal series representation, one can not capture the information of the Hodge filtration in
D_{\dR}(\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})})
from
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
. For example, assume that
\rho_{p}:\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\rightarrow\GL_{2}(E)
is a non-split extension of a crystalline character
\chi
of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
by the trivial character
1
where
\chi
has Hodge-Tate weight
k+1\geq 1
and
\chi
is not the inverse of the cyclotomic character. Then both
\rho_{p}
and its semisimplification
\rho_{p}^{\rm ss}=\chi\oplus 1
are crystalline representations with the same associated Weil–Deligne representation but different positions of Hodge filtrations, see
[
Col08
, §4.5]
. In this case, the smooth
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-representation
\pi_{p}
is an irreducible principal series representation, and
\tau_{p}=0
. If
\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
has the same semisimplification as
\rho_{p}
, then the
D_{p}^{\times}
-representation
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}C
cannot distinct
\rho_{p}
and
\rho_{p}^{\rm ss}
. This is a new phenomenon in the
p
-adic local Langlands program in the
D_{p}^{\times}
-case, compared to the
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-case. In the mod
p
setting, similar results were known by
[
HW24a
, Theorem 1.3(ii)(a)]
. On the other hand, when
\pi_{p}
is a twist of the smooth Steinberg representation, we expect that
\check{\Pi}(\rho)^{\mathrm{la}}
still determines the Hodge filtration of
\rho|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
as in
[
HW24a
, Remark 8.31]

3.4
Applications to the Scholze functor

In this section, we want to discuss some applications to the
p
-adic Jacquet–Langlands correspondence. Our main goal in this section (Theorem
3.20
) is to compute the value of the Scholze functor
[
Sch18
,
DC24
]
on the locally analytic representations of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
attached to
2
-dimensional de Rham Galois representations, in terms of the locally analytic representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
considered in the previous sections. Let
\check{G}=D_{p}^{\times}
and
G=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

We briefly recall the definition of the Scholze functor, following
[
Sch18
,
DC24
]
. Let
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
be the Gross–Hopkins period map on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
, which is a pro-étale
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-torsor. Given an admissible unitary Banach representation
\Pi
of
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, we can descend the constant sheaf on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{LT},\infty}
with fiber
\Pi
to
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
, so that we get a pro-étale sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{\Pi}
on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
. The pro-étale cohomology group
R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro}\text{\'{e}t}}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\mathcal{F}_{\Pi})
is again a unitary Banach representation of
D_{p}^{\times}
, which is also equipped with a commuting action of
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. As is explained in
[
DC24
, Corollary 5.3.5]
, if
\Pi^{0}
is a
G
-stable lattice in
\Pi
, then

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro}\text{\'{e}t}}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\mathcal{F}_{\Pi})\cong R\varprojlim_{n}R\Gamma_{\text{\'{e}t}}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\mathcal{F}_{\Pi^{0}/p^{n}})[1/p].

(53)

Moreover, if we denote
\Pi^{\mathrm{la}}\subset\Pi
as the subspace of locally analytic vectors of
\Pi
, we can also descend
\Pi^{\mathrm{la}}
along
\pi_{\mathrm{LT},\mathrm{GM}}
so that to get a pro-étale sheaf
\mathcal{F}_{\Pi^{\mathrm{la}}}
on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
. By
[
DC24
, Theorem 5.3.6]
, we know that

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro}\text{\'{e}t}}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\mathcal{F}_{\Pi})^{R\check{G}\text{-}\mathrm{la}}\cong R\Gamma_{\mathrm{pro}\text{\'{e}t}}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\mathcal{F}_{\Pi^{G\text{-}\mathrm{la}}}).

(54)

For
i\geq 0
, set

S^{i}(\Pi^{\mathrm{la}}):=H^{i}_{\mathrm{pro}\text{\'{e}t}}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\mathcal{F}_{\Pi^{\mathrm{la}}}).

Let
Z_{K^{p}}
be the Shimura set associated to
\overline{G}
in §
2.1.2
. Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},E}:=\mathcal{C}^{\cont}(Z_{K^{p}},E)

to be the completed cohomology of the Shimura set. This is an admissible unitary Banach representation of
\overline{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=G
, which is also equipped with an action of the Hecke algebra
\mathbb{T}^{S}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{la}}_{\overline{G},E}
be the subspace of locally
G
-analytic vectors. Similar to
[
Sch18
, Theorem 6.2]
, we can use the
p
-adic uniformization of the quaternionic Shimura curve
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
to deduce the compatibility of Scholze functor with the completed cohomology groups
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},E}
and
\widetilde{H}^{i}(K^{p},E)
in §
2.2.6
.

Theorem 3.19
.

For
i\geq 0
, there is a
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\mathbb{T}^{S}\times\check{G}
-equivariant isomorphism:

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\mathrm{pro}\text{\'{e}t}}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\mathcal{F}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{la}}_{\overline{G},E}})\cong\widetilde{H}^{i}(K^{p},E)^{\check{G}\text{-}\mathrm{la}}.

Proof.

Write
\pi_{\HT}=\pi_{K^{p},\HT}:\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\to\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
for the Hodge–Tate period map on the quaternionic Shimura curve
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
of infinite level (recalled in §
2.1.1
). Define
\mathcal{F}_{K^{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
as the pro-étale sheaf on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
by descending the unitary Banach
G
-representation
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
(with
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-coefficients). We show that there is a
\check{G}
-equivariant and Weil group equivariant isomorphism of sheaves on the pro-étale site of
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}

R\pi_{\HT,\proet*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\cong\mathcal{F}_{K^{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}}.

Then one can use
[
Cam24
, Corollary 6.1.7]
to deduce the claim.

We first show that
R^{n}\pi_{\HT,\proet*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=0
when
n>0
. Since
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}\cong\sqcup_{i}\Gamma_{x_{i}}\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
as in (
6
) and the Hodge–Tate period map on
\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}}
is compatible with the one on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
, it suffices to show the vanishing of higher direct image of
\pi_{\HT,\Gamma}:\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}:=\Gamma_{x_{i}}\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C}
for each
\Gamma=\Gamma_{x_{i}}
. For
n>0
,
R^{n}\pi_{\HT,\Gamma,\proet*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is the sheafification of

X\to H^{n}_{\proet}(X\times_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C}}\mathcal{S}_{K^{p}},\mathbb{Q}_{p})

where
X
are affinoid perfectoid spaces proétale over
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C}
. Since
\pi_{\mathrm{Dr},\HT}
exhibits
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}
as a proétale
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-torsor on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{C}
, for each proétale covering of
X
, we can find a proétale refinement by strictly totally disconnected spaces
S_{i}
such that there is a
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant isomorphism
S_{i}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Dr},\infty}\cong S_{i}\times\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
T=\Gamma\backslash\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be the profinite set. Because
\Gamma
acts trivially on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}
, we get an isomorphism
S_{i}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{C}_{p}}}\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}\cong S_{i}\times T
. Now the claim follows from

H^{n}_{\proet}(S_{i}\times T,\mathbb{Q}_{p})=0

as
S_{i}\times T
is still a strictly totally disconnected space (for example by
[
Sch22
, Lemma 7.19]
). Note that here we need
T
to be profinite (rather than just locally profinite) to have
H^{n}_{\proet}(S_{i}\times T,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cong H^{n}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(S_{i}\times T,\mathbb{Z}_{p})[1/p]=0
.

To identify
\pi_{\HT,\proet*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with
\mathcal{F}_{K^{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Using the same argument as in the end of the proof of
[
Sch18
, Proposition 6.5]
, replacing
\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}
by
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we have a map
\pi_{\HT,\proet*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to\mathcal{F}_{K^{p},\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. To check this is an isomorphism, it suffices to check it on strictly totally disconnected
S_{i}
as in the last paragraph. In this case, it is easy to see both sides can be identified with
\mathrm{Cont}(|S_{i}\times T|,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.
∎

The
G
-smooth vectors
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{sm}}_{\overline{G},E}
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},E}
is the space of
E
-valued classical automorphic forms on
\overline{G}
of tame level
K^{p}
and trivial weight at
p
(i.e. take
k=0
in Definition
2.23
and with
E
-coefficients). Take a cuspidal Hecke eigensystem
\lambda:\mathbb{T}^{S}\rightarrow E
appearing in
\mathcal{A}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{sm}}_{\overline{G},E}
. Let
\rho_{\lambda}:\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow\GL_{2}(E)
be the Galois representation associated with
\lambda
. Then as (
42
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{sm}}_{\overline{G},E}[\lambda]\cong\pi_{p,E}\otimes(\pi_{\lambda,E}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}

where
\pi_{\lambda,E}
is the corresponding automorphic representation of
\overline{G}(\mathbb{A})
and
\pi_{p,E}
is its
p
-component, a smooth irreducible admissible representation of
G
over
E
. We furthur assume that mod
p
reduction
\overline{\rho}_{\lambda}|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
is absolutely irreducible for simplicity (this is the same assumption as
[
DLB17
, §5.1, Hypothése]
, which can certainly be weakened, cf.
[
Paš22
, Corollary 5.7]
). Then using the
p
-adic local-global compatibility results
[
Eme11
,
DLB17
]
, we know that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{la}}_{\overline{G},E}[\lambda]\cong\pi_{E}\otimes(\pi_{\lambda,E}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}},

(55)

where
\pi_{E}
is a locally analytic representation of
G
over
E
, corresponding to
\rho_{\lambda}|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
via the
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence for
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. See for example
[
Pan26
, §7.3]
for a concrete description of
\pi_{E}
. We note that
\pi_{p,E}
is the closed subspace of smooth vectors of
\pi_{E}
.

Starting from
\pi_{p,E}
, we have defined various locally analytic
\check{G}=D_{p}^{\times}
-representations over
E
. Let
\tau_{p,E}
be the Jacquet–Langlands transfer of
\pi_{p,E}
to
D_{p}^{\times}
with
\tau_{p,E}=0
if
\pi_{p,E}
is an irreducible principal series representation as in §
3.1
. Set
\pi_{p}:=\pi_{p,E}\widehat{\ox}_{E}C
. Let
\widetilde{\tau},\tau_{c}
be the representations of
D_{p}^{\times}
over
C
defined as in Definition
3.1
using
\pi_{p}
, with the
E
-structures
\widetilde{\tau}_{E},\tau_{c,E}
constructed in §
3.2.2
. Also, recall that we have constructed a locally analytic representation
\tau_{E}
depending only on
\rho_{\lambda}|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
in the proof of Theorem
3.10
, so that

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)[\lambda]=\rho_{\lambda}\otimes_{E}\Hom_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})}(\rho_{\lambda},\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E))=\rho_{\lambda}\otimes_{E}\tau_{E}\otimes(\pi_{\lambda,E}^{p,\infty})^{K^{p}}.

(56)

Theorem 3.20
.

Let
\lambda:\mathbb{T}^{S}\rightarrow E
be a cuspidal Hecke eigensystem appearing in
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{sm}}_{\overline{G},E}
, with the associated Galois representation
\rho_{\lambda}
. Assume that the reduction and the restriction
\overline{\rho}_{\lambda}|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
of
\rho_{\lambda}
is absolutely irreducible. Let
\pi_{E}
(resp.
\tau_{E}
) be the locally analytic
G
(resp.
\check{G}
)-representation associated to
\lambda
. Then we have a
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant isomorphism of locally analytic
D_{p}^{\times}
-representations

\displaystyle S^{1}(\pi_{E})\cong\rho_{\lambda}|_{\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\ox_{E}\tau_{E}.

Proof.

This is a combination of Theorem
3.19
and Proposition
3.21
below, with the descriptions of the Hecke eigenspaces (
55
) and (
56
).
∎

Using a similar treatment as in
[
Sch18
, Proposition 7.7]
and Lemma
3.22
below, we have the following result.

Proposition 3.21
.

Let
\lambda
be as in Theorem
3.20
. Then the natural map

\displaystyle S^{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{la}}_{\overline{G},E}[\lambda])\xrightarrow{\sim}S^{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{la}}_{\overline{G},E})[\lambda].

(57)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By our assumption, the mod
p
reduction of
\rho_{\lambda}
is absolutely irreducible. This gives a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
of the abstract Hecke algebra
\mathbb{T}^{S}
over
\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\mathbb{T}(K_{p}K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
be the
\mathfrak{m}
-adic completion of the image of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
in
\End_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(H^{1}(S_{K^{p}K_{p}}(\mathbb{C}),\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
, and put
\mathbb{T}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}:=\varprojlim_{K_{p}}\mathbb{T}(K_{p}K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
. It acts faithfully on
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)_{\mathfrak{m}}
, where
(-)_{\mathfrak{m}}
denotes the localization at
\mathfrak{m}
of
\mathbb{T}(K^{p})
-modules. Using
[
Sch18
, Corollary 7.3]
and taking
p
-adic completion, we know that the
\mathbb{T}^{S}
-action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p}}_{\overline{G},E,\mathfrak{m}}
extends to a continuous action of
\mathbb{T}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

For simplicity, we write
{\mathcal{A}}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{la}}_{\overline{G},E,\mathfrak{m}}
. Using a finite set of generators of the ideal of
\mathbb{T}(K^{p})_{\mathfrak{m}}
corresponding to
\lambda
, there is an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to{\mathcal{A}}[\lambda]\to{\mathcal{A}}\to\oplus_{i\in I}{\mathcal{A}}

(58)

with
I
finite. The above exact sequence induces the following two exact sequences

\displaystyle 0\to{\mathcal{A}}[\lambda]\to{\mathcal{A}}\to M\to 0,

\displaystyle 0\to M\to\oplus_{i\in I}{\mathcal{A}}\to Q\to 0.

where
M
is the cokernel of the first map and
Q
is the cokernel of the second map. As the formation
\pi\mapsto\mathcal{F}_{\pi}
is exact, we get the following exact sequences

\displaystyle S^{0}(M)\to S^{1}({\mathcal{A}}[\lambda])\to S^{1}({\mathcal{A}})\to S^{1}(M),

\displaystyle S^{0}(Q)\to S^{1}(M)\to S^{1}(\oplus_{i\in I}{\mathcal{A}}).

where
S^{i}(\pi)=H^{i}_{\mathrm{pro}\text{\'{e}t}}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\mathcal{F}_{\pi})
. First,
S^{0}(M)=0
. Indeed, as
M\subset\oplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{A}
, the Hecke action on
S^{0}(M)\subset\oplus_{i\in I}S^{0}(\mathcal{A})
is Eisenstein by Theorem
3.19
. But
S^{0}(M)
is also a subspace of
S^{1}({\mathcal{A}}[\lambda])
, which is non-Eisenstein. Hence
S^{0}(M)=0
. Then we know there is an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\to S^{1}(\mathcal{A}[\lambda])\to S^{1}(\mathcal{A})[\lambda]\to\ker\beta

where
\beta:S^{1}(M)\to S^{1}(\oplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{A})
. In particular, we know that there is a surjection
S^{0}(Q)\twoheadrightarrow\ker\beta
. Now we claim that the action of
\check{G}
on
S^{0}(Q)
factors through the reduced norm map. Indeed, using the definition of
Q
and that Hecke operators act unitarily, we see that
Q
has an admissible unitary Banach completion
\widehat{Q}
, whose subspace of locally analytic vectors is
Q
. Let
\widehat{Q}^{0}
be a lattice in
\widehat{Q}
. Then by (
53
) we know

\displaystyle S^{0}(\widehat{Q})=\varprojlim_{n}S^{0}(\widehat{Q}^{0}/p^{n})[\frac{1}{p}].

Using
[
Sch18
, Proposition 4.7]
applied to
\widehat{Q}^{0}/p^{n}
, we deduce that the
\check{G}
-action on
S^{0}(\widehat{Q})
factors through the reduced norm map, and so does its subspace
S^{0}(Q)
. Hence we know that
\check{G}
also acts on
\ker\beta
via the reduced norm map by the surjection
S^{0}(Q)\twoheadrightarrow\ker\beta
. On the other hand, we know that
S^{1}(\mathcal{A})[\lambda]
has trivial infinitesimal character. Indeed, from Theorem
3.19
we know that
S^{1}(\mathcal{A})=\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\check{G}\text{-}\mathrm{la}}
. Hence using
[
DPS25
, Theorem 1.4]
we know that
\widetilde{H}^{1}(K^{p},E)_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\check{G}\text{-}\mathrm{la}}[\lambda]
has trivial infinitesimal character. So that the image of
S^{1}(\mathcal{A})[\lambda]\to\ker\beta
also has trivial infinitesimal character. This forces that this image must be a smooth
\check{G}
-representation. By Lemma
3.22
below, we know that
S^{1}(\mathcal{A})[\lambda]
can not have such quotients. Hence the image of
S^{1}(\mathcal{A})[\lambda]
in
\ker\beta
is zero. In other words, we get the desired isomorphism
S^{1}(\mathcal{A}[\lambda])\xrightarrow{\sim}S^{1}(\mathcal{A})[\lambda].

∎

Lemma 3.22
.

Let
\lambda:\mathbb{T}^{S}\rightarrow E
be a cuspidal Hecke eigensystem appearing in
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}^{K^{p},G\text{-}\mathrm{sm}}_{\overline{G},E}
as above, with the associated locally analytic representation
\tau_{E}
. Then
\tau_{E}
has no non-zero smooth quotients.

Proof.

The proof is similar to
[
Su25
, Proposition 2.5.2, Theorem 2.5.7]
or
[
QS25
, Theorem 4.7.6]
. Recall that
\DR,\DR^{\prime}
are certain complexes on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
defined in §
2.4
, such that
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\DR)[\lambda]\cong\widetilde{\tau}^{\oplus n}
and
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\DR^{\prime})[\lambda]\cong\tau_{c}^{\oplus n}
, where
n
is the multiplicity coming from the tame part of the automorphic representation. By Proposition
2.15
, we know
\mathbb{H}^{2}(\check{\mathscr{F}\ell},\DR^{\prime})
is the second cohomology of an abelian sheaf on
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
, so that it is zero since
\check{\mathscr{F}\ell}
is of dimension
1
. This shows that the natural map induced by wall-crossing
[
JLS22
,
Din24
]

\Theta_{s}\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR)\to\mathbb{H}^{1}(\DR)
is surjective (since the cokernel is a subspace of
\mathbb{H}^{2}(\DR^{\prime})
by the exact triangle
\DR^{\prime}\rightarrow\Theta_{s}\mathrm{DR}\rightarrow\DR\rightarrow
as in the proof for
[
QS25
, Theorem 4.7.6]
). Since the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}
on these cohomology groups are semisimple by Theorem
2.29
and Theorem
2.39
, we get that the natural map
\Theta_{s}(\widetilde{\tau})\to\widetilde{\tau}
is surjective. By Corollary
2.43
, we know
\tau_{E}\widehat{\ox}_{E}C
is a quotient of
\widetilde{\tau}
by a smooth representation. The wall-crossing of a smooth representation is zero. From this one can deduce that the natural map induced by adjunction property of the wall-crossing functor gives a surjection map
\Theta_{s}(\tau_{E})\twoheadrightarrow\tau_{E}
. Let
\tau^{\prime}
be a smooth quotient of
\tau_{E}
. Then by functoriality, we get a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Theta_{s}(\tau_{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Theta_{s}(\tau^{\prime})=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau_{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tau^{\prime}}}

which forces that
\tau^{\prime}=0
, as desired.
∎

Appendix A
Proper pushforward of solid abelian sheaves on adic spaces

In this section we develop the formalism for proper pushforward of solid abelian sheaves, following
[
AL24
]
.

A.1
Setup and Definitions

For an adic space, we assume it is a separated analytic adic space over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For a partially proper morphism
f:X\to Y
between adic spaces, we mean it is partially proper after passing to the associated diamonds
[
Sch22
, Definition. 18.4]
. The morphism
f
is proper if it is quasi-compact, separated and universally closed. This definition coincides with
[
Sch22
, Definition. 18.1]
since passing to diamonds does not change the underlying topological spaces
[
Sch22
, Lemma. 15.6]
.

Definition A.1
.

A germ is a pairing
(S,X)
where
X
is an adic space and
S\subseteq X
is a closed subset. A morphism of germs
(S,X)\to(T,Y)
is a morphism
f:X\to Y
of adic spaces which induces a map
S\to T
.

Remark A.2
.

The category of analytic adic spaces admits fiber product (the morphisms are adic, c.f
[
SW20
, Proposition 5.1.5]
). In particular, if
(S,X)\longrightarrow(W,Z)\longleftarrow(T,Y)
are morphisms of germs then
K:=X\times_{Z}Y
exists as an adic space and
V:=p^{-1}(S)\cap q^{-1}(T)
is closed, where
p
and
q
are projections from
K
to
X
and
Y
such that
(V,K)
is the fiber product.

Recall the following definition in Huber’s theory of pesudo-adic spaces.

Definition A.3
.

Let
f:(S,X)\to(T,Y)
be a morphism of germs, we call
f
is

1.

quasi-compact (quasi-separated or coherent or taut) if the map of topological spaces
S\to T
has such a property.

2.

separated if
\Delta(S)\subseteq V
is closed where
(V,Z)=(S,X)\times_{(T,Y)}(S,X)
.

3.

Universally closed if for any morphism of germs
(V,Z)\to(T,Y)
with fiber product
(W,P)
, the map
W\to V
is a closed map.

4.

proper if it is quasi-compact, separated and universally closed.

Remark A.4
.

Being quasi-compact or being universally closed is stable under composition and base change: By a standard argument, if
h:(S,X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}(T,Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}(V,Z)
is the composition of morphisms of germs such that
h
is quasi-compact (or universally closed) and
g
is separated, then
f
is quasi-compact (or universally closed). For base change of quasi-compactness, it’s because morphisms between analytic adic spaces are adic morphisms.

A.2
Sheaves in condensed abelian groups

We use the condensed mathematics developed in
[
Sch19
]
. Fix
\kappa
an uncountable strong limit cardinality. Let
\ExtDisc_{\kappa}
be the category of
\kappa
-small extremally disconnected sets endowed with coverings given by finite families of jointly surjective maps. A
\kappa
-small condensed set is a sheaf of sets on the site
\ExtDisc_{\kappa}
. In the following context, we will fix this
\kappa
and omit
\kappa
in the notation. Let
\cond({\mathbb{Z}})
be the category of condensed abelian groups and
\solid({\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
be the full subcategory of solid abelian groups. They are Grothendieck abelian categories.

Let
X
be an adic space. We denote by
\mathit{PSh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
the category of presheaves in condensed abelian groups on
X
and
\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
be the full subcategory of sheaves. The category of condensed abelian groups satisfies the condition
[
KS06
, (17.4.1)]
, so the inclusion
\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))\subseteq\mathit{PSh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
admits an exact left adjoint, called sheafification. Similarly, we denote by
\mathit{PSh}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
(resp.
\mathit{Sh}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
) the category of presheaves (sheaves) of solid abelian groups on
X
. They also admit an exact sheafification functor.

Remark A.5
.

1.

Since each covering of an extremally disconnected set splits, a map
f:M\to N
between two condensed sets is an epimorphism (resp. a monomorphism) if and only the map
f(S):M(S)\to N(S)
is surjective (resp. injective) for each
S\in\ExtDisc
.

2.

Assume
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
. For each
S\in\ExtDisc
, the functor
{\mathscr{F}}(-)(S)
is a sheaf of abelian groups on
X
.

Lemma A.6
.

The functor (See Remark
A.5
(2))

\Phi:\mathit{(P)Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))\to\Func(\ExtDisc^{op},\mathit{(P)Sh}(X,\mathrm{Ab}));\ {\mathscr{F}}\mapsto(S\to{\mathscr{F}}(-)(S))

induces an equivalence between
\mathit{(P)Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
and
(P){\mathscr{C}}\subseteq\Func(\ExtDisc^{op},\mathit{(P)Sh}(X,\mathrm{Ab}))
containing functors
F:\ExtDisc^{op}\to\mathit{(P)Sh}(X,\mathrm{Ab})
such that
F(\emptyset)
is the zero sheaf and for
S_{1},S_{2}\in\ExtDisc
,
