F(S_{1}\coprod S_{2})=F(S_{1})\times F(S_{2})
.

Proof.

We have equivalences of categories

\mathit{PSh}(X,\Func(\ExtDisc^{op},\mathrm{Ab}))\simeq\Func(\open^{op}(X)\times\ExtDisc^{op},\mathrm{Ab})\simeq\Func(\ExtDisc^{op},\mathit{PSh}(X,\mathrm{Ab}))

thus
\Phi
is fully faithful. We prove the claim for
\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
and the claim for
\mathit{PSh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
is inherited in the proof. A functor
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{PSh}(X,\Func(\ExtDisc^{op},\mathrm{Ab}))
lies in
\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
if and only if for any
U\subseteq X
open,
S_{1},S_{2}\in\ExtDisc
,
{\mathscr{F}}(U)(\emptyset)=0
,
{\mathscr{F}}(U)(S_{1}\coprod S_{2})={\mathscr{F}}(U)(S_{1})\times{\mathscr{F}}(U)(S_{2})
and for any covering
\{U_{i}\to U\}_{i\in I}
, the sequence

0\to{\mathscr{F}}(U)\to\prod_{i\in I}{\mathscr{F}}(U_{i})\to\prod_{i,j\in I}{\mathscr{F}}(U_{i}\cap U_{j})

(59)

is an exact sequence in
\cond({\mathbb{Z}})
. The sequence (
59
) is exact if and only if it is exact after evaluating on each
S\in\ExtDisc
. Then it is easy to check that the functor

\Psi:{\mathscr{C}}\to\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}));\ F\mapsto(U\to F(-)(U))

is an inverse of
\Phi
.
∎

Lemma A.7
.

Suppose
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{PSh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
, the sheafification
{\mathscr{F}}^{a}
of
{\mathscr{F}}
corresponds to the functor
F:S\to(U\to{\mathscr{F}}(U)(S))^{a}
under the equivalence
\Phi
.

Proof.

It is clear that
F
lies in
{\mathscr{C}}
. It suffices to see
F
satisfies the universal property of sheafification: Evaluating on each
S\in\ExtDisc
, it is clear that
F(S)
satisfies the universal property. For functoriality in
S
, it follows from that sheafification of abelian sheaves on
X
is a functor and the unit of an adjunction pair is a natural transformation, which is functorial.
∎

Remark A.8
.

Assume
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{PSh}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
. Since the category of solid abelian groups is stable under limit and colimit in the category of condensed abelian groups, by the construction of sheafification (cf.
[
KS06
, Definition 17.4.5]
), the sheafification of
{\mathscr{F}}
in
\mathit{PSh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
and
\mathit{PSh}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
coincides. This implies that the inclusion functor
\mathit{Sh}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})\to\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
is exact.

Corollary A.9
.

Let
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{PSh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
and
{\mathscr{F}}^{a}
be its sheafification. For each
x\in X
,
{\mathscr{F}}_{x}\cong{\mathscr{F}}_{x}^{a}
.

Proof.

For each
S\in\ExtDisc
,

{\mathscr{F}}_{x}(S)=(\colim_{U\ni x}{\mathscr{F}}(U))(S)=\colim_{U\ni x}({\mathscr{F}}(U)(S))=\Phi({\mathscr{F}})(S)_{x}.

(60)

But by Lemma
A.7
, we have

\Phi({\mathscr{F}}^{a})(S)_{x}=(\Phi({\mathscr{F}})(S))^{a}_{x}=\Phi({\mathscr{F}})(S)_{x}.

The result follows.
∎

Corollary A.10
.

A sequence
0\to{\mathscr{F}}\to{\mathscr{G}}\to{\mathscr{H}}\to 0
is exact in
\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
if and only if for any
S\in\ExtDisc
the sequence
0\to{\mathscr{F}}(-)(S)\to{\mathscr{G}}(-)(S)\to{\mathscr{H}}(-)(S)\to 0
is an exact sequence of abelian sheaves on
X
if and only if
0\to{\mathscr{F}}_{x}\to{\mathscr{G}}_{x}\to{\mathscr{H}}_{x}\to 0
is an exact sequence of condensed abelian groups for all
x\in X
.

Proof.

For any morphisms
{\mathscr{F}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}{\mathscr{G}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}{\mathscr{H}}
in
\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
such that
g\circ f=0
, there is a natural map
\eta:\mathrm{Im}(f)\to\ker(g)
. Recall that
\mathrm{Im}(f)
is the sheafification of the presheaf image of
f
. By the description of sheafification in Lemma
A.7
,
\eta
is an isomorphism if and only if
\eta(-)(S):\mathrm{Im}(f(-)(S))\to\ker(g(-)(S))
is an isomorphism for each
S
. The first equivalence now follows formally. For the second equivalence, we can evaluate on
S\in\ExtDisc
and use stalkwise criterion for abelian sheaves and (
60
).
∎

Corollary A.11
.

A map
f:{\mathscr{F}}\to{\mathscr{G}}
in
\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
is an isomorphism if and only if for each point
x\in X
, the induced map on stalks
f_{x}:{\mathscr{F}}_{x}\to{\mathscr{G}}_{x}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

One direction is clear. Assume
f_{x}
are isomorphisms. By Lemma
A.6
, we need to show
f(-)(S):{\mathscr{F}}(-)(S)\to{\mathscr{G}}(-)(S)
is an isomorphism of abelian sheaves. By stalkwise criterion of abelian sheaves, we need to show the map

\colim_{U\ni x}{\mathscr{F}}(U)(S)\to\colim_{U\ni x}{\mathscr{G}}(U)(S)

is an isomorphism for all
x\in X
. Considering (
60
), this is our condition.
∎

If
f:X\to Y
is a morphism of adic spaces, by
[
KS06
, §17]
, there is an adjoint pair
(f^{-1},f_{*})
between condensed abelian sheaves on
X
and
Y
. We have
((f_{*}{\mathscr{F}})(V))(S)=f_{*}({\mathscr{F}}(-)(S))(V)
. It is formal to check
f^{-1}
preserves stalks, thus it is an exact functor by corollary
A.11
. When
f
is an open immersion,
f^{-1}
has a left adjoint
f_{!}
given by the extension by zero functor. That is,
f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}
is the sheafification of the presheaf

U\to\begin{cases}{\mathscr{F}}(f^{-1}(U)),&\text{if }U\subseteq X\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Note that if
j:U\to X
is an open immersion and
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(U,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
, the explicit formula above tells that
j_{!}{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
.

Assume
j:U\to X
is an open immersion,
i:Z\to X
is the closed complement and
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
. By Corollary
A.11
, we have an exact sequence in
\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))

0\to j_{!}j^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\to{\mathscr{F}}\to i_{*}i^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\to 0.

We call
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
flasque if for any open subsets
U\subseteq V\subseteq X
, the map

{\mathscr{F}}(V)\to{\mathscr{F}}(U)
is an epimorphism of condensed abelian groups.

Proposition A.12
.

Assume
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
is flasque, then
{\mathscr{F}}
is
\Gamma(X,-)
-acyclic.

Proof.

By abstract nonsense, it suffices to show injective sheaves are flasque and for an exact sequence
0\to{\mathscr{F}}\to{\mathscr{G}}\to{\mathscr{H}}\to 0
in
\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
with
{\mathscr{F}},{\mathscr{G}}
flasque, the sequence

0\to{\mathscr{F}}(X)\to{\mathscr{G}}(X)\to{\mathscr{H}}(X)\to 0

is exact in
\cond({\mathbb{Z}})
. For
S\in\ExtDisc
, let
{\mathbb{Z}}[S]_{X}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
be the sheaf associated to the presheaf
U\to{\mathbb{Z}}[S]\in\cond({\mathbb{Z}})
. Then for any
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
,
\Hom({\mathbb{Z}}[S]_{X},{\mathscr{F}})=\Hom({\mathbb{Z}}[S],{\mathscr{F}}(X))={\mathscr{F}}(X)(S)
. So, for an injective object
{\mathscr{I}}
in
\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
, the first claim follows from applying
\Hom(-,{\mathscr{I}})
to
0\to j_{!}j^{-1}{\mathbb{Z}}[S]_{X}\to{\mathbb{Z}}[S]_{X}
for all
S\in\ExtDisc
. For the second claim, we need to show the sequence
0\to{\mathscr{F}}(X)(S)\to{\mathscr{G}}(X)(S)\to{\mathscr{H}}(X)(S)\to 0
is exact for all
S\in\ExtDisc
. By Corollary
A.10
,
0\to{\mathscr{F}}(-)(S)\to{\mathscr{G}}(-)(S)\to{\mathscr{H}}(-)(S)\to 0
is an exact sequence of abelian sheaves on
X
and
{\mathscr{F}}(-)(S)
is flasque. We can apply the classical argument to conclude.
∎

Proposition A.13
.

Let
X
be an adic space,
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a filtered diagram of open subsets of
X
such that each
U_{i}
is spectral and
Z=\cap_{i\in I}U_{i}
. Assume
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
, then for each
q\geq 0
the map

\colim_{i\in I}H^{q}(U_{i},{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}})\to H^{q}(Z,{\mathscr{F}}|_{Z})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof is similar to
[
FK17
, Proposition 3.1.19]
and the argument is standard, so we just sketch it:
For the first, one establishes statements
[
FK17
, Proposition 3.1.8; 3.1.10]
in the condensed setting. It is enough to check both isomorphisms after evaluating on
S\in\ExtDisc
, then one can use the same argument there.

For the second, we need the Godement resolution in condensed setting: For
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,\cond({\mathbb{Z}}))
, let
\mathcal{G}({\mathscr{F}})
be the sheaf
\mathcal{G}({\mathscr{F}})(U)=\prod_{x\in U}{\mathscr{F}}_{x}
and one defines a complex

0\to{\mathscr{F}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d^{0}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{G}^{0}({\mathscr{F}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d^{1}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{G}^{1}({\mathscr{F}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d^{1}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{G}^{2}({\mathscr{F}})\to\cdots

inductively by setting
\mathcal{G}^{0}({\mathscr{F}}):=\mathcal{G}({\mathscr{F}})
and
\mathcal{G}^{n}({\mathscr{F}}):=\mathcal{G}(\coker(d^{n-1}))
. It is easy to check the functor
\mathcal{G}(-)
is exact,
\mathcal{G}({\mathscr{F}})
is flasque and
d^{0}
is injective. So the above complex is a functorial flasque resolution of
{\mathscr{F}}
.

Let
p_{i}:Z\to U_{i}
be the inclusion. If
{\mathscr{F}}_{i}
is a flasque sheaf on
U_{i}
then
p_{i}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}_{i}
is also flasque and
\colim_{i}p^{-1}_{i}{\mathscr{F}}_{i}
is quasi-flasque (See
[
FK17
, Exercise 0.3.1]
). Now we can use the same argument as in
[
FK17
, Proposition 3.1.19]
.
∎

Remark A.14
.

Note that by Remark
A.8
, Corollary
A.9
,
A.10
,
A.11
, Proposition
A.12
,
A.13
all hold when we work in
\mathit{(P)Sh}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
.

A.3
Sections with compact support

We only define sections with compact support for partially proper morphisms: Let
f:X\to Y
be a partially proper morphism of adic spaces. Assume
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
, we define a subsheaf
f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}
of
f_{*}{\mathscr{F}}
. For each
V\subseteq Y
being open,
S\in\ExtDisc
and
s\in f_{*}{\mathscr{F}}(V)(S)
being a section. Let

\supp(s):=\{x\in f^{-1}(V):0\neq\mathrm{Im}(s)\in{\mathscr{F}}_{x}(S)\}.

It is a closed subset of
f^{-1}(V)
and
(\supp(s),f^{-1}V)
is a germ.

Definition A.15
.

Let
f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}\subseteq f_{*}{\mathscr{F}}
be the subsheaf (use lemma
A.6
) such that for each
S\in\ExtDisc

f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}(V)(S):=\{s\in f_{*}{\mathscr{F}}(V)(S):(\supp(s),f^{-1}(V))\to(V,V)\text{ is proper}\}

We also write
H^{0}(Y,f_{!}{\mathscr{F}})
as
H^{0}_{c}(X/Y,{\mathscr{F}})

Lemma A.16
.

Assume
f:X\to Y
is a partially proper morphism of adic spaces,
V\subseteq Y
is a quasi-compact open subset and
s\in f_{*}{\mathscr{F}}(V)
, then
f_{s}:(\supp(s),f^{-1}(V))\to(V,V)
is proper if and only if
\supp(s)
is quasi-compact.

Proof.

One direction is clear. Assume
\supp(s)
is quasi-compact, then
f_{s}
is quasi-compact (Remark
A.4
). Since
f^{-1}(V)\to V
is separated and
\supp(s)\subseteq f^{-1}(V)
is closed,
f_{s}
is separated.

To check universally closed: Since partially proper morphism between adic spaces and quasi-compact morphism between germs are stable under base change, it is enough to show
f_{s}^{\prime}:\supp(s)\to|V|
is a closed map. We can prove it after passing
f
to its associated diamond
f^{\diamond}:X^{\diamond}\to Y^{\diamond}
and identifying
\supp(s)
with a quasi-compact closed subset of
|X^{\diamond}|
which we still denote by
\supp(s)
. By
[
Sch22
, Proposition 18.10]
and its proof,
\supp(s)
is contained in a quasicompact closed generalizing subsets
\overline{U}\subseteq|X^{\diamond}|
which corresponds to a spatial sub
v
-sheaf
X^{\prime}\subseteq X^{\diamond}
such that
X^{\prime}\to Y^{\diamond}
is proper. In particular
\overline{U}\to|Y^{\diamond}|=|Y|
is a closed map, so is
\supp(s)\to|Y|
as
\supp(s)
is closed in
\overline{U}
.
∎

Lemma A.17
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a partially proper morphism between adic spaces where
Y
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated. Then there exists a covering of
X
by cofiltered family of quasi-compact open subsets
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
each of which is closed under specialization and has quasi-compact closure.

Proof.

By the proof of
[
Hub13
, Lemma 5.3.3]
, the result holds when
|X|
is taut and valuative (valuative means it is locally spectral and the set of generalisation of any point is totally ordered). Since passing to associated diamond preserves underlying topological spaces, the desired properties follow from
[
Sch22
, Proposition 18.10, 11.19]
.
∎

Lemma A.18
.

Assume
{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
and
f:X\to Y
is a partially proper morphism of adic spaces. Then
f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}\in\mathit{Sh}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
.

Proof.

Since the category of solid abelian groups is closed under limit in the category of condensed abelian groups, it suffices to check
f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}(V)
is solid for each
V\subseteq Y
quasi-compact quasi-separated open subset and we will assume
Y=V
is quasi-compact quasi-separated. By Lemma
A.17
, there is a covering
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
X
satisfying the condition in the cited lemma. Let
j_{i}:U_{i}\to X
be the open immersion and
f_{i}:U_{i}\to Y
be the restriction of
f
to
U_{i}
. There is a natural injective map
f_{i*}j_{i!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}}\to f_{*}{\mathscr{F}}
. Since
\overline{U}_{i}
is quasi-compact, for each
S
extremally totally disconnected and
s\in(f_{i*}j_{i!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}})(Y)(S)
, we have
\supp(s)
is quasi-compact. By Lemma
A.16
,
s\in f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}(Y)(S)
thus we get a natural injective map
(f_{i*}j_{i!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}})\to f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}
. For each
U_{i}\subseteq U_{k}
there is a natural injective map
f_{i*}j_{i!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}}\to f_{k*}j_{k!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{k}}
. Thus there is an injective map
\colim_{i}f_{i*}j_{i!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}}\to f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}
. It suffices to show this is an isomorphism because then (use
Y
is quasi-compact in the second equality)

f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}(Y)=(\colim_{i}f_{i*}j_{i!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}})(Y)=\colim_{i}(j_{i!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}})(U_{i}).

The right hand side is solid since
(j_{i!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}})(U_{i})
is solid (see the paragraph after Corollary
A.11
) and solid abelian groups are stable under colimit. To show the isomorphism, by Corollary
A.10
we can evaluate on
S
. Since
\cup_{i}U_{i}=X
, for each
s\in{\mathscr{F}}(X)(S)
such that
\supp(s)
is quasi-compact, there is
U_{i}
such that
\supp(s)\subseteq U_{i}
which implies
s
lies in the image of
j_{i!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}}(X)(S)
.
∎

Lemma A.19
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a partially proper morphism of adic spaces.

(1)

f_{!}
is a left exact functor.

(2)

If
f
is an open immersion, then
f_{!}
is extension by zero.

(3)

f_{!}
commutes with filtered colimits.

(4)

Assume
f:X\to Y;g:Y\to Z
are partially proper morphisms, then
(g\circ f)_{!}{\mathscr{F}}=g_{!}f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}
for any condensed abelian sheaf
{\mathscr{F}}
.

Proof.

(1): It can be deduced easily from that
f_{*}
is left exact,
f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}\subseteq f_{*}{\mathscr{F}}
is a subsheaf and that if
\alpha:{\mathscr{F}}\to{\mathscr{G}}
is an injective morphism of condensed abelian sheaves on
X
and
s\in{\mathscr{F}}(X)(S)
then
\supp(s)\subseteq\supp(\alpha(s))
(actually
\supp(s)=\supp(\alpha(s))
).

(2): There is an obvious functor from extension by zero sheaf to
f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}
, then we can apply the same proof as in
[
AL24
, Lemma 4.2]
using corollary
A.11
.

(3): By the universal property of colimit, there is a natural functor
\colim_{i}(f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}_{i})\to f_{!}(\colim_{i}{\mathscr{F}}_{i})
. Note that by the proof of Lemma
A.18
, we have

f_{!}(\colim_{i}{\mathscr{F}}_{i})=\colim_{U}f_{U!}((\colim_{i}{\mathscr{F}}_{i})|_{U})=\colim_{U}f_{U!}(\colim_{i}{\mathscr{F}}_{i}|_{U})

(61)

where
U\subseteq X
are quasi-compact open subspaces. For
V\subseteq Y
quasi-compact open (for the equality below, use that any cover of
V
has a finite refinement)

(\colim_{i}f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}_{i})(V)\longrightarrow(\colim_{i}f_{*}{\mathscr{F}}_{i})(V)=\colim_{i}{\mathscr{F}}_{i}(f^{-1}(V))

contains objects which comes from some
{\mathscr{F}}_{n}(f^{-1}(V))
whose support is proper over
V
(after evaluating on
S
). Evaluate (
61
) on
V
, it is easy to see
f_{!}(\colim_{i}{\mathscr{F}}_{i})(V)=(\colim_{i}f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}_{i})(V)
.

(4): Let
h=g\circ f
. Since
h_{*}{\mathscr{F}}=g_{*}f_{*}{\mathscr{F}}
and
h_{!}{\mathscr{F}}
,
g_{!}f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}
are subsheaves, we can assume
Z
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated. Properness of a morphism can be checked locally on the target, the result follows easily from Lemma
A.16
and Remark
A.4
. ∎

For an adic space
X
, let
D^{+}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
be the left bounded derived category of solid abelian sheaves on
X
. Note that it only depends on the underlying topological space
|X|
.

Definition A.20
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a partially proper morphism of adic spaces. Define

Rf_{!}:D^{+}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})\to D^{+}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})

to be the right derived functor of
f_{!}
(Lemma
A.18
).

Lemma A.21
.

Assume that
f:X\to Y
is a partially proper morphism of adic spaces. Then flasque sheaves are
f_{!}
-acyclic.

Proof.

The question is local so we assume
Y
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated. There exists
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
as in Lemma
A.17
and we put
j_{i}:U_{i}\to X
. Then by the same argument as
[
AL24
, Lemma 4.8(2), 4.9]
using Proposition
A.13
, we get an isomorphism
R^{q}f_{!}{\mathscr{F}}\simeq\colim_{i}R^{q}f_{i*}(j_{i!}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U_{i}}))
where
f_{i}:\overline{U_{i}}\to Y
are morphisms of topological spaces which is the restriction of
f
to
\overline{U_{i}}
. In fact,
[
AL24
, Lemma 4.9]
shows that if
{\mathscr{I}}
is a flasque sheaf on
U_{i}
then
R^{q}f_{i*}(j_{i!}{\mathscr{I}})=0
for
q>0
. The result follows.
∎

Proposition A.22
.

Let
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\longrightarrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z
be partially proper morphisms of adic spaces. Then there is a canonical isomorphism
R(g\circ f)_{!}\cong Rg_{!}\circ Rf_{!}
of functors
D^{+}(X,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})\to D^{+}(Z,{\mathbb{Z}}_{\square})
.

Proof.

Since
(g\circ f)_{!}=g_{!}\circ f_{!}
, it suffices to show that for an injective sheaf
{\mathscr{I}}
on
X
,
R^{q}g_{!}(f_{!}{\mathscr{I}})=0
when
q>0
. The question is local on
Z
so we assume
Z
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated. We can find open cover
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
Y
as in Lemma
A.17
and let
j_{i}:V_{i}\to\overline{V_{i}}
and
g_{i}:\overline{V_{i}}\to Z
. By the proof of lemma
A.21
, we have

R^{q}g_{!}(f_{!}{\mathscr{I}})=\colim_{i\in I}R^{q}g_{i*}(j_{i!}(f_{!}{\mathscr{I}})|_{V_{i}})

and it suffices to show
(f_{!}{\mathscr{I}})|_{V_{i}}
is flasque. So we can assume
V_{i}=Y
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated.

Consider
W\subseteq Y
be an open subset, it suffices to show
f_{!}{\mathscr{I}}(Y)(S)\to f_{!}{\mathscr{I}}(W)(S)
is surjective for any
S\in\ExtDisc
. Pick a section
s\in f_{!}{\mathscr{I}}(W)(S)
with proper support over
W
. Let
T
be the closure of
\supp(s)
in
X
, and let
s^{\prime}\in H^{0}(f^{-1}(W)\cup(X\setminus T),{\mathscr{I}})(S)
be the unique section with
s^{\prime}|_{f^{-1}(W)}=s
and
s^{\prime}|_{X\setminus T}=0
. Since
{\mathscr{I}}
is flasque, we can pick
s^{\prime\prime}\in\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{I}})(S)
with
s^{\prime\prime}|_{f^{-1}(W)\cup X\setminus T}=s^{\prime}
. Then
\supp(s^{\prime\prime})\subseteq T
. Since
|X|
is taut (by
[
Sch22
, Proposition 18.10]
),
T
is quasi-compact so
\supp(s^{\prime\prime})
is proper over
Y
.
∎

A.4
Equivariant sheaves

Let
\Gamma
be a discrete group. Suppose that
\Gamma
acts properly discontinuous on an adic space
X
and let
f:X\rightarrow Y=X/\Gamma
be the quotient map. Consider the Čech nerve

(X^{\bullet/Y})_{\bullet}\rightarrow Y

where for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
X^{n/Y}:=\underbrace{X\times_{Y}\cdots\times_{Y}X}_{n}
and let
f_{n}:X^{n/Y}\rightarrow Y
.

Let
{\mathscr{F}}\in D^{+}(Y,\mathbb{Z}_{\square})
and set
{\mathscr{G}}=f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}
which is a
\Gamma
-equivariant sheaf (or a complex of
\Gamma
-equivariant sheaves) on
X
. For
\gamma\in\Gamma
that induces
\gamma:X\rightarrow X
, the isomorphism
{\mathscr{G}}\simeq\gamma_{*}{\mathscr{G}}
induces an action of
\gamma
on
f_{!}{\mathscr{G}}
via
f_{!}{\mathscr{G}}\simeq f_{!}\gamma_{*}{\mathscr{G}}=f_{!}{\mathscr{G}}
by Lemma
A.19
. Locally on
Y
where the
\Gamma
-covering
f
is trivialized, it follows from the definition that
f_{!}{\mathscr{G}}=Rf_{!}{\mathscr{G}}
can be identified with the
\Gamma
-module
\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathscr{F}}
.

Lemma A.23
.

Suppose that
{\mathscr{F}}\in D^{+}(Y,\mathbb{Z}_{\square})
. We have natural maps
f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow{\mathscr{F}}
and for all
n
. The natural map

\colim_{[n]\in\Delta^{\rm op}}f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow{\mathscr{F}}

is an isomorphism, where
\Delta
is the simplex category. Moreover
f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma^{n}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}f_{!}{\mathscr{G}}
and
{\mathscr{F}}=\mathbb{Z}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}f_{!}{\mathscr{G}}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathscr{G}}=f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}
which is a
\Gamma
-equivariant sheaf on
X
. Take
U\subset Y
small enough such that
f^{-1}(U)\simeq U\times\Gamma
. Then
f_{n}|_{f_{n}^{-1}(U)}
can be identified with the projection
U\times\Gamma^{n}\rightarrow U
. By Lemma
A.19
,
f_{n,!}
commutes with direct sums. Since
f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}|_{f^{-1}(U)}=\oplus_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{n}}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}|_{(U,\gamma)}
, we have a natural map
(f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}})|_{U}=\oplus_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{n}}(f_{n}|_{(U,\gamma)})_{!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}|_{(U,\gamma)}=\oplus_{\gamma\in\Gamma^{n}}{\mathscr{F}}|_{U}\rightarrow{\mathscr{F}}|_{U}
. Let
U
ranges over basis of
Y
we see there exists a natural map
f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow{\mathscr{F}}
for any
n
.

Let
d_{n,i}:X^{(n+1)/Y}\rightarrow X^{n/Y}
be the
i
-th face map for
i=0,\cdots,n
. Similarly as above, there exists a natural map
d_{n,i,!}d_{n,i}^{-1}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}
. Applying
f_{n,!}
, we obtain a map
d_{n,i}:f_{n+1,!}f_{n+1}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}
and let

\delta_{n}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}d_{n,i}:f_{n+1,!}f_{n+1}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}

be the alternating sum of the face maps. We need to check that the map
\colim_{[n]}f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow{\mathscr{F}}
is an isomorphism. The problem is local and we may assume that there is a section
Y\hookrightarrow X
such that
\Gamma\times Y=X:(\gamma_{n-1},y)\mapsto\gamma_{n-1}y
.
Then

\Gamma^{n}\times Y\simeq X^{n/Y}:(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{n-1},y)\mapsto(\gamma_{0}y,\cdots,\gamma_{n-1}y),

(62)

and
f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}=\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma^{n}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathscr{F}}:s\mapsto\gamma\otimes s|_{\gamma^{-1}U}
for
s\in(f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}})(U),\gamma\in\Gamma^{n}
.
The map
\delta_{n}
can be identified with

\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma^{n}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathscr{F}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma^{n-1}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathscr{F}}:[(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{n-1})]\otimes s\mapsto\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{i}[(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\widehat{\gamma}_{i},\cdots,\gamma_{n-1})]\otimes s.

Hence
\colim_{[n]}f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}=[\cdots\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma^{n}]\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]]\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathscr{F}}={\mathscr{F}}
since the complex
\cdots\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma^{n}]\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}

is a standard resolution of
\mathbb{Z}
. Note that as in (
62
), we have

\Gamma^{n-1}\times X\simeq X^{n/Y}:(\gamma_{0},\cdots,\gamma_{n-2},x)\mapsto(\gamma_{0}x,\cdots,\gamma_{n-2}x).

We see
f_{n,!}f_{n}^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}=\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma^{n}]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}f_{!}{\mathscr{G}}
.
∎

Proposition A.24
.

Let
g:Y\rightarrow*
be the structure map. Let
{\mathscr{F}}\in D^{+}(Y,\mathbb{Z}_{\square})
. Suppose that
\Gamma
has finite cohomological dimension (e.g.
[
BS76
, §6.1]
). Then
Rg_{!}{\mathscr{F}}=\mathbb{Z}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}Rg_{!}(f_{!}f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}).

Proof.

By the local description of
f_{!}f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}
, we see
f_{!}f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}\in D^{+}(Y,\mathbb{Z}_{\square})
. By Lemma
A.23
,
{\mathscr{F}}=\mathbb{Z}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}f_{!}{\mathscr{G}}
. We need to show that
Rg_{!}(\mathbb{Z}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}f_{!}f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}})=\mathbb{Z}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}Rg_{!}(f_{!}f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}})
. If
\Gamma
has finite cohomological dimension
d
, there exists a finite projective resolution

0\rightarrow P^{-d}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow P^{-1}\rightarrow P_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow 0

of the trivial
\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]
-module
\mathbb{Z}
. Hence
Rg_{!}(\mathbb{Z}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}f_{!}f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}})=Rg_{!}(P^{\bullet}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}f_{!}f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}})=P^{\bullet}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}Rg_{!}(f_{!}f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}})
since the derived functor
Rg_{!}
preserves finite colimits. (One can replace
f_{!}f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}}
by an injective resolution
{\mathscr{I}}^{\bullet}
of sheaves of condensed
\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]
-modules. Then
Rg_{!}(\mathbb{Z}\otimes^{L}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}f_{!}f^{-1}{\mathscr{F}})
is calculated as the
g_{!}\mathrm{Tot}(P^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}{\mathscr{I}}^{\bullet})=\mathrm{Tot}(P^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\Gamma]}g_{!}{\mathscr{I}}^{\bullet})
.)
∎
